## Snapshot
SK hynix is the world's #1 high-bandwidth memory (HBM) maker and the #2 DRAM/NAND producer overall, and is **NVIDIA's primary HBM supplier**. Near-monopoly HBM3E history — its HBM tool/process share into NVDA was ~100% "until last fall" (SemiAnalysis HBM). UBS models 2026 industry HBM share at **SK Hynix / Samsung / Micron = 56% / 22% / 22%**, with SK hynix "already shipping HBM4" and larger volumes by 2Q26 (UBS 2026 overview, Dec-2025) — i.e. share normalizing off the historical ~90% as Samsung/Micron qualify. FY2025 revenue 97.1tn KRW (+47% YoY), operating profit 47.2tn (+101%), 49% OP margin (SK hynix IR FY25, 2026-01-29). Mix: DRAM (incl. HBM) is the profit engine; NAND/enterprise SSD via wholly-owned **Solidigm**, where the group's eSSD share reached 30.2% in Q4-2025 (search digest, 2026-06); SK Hynix+Solidigm is modeled at ~36% of 2026e QLC eSSD output, the largest single bloc (MS AI NAND overview). It sits at the demand epicentre of the AI capex cycle: HBM has been "sold out since 2023," 2026 DRAM/HBM volume is contracted out (Q3 2025 call, 2025-10-29), and vendors reaffirm the 2026 sold-out/allocation tone (DeepResearch TSM, citing SK hynix/Micron commentary).

## At a glance — product · buyer · supplier
| | |
|---|---|
| **Sells (top 3)** | 1) HBM (HBM3E/HBM4 — #1, profit engine) · 2) DRAM (DDR5/LPDDR/GDDR7) · 3) NAND / enterprise SSD (Solidigm) |
| **Main buyer(s)** | NVIDIA (primary HBM customer, into Rubin; ~56% 2026 HBM share, non-NVDA capped ≤20%); other hyperscalers/ASIC; mobile & PC OEMs |
| **Key suppliers** | ASML (EUV litho), AMAT/LRCX (tools), silicon wafers; Hanmi (TC-bonding for HBM packaging); TSMC (HBM4 logic base die) |

## Position in the value chain
SK hynix converts silicon wafers and litho/etch/deposition tools (ASML EUV, AMAT, LRCX) into DRAM + HBM stacks and NAND/eSSD, then ships to NVIDIA (primary HBM customer, into the Rubin platform), other accelerator/hyperscaler buyers, and mobile/PC OEMs. Its moat is HBM process + advanced packaging lead (HBM3E 12-high, first-to-mass-production HBM4) plus a net-cash balance sheet (35tn KRW at Q1-2026) funding the M15X / Yongin / Indiana packaging build-out. On DRAM node mix it also leads Samsung: ~40-50% of SK hynix DRAM bits on 1a-nm-or-more-advanced nodes vs Samsung ~30% (Micron 70%+) — a key reason for Samsung's relative de-rating (Bernstein Semis deck, May-2025). HBM packaging hinges on a few specialist tools: SK hynix's thermocompression (TC) bonding for HBM3E 12-high has run almost entirely on Hanmi tools; a Q2-2025 dispute (Hanmi pulled field service) showed how concentrated this dependency is, with ASMPT/Besi/Hanwha alternatives not yet qualified (SemiAnalysis HBM).

## Current state (latest quarter)

**🆕🔴 2026-08-27 — TWO DAMNANG PIECES INGESTED TOGETHER PUT ARITHMETIC UNDER THE DE-SPEC, AND THE CONCLUSION RUNS THE OPPOSITE WAY FROM THE MARKET'S FIRST REACTION: HBM REVENUE FALLS FAR LESS THAN THE BIT COUNT, AND MAY NOT FALL AT ALL.** ⚠️⚠️ **INDEPENDENT SUBSTACK AUTHOR, NOT A BROKER — no rating, no PT, no model. The author states outright that the 8-Hi configuration is *"UNCONFIRMED information based on industry checks"* and that the revenue figures are *"scenario SENSITIVITIES, not company guidance or shipment forecasts."* Filed as analysis, not as a datapoint.** ("What Does Rubin Ultra's 8-Hi HBM Mean?", **2026-08-10** [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/What_Does_Rubin_Ultras_8-Hi_HBM_Mean__-_Damnangs_Substack.html) · "HBM Density Peak: Why AI Value Is Moving From Memory to Connectivity", **2026-08-20** [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/HBM_Density_Peak__Why_AI_Value_Is_Moving_From_Memory_to_Connectivity.html))
- 🔴🔴 **THE SENSITIVITY THAT MATTERS FOR THIS PAGE, AND IT IS THE MISSING QUANTIFICATION BEHIND SK HYNIX'S OWN "DE-SPEC IS NOT A DEMAND SIGNAL" MESSAGE:** dropping 12-Hi → 8-Hi lifts effective output by **up to 1.5x on stacking time × 1.084x on stack yield (98% per-core-die, four fewer layers) = ~1.63x**. If [[NVDA]] absorbs all of that into extra GPU shipments, **total HBM revenue lands at ~81.5% of plan with 8-Hi priced at 50% of the planned HBM4E, and ~98% at 60%.** ➤ **The falsifiable bar, in the author's words: *"if 12-Hi HBM4E could not actually meet more than 81.5% of the required volume, this despec can actually result in HIGHER revenue."* That is the right test to hold this page's supply-tightness claims to — and SK Hynix's own 08-26/27 statement that supply is tight *"even over the long term"* is precisely the condition under which the de-spec is revenue-ACCRETIVE.**
- ⚠️ **WHY THE NAIVE CALCULATION IS WRONG, WHICH IS THE USEFUL MODELLING POINT:** assuming price scales with bits at a 10% per-GB discount gives 45% of the 4E price × 1.63x = **~73% of plan**. The author argues that undershoots because *"the actual bit count falls by half, but the **CORE DIE COUNT is 8 versus 12, about two thirds, and the BASE DIE goes in the same** whether it is 8-Hi or 12-Hi"* — plus the bandwidth uplift being designed in. ➤ **Anyone modelling this page's HBM revenue off bits-per-GPU alone will be too bearish by roughly 10-25 points.**
- 🔴 **THE COMPETITIVE CONSEQUENCE FOR SK HYNIX SPECIFICALLY, AND IT CUTS BOTH WAYS: *"In the regime where 12-Hi and 16-Hi stack height was itself the AXIS OF DIFFERENTIATION, high-stack qualification and stacking technology carried a LARGE PREMIUM. **If the life of 8-Hi extends, YIELD, CAPACITY, SUPPLY STABILITY and BASE DIE FUNCTIONALITY carry more weight.**"*** ➤ **This is the single most important strategic line in the two pieces for this page. SK Hynix's lead has been built on high-stack qualification. A durable 8-Hi node moves the axis of competition toward exactly the dimensions where [[SAMSUNG]] and [[MU]] are closest — capacity and yield — while rewarding the custom-base-die investment SK Hynix is also making. The author reads *"SK hynix expanding CUSTOM BASE DIE and a CXL-based memory tier together"* as sitting against precisely this change.** ⚠️ **Directionally negative for stack-height premium, directionally positive for the base-die and CXL strategy. Not a rating.**
- **THE CAUSE, FROM FIELD CHECKS, AND IT IS A QUALIFICATION FAILURE RATHER THAN A DEMAND FAILURE:** [[NVDA]] has been demanding **~16Gbps pin speed on HBM4E, far above spec**, and *"the HBM4E samples submitted so far by **the three memory makers** are reportedly having a HARDER TIME THAN EXPECTED PASSING QUALIFICATION at that spec"* — echoing the 12Gbps HBM4 episode. With HBM4 already holding **10-11Gbps against an 8Gbps JEDEC baseline**, proven HBM4 plus a bandwidth bump beat forcing Rubin Ultra (2H 2027) onto 4E. ⚠️ **Note the author's own caveat: the newly requested HBM4 pin speed *"comes close to the 15 to 16Gbps"* originally asked of HBM4E, so *"on pin speed alone the newly demanded HBM4 could just as well be called HBM4E class."* He calls it HBM4 on core-die density (3GB) and qualification path. The HBM4/HBM4E label is NOT a clean bandwidth distinction and should not be used as one in a bit or ASP model.**
- 🔴 **THE SECOND PIECE (08-20) CONNECTS THIS PAGE TO THE OPTICAL CHAIN THROUGH SK HYNIX'S OWN RESEARCH, AND IT IS A GENUINE NET-NEW LINK.** A **Nature Electronics review, *"Co-packaged optics for high-performance computing and artificial intelligence"* (Kim, B. et al., 2026), published 2026-08-20 with an SK HYNIX RESEARCHER AS CORRESPONDING AUTHOR**, sets out the physical driver: **compute throughput has roughly TRIPLED every two years while interconnect bandwidth advanced only ~1.4x over the same cycle.** The integration ladder runs pluggable → on-board → **2.5D CPO → 3D TSV / hybrid bonding → monolithic**, moving the optical conversion point closer to compute at each step; the long-term figure separates an **XPU pool and a MEMORY POOL linked by a PHOTONIC INTERPOSER.** ⚠️⚠️ **READ THE STATUS CORRECTLY: the paper explicitly names an optical memory interface preserving coherence, low-power photonic switching and multi-hop regeneration as UNSOLVED. It is a RESEARCH DIRECTION, and the author says so — *"NOT an SK hynix commercial product roadmap."* Do not log the photonic interposer as a product.** ➤ **What it IS good for: a memory maker's own research is now arguing that the AI bottleneck extends from HBM bandwidth to DATA MOVEMENT between compute and memory — which is the intellectual foundation for the CXL/CMM-Ax tier SK Hynix is commercialising with [[MRVL]], and it is the same claim [[LITE]] management made from the laser end on 08-27 (*"does it go IN-TRAY and serve high-bandwidth connectivity between memory and between GPUs"*). Two independent sources, opposite ends of the stack, same transition.**
- **AND THE DISAGGREGATION MECHANIC, WHICH IS WHY CAPACITY PER GPU CAN FALL WITHOUT PERFORMANCE FALLING:** local HBM stays the fastest tier — **Rubin local HBM ~21-22TB/s vs NVLink 6 at ~3.6TB/s GPU-to-GPU** — so *"another GPU's HBM does NOT replace local HBM. What changes is HOW MUCH of the weights and KV cache must sit in ONE GPU's HBM."* NVL576's 8-rack / 576-GPU NVLink domain keeps active weights and hot KV local while distributable weights and KV spread to peer HBM. ➤ **The bit demand does not disappear; it MOVES — into peer HBM, CXL memory and (eventually) optically-shared memory. For a memory maker that is a mix shift, not a demand cut, and it is why the author's headline is "value moving from memory to CONNECTIVITY" rather than "away from memory."**

➤ **WHY (1)+(2) MATTER MORE THAN ANYTHING ELSE ON THIS PAGE THIS WEEK: they make [[SAMSUNG]] IR's 08-25 *"still no HBM de-spec for high-end GPUs"* and SemiAnalysis/Jefferies/TrendForce's active Rubin-Ultra de-spec checks BOTH TRUE. De-spec is a RATIONING INSTRUMENT applied down the customer tier stack, with the flagship protected last.**

The main driver is Nvidia's procurement strategy of securing a broader range of HBM options to address THERMAL-MANAGEMENT concerns"*** (Zdnet Korea, via @jukan05, corpus, 2026-08-26). ➤ **If the driver is THERMAL rather than cost, the cut does not reverse when memory pricing normalises — and it converges with the KYEC check attributing Rubin's end-of-3Q push-out to *thermal design changes*.**

**🆕🔴 2026-08-26 — SK HYNIX'S OWN HYBRID-BONDING PRESS RELEASE, WHICH DATES THE BACK-END CAPEX CYCLE AND PARTLY FALSIFIES THE BEAR PREMISE ON ITS OWN TERMS.** (SK hynix press release, relayed by Jefferies · O'Mara, 2026-08-26.)
- **TIMING: *"While existing packaging approaches remain viable UP TO HBM4, structural limitations are likely to emerge from HBM4E ONWARD… full-scale adoption of hybrid bonding is most likely to occur in HBM4E OR HBM5, where stack heights are projected to EXCEED 20 LAYERS."***
- **GATES: *"manufacturing costs that are TWO TO THREE TIMES HIGHER than those of flip-chip packaging, the maturity of process technology, and the pace of equipment development… a SIGNIFICANT GAP in equipment capabilities between domestic and overseas suppliers."***
- **THE PHASED PATH, AND THIS IS THE ORDER BOOK: *"TCB is expected to be increasingly replaced by hybrid bonding in the future… At present, a FLUX-FREE variant of TCB is being adopted, and further attempts are expected to directly connect COPPER TO COPPER using TCB-based processes. When no viable alternatives remain, the industry will shift toward hybrid bonding."***
- **THE PRIZE: hybrid bonding vs conventional TCB — **speed ~11.9x, heat dissipation >100x, integration density 15x**; eliminating bumps and underfill *"reduces chip height and thermal resistance… provid[ing] a foundation for moving beyond 2.5D architectures toward FULLY 3D HBM packaging,"* with research underway on **3D HBM with NO TSV at all**.
- **SK HYNIX'S THERMAL ANSWER — **I-HBM**: *"embeds a high-thermal-conductivity, electrically insulating cooling material within the HBM D2D PHY area, close to the hotspot, creating a dedicated heat path that can FURTHER REDUCE THERMAL RESISTANCE BY MORE THAN 30%,"* to support **HBM5** alongside proprietary **Advanced MR-MUF**. **Already using advanced MR-MUF for 16-Hi HBM3e**, and *"looking to hybrid bonding as a key path BEYOND 16-Hi HBM."* Its **2.5D roadmap features [[INTC]] EMIB alongside CoWoS-L, CoWoS-R and CoWoS-S**; at Hot Chips it compared *"how CoWoS-S, CoWoS-L and Intel EMIB impose different mechanical and thermal stresses on HBM stacks and silicon interposers"* (Hot Chips 2026, via Wccftech / ServeTheHome).
➤ ⚠️ **SCORING THE 08-25 BACK-END DEBATE (MS desk: *"if you don't scale to 16-high, you don't need hybrid bonding"* → bearish [[BESI]]/CAMT/ONTO/[[TER]]; BofA: architecture migration RAISES WFE intensity): MS wins the timing half — HBM4 does not need it, fluxless TCB comes first. BofA wins the direction half — Hynix calls the shift inevitable and every intervening step is tool-and-materials intensive. AND THE BEAR PREMISE IS ALREADY WRONG ABOUT THE PRESENT: SK Hynix IS SHIPPING 16-Hi HBM3e TODAY on advanced MR-MUF. The live question is 20+ layers, not 16.** ⚠️ **Unresolved against it: the Mizuho relay's *"16/20Hi and hybrid bonding remain HIGHLY UNCERTAIN for subsequent generations"* — the supplier's roadmap and a broker's checks now point opposite ways on the variable that decides the back-end cycle.**

- **DRAM: ASPs **+25% q/q in 3Q26 and +9% q/q in 4Q26**; then sequential increases/decreases through 1Q-4Q27 with the **full-year 2027 average still +25% y/y**; then **FALLING 8% IN 2028.** **80%+ OP margin sustainable even in late-2027 and 2028.**

**🆕🔴 2026-08-27 — UBS KOREA SUMMIT: THE PAYOUT POLICY CHANGED SHAPE, AND THE DEMAND-FULFILMENT RATIO IS THE BEST SINGLE S/D NUMBER ON THIS PAGE.** (UBS · **Nicolas Gaudois** / Jimmy Yoon / Luke Yoo / Randy Abrams, *"Key takeaways from UBS Korea Summit 2026"*, 2026-08-27.)

- ⚠️ **CHINA COMPETITION, THREE INCOMPATIBLE READS ON CXMT IN 24 HOURS: MS initiates CXMT at OW (PT Rmb88) with *"3-4mn units of HBM3E stacks in 2027"*; TechInsights (via Redburn) has CXMT at **HBM2E risk production / HBM3 sampling** while confirming **high-k metal gate in volume production on G4 16nm-class LPDDR5X**; DIGITIMES reports **CXMT DDR5 at 8,000 MT/s with the gap to SK Hynix "below 1%."** Not reconcilable; all logged, none adopted. And **YMTC *"aims to overtake Samsung AND SK Hynix to become the market share LEADER in NAND by the end of 2027"*** (FT, via Jefferies, 2026-08-26) versus MS's *"global #2 by 2028."* See [themes/china-export](themes/china-export.md).**



**🆕🔴🔴 2026-08-26 — TWO ITEMS FROM THE NVIDIA Q2 FY27 PRINT, AND THE FIRST IS SPECIFIC TO THIS NAME.** (NVIDIA press release + Q2 FY27 earnings call, both 2026-08-26 — company primary. **No SK hynix rating, PT or estimate; NVIDIA does not cover it.**)
- 🔴 **A NAMED, DATED PARTNERSHIP: NVIDIA *"announced a MULTIYEAR TECHNOLOGY PARTNERSHIP WITH SK HYNIX to advance NEXT-GENERATION MEMORY for the global AI factory buildout"*** (press release, Data Center highlights). ⚠️⚠️ **WHAT IS *NOT* IN IT, and the omissions matter as much as the announcement: no dollar value, no volume, no wafer or bit commitment, no node, no product generation, no term length beyond "multiyear," and NO EXCLUSIVITY LANGUAGE. It is a technology-development partnership on its face, NOT a supply agreement.** ➜ **It sits alongside — and does not supersede — the NVDA/SK Group $500bn+ initiative already on this page (2026-07-27: SK Telecom 2GW AI datacentre on Vera Rubin, first facility online 2027, SK ramping to 15GW by 2035, plus NVDA's $1bn investment in NAVER). The same call and release also name SK Telecom, NAVER and Brookfield as partners in NVIDIA's Korea AI-factory ecosystem build.** ⚠️ **Do not read it as an allocation or share datapoint: in the same call NVIDIA's CFO refers generically to *"all three major memory suppliers"* with whom it is *"working closely… to further increase the capacity."* SK hynix is named in the press release for a technology partnership and nowhere in the supply commentary.**
- 🔴🔴 **AND THE ONE THAT MATTERS FOR THE WHOLE COMPLEX: NVIDIA CUT ITS OWN GROSS-MARGIN GUIDE AND NAMED MEMORY AS THE CAUSE.** CFO Colette Kress: *"we are experiencing **EXTREME PRICING CONDITIONS IN MEMORY. The magnitude of the price increase has EXCEEDED OUR PRIOR EXPECTATIONS and are headed even higher into next year. As a result, we are resetting expectations today.**"* **FQ3 GM 74.0% ±50bp, FQ4 trough 71-72%, FY28 settle 72-73%, from 75.0% actual — with recovery only *"as executed price increases take effect in Q1."*** ➜ **The buyer taking a ~300-400bp hit and forecasting further increases into 2027 is worth more as cycle evidence than any supplier guide, because the supplier has an incentive to talk the cycle up and the customer paying for it does not.** ⚠️ **It does not adjudicate the widest open number on [themes/hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md) — UBS's [[SAMSUNG]] blended HBM ASP +90% y/y '27E vs JPM's industry +42% (08-24) — but it does rule out the low end and puts the largest counterparty on record expecting 2027 above 2026.** ⚠️ **Offsetting near-term item: NVIDIA's inventory rose to $32bn from $21.4bn at Jan-26 ahead of the Vera Rubin launch — memory already bought and parked in the customer's working capital, i.e. a pull-forward rather than incremental demand.** [Source — call](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/NVDA_conf_call_transcript_ago_26.html) · [Source — press release](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/NVIDIAAn.html)

**🆕🔴 2026-08-25 — THREE SEPARATE NEGATIVES LAND ON THE SAME DAY, AND ONE OF THEM IS A NAMED SELL-SIDE CLAIM THAT HYNIX IS *BEHIND* ON HBM AT NVIDIA.**
- **(1) Competitive position — the hardest claim:** Jefferies' Asia TMT desk, relaying its analyst **Sherman** from a client call, writes: *"**Sherman saying he sees Nvidia product with Samsung and Micron HBM but not with Hynix — they are late.** That explains the miss in OP and why Hynix were quite vague on their competitive position v peers"* (Jefferies Asia TMT sales · Conor O'Mara, 2026-08-25). ⚠️ **Sales-desk relay of an analyst comment — no product, node, generation or share attached — but the first source on this page to tie the OP miss to an HBM qualification lag at the largest customer, and it is directly falsifiable.**
- **(2) Labour — the wage deal was VOTED DOWN:** union members **rejected** the tentative collective bargaining agreement, **50.08% against**. The spurned deal carried a **6.3% salary raise** plus performance bonuses paid **40% cash / 60% company stock**. The union has rejected preliminary agreements in past years and is expected to resume negotiations (Yonhap via Jefferies Asia TMT sales; Reuters via Barclays desk · Jeffrey Rand, both 2026-08-25). ⚠️ **A cost / timing risk, not yet a quantified one.**
- **(3) The buyback is now a material share of the tape:** *"SK hynix's **650K shs of daily buyback quantity easily becomes 15% of the day's KRX-only volume**"* (Jefferies Asia TMT sales, 2026-08-25). 🔴 **Read it as price support that flatters the tape, not as a fundamental datapoint — and note the flip side: a large share of daily liquidity is non-economic.**

Both readings are live.**

**🆕🔴 2026-08-23/24 — TWO THINGS CHANGED FOR HYNIX THIS WEEK AND THEY POINT OPPOSITE WAYS: MANAGEMENT COMMITTED ON THE RECORD TO LIFTING THE RETURN POOL ABOVE 50% OF ACCUMULATED FCF, WHILE SUSQUEHANNA IS THE FIRST HOUSE ON THIS PAGE TO SIT AT *NEUTRAL* ON HYNIX WHILE BEING POSITIVE ON SAMSUNG, MICRON AND SANDISK.**

**🆕🔴 2026-08-24 (PM) — THE HBM SPEC CUT GETS A THIRD, DIRECTLY CONTRARY READING — AND IT COMES FROM THE MOST TECHNICAL SOURCE ON THE PAGE.** (@SemiAnalysis_, 2026-08-24 21:00 UTC — *independent research, no rating, no PT*)
- **Verbatim:** *"**HBM MASSIVE CONTENT DOWNGRADE ALERT🚨: Nvidia Rubin Ultra will ship with 192GB of HBM4 8-hi. Not only is this a huge downgrade from the original Rubin Ultra which was previewed with 1TB of HBM, but it is even LOWER than regular Rubin which has 288GB.** How did HBM content for Rubin Ultra get cut to 1/5th of the original? **The number of cubes got cut to 8 when 4-die Rubin Ultra was scrapped. Then the stack height got reduced to 8-hi instead of 16-hi. Last it is cut to HBM4 which uses 24Gb dies instead of 32Gb dies with HBM4E**, although an 8-hi HBM4E upgrade could come later."*
- 🔴 **THIS IS THE SAME FACT SET THAT THREE HOUSES RESOLVED *TOWARDS THE BULLS* EARLIER THE SAME DAY, READ THE OPPOSITE WAY. The wiki logged this morning: JPM/Sur (customer side) and JPM/Jay Kwon (supplier side, Asia Tech Tour Seoul) both attributing the 12-Hi→8-Hi and SOCAMM changes to *"insufficient supply (availability issue) rather than the intent to de-spec,"* with *"limited changes to the total bit demand TAM"*; UBS/Arcuri added a third leg the same afternoon (*"changes for 2H:27 as NVDA manages through HBM supply constraints… does not negatively affect our estimates"*). **SemiAnalysis accepts the same spec facts and rejects the benign conclusion — it decomposes the cut into THREE INDEPENDENT MULTIPLICATIVE STEPS (cube count 16→8 via the scrapped 4-die design, stack height 16-Hi→8-Hi, die density 32Gb→24Gb) and calls the result a 5x content reduction on the flagship SKU.**
- 🔴 **The decomposition is the reason this cannot be dismissed as a restatement: only ONE of the three steps (stack height) is plausibly a supply-availability decision. The scrapping of the 4-die Rubin Ultra is a PACKAGE/DESIGN decision, and HBM4-vs-HBM4E is a GENERATION decision. If the cube-count and generation steps are real, the bit-demand-neutral claim does not hold on this SKU regardless of why the stack height moved.** ⚠️ **Not adopted as the page view — it is a single independent source against three broker legs. Logged as the strongest available bear rebuttal, with the specific testable claim being the 8-cube / 8-Hi / HBM4 configuration for Rubin Ultra.**
- **And a fourth, even more aggressive variant is circulating unattributed:** **@TBU12345678 (RT'd by @insane_analyst, 2026-08-24): _"4-Hi is going to be a term you hear a lot going forward. Not 8, not 16 but 4. $NVDA $MU"_** ⚠️ **Anonymous handle, no evidence, no source — carried ONLY to mark that the downgrade rumour has a further leg below the SemiAnalysis version. Do not treat as a datapoint.**

**🆕🔴 2026-08-24 — SK HYNIX IS ADDING ~30k WPM OF NAND CAPACITY *IN CHINA* INTO 2027.** (@jukan05, 2026-08-24, relaying Korean industry reporting) *"Samsung, SK Hynix Step Up NAND Investment in China… **SK Hynix is also expected to secure around 30,000 wafers per month of production capacity between the second half of this year and next year**… both plan to actively carry out equipment investments for their NAND mass-production lines in China through next year."* (The companion Samsung leg — Xi'an X2 converting to **V9 NAND, ~280 layers** — is on [[SAMSUNG]].)
- ➜ 🔴 **Two consequences. (1) For the NAND cycle: ~30k wpm is real incremental supply arriving into a market this wiki has been treating as capital-disciplined. (2) For [[themes/semicap-wfe]]: "equipment investments… through next year" in Chinese NAND lines is incremental WFE demand that sits INSIDE China's import data — relevant to the July SPE print logged today (front-end $3,444mn, +3% y/y).** ⚠️ Second-hand relay, no capex figure, no equipment vendor named.
- **Political cross-current the same day:** *"the U.S. government has asked the South Korean government to build memory fabs in the U.S. and ensure a stable supply of memory chips"* (@jukan05, 2026-08-24). **Both Korean makers are being pulled toward China by economics and toward the US by policy in the same week.**

**🆕 2026-08-25 (KST) — BofA'S KOREA MEMORY TECH TOUR MAPS THE BACKEND LAYER.** (BofA · Simon Woo, 2026-08-24) — four sessions on 25 Aug covering **EO Technics** (laser wafer dicing/marking), **Daeduck Electronics** (FC-BGA logic PCB, **LPDDR5 memory board**, multi-layer, **glass substrate, CPO**), **Samsung Electronics** (*"already full… overly crowded – no more extra seats"*) and **Hana Micron** (memory OSAT for Korean chipmakers, **DDR5 backend capacity**, Vietnam and Brazil operations). ➜ **The DDR5 OSAT and memory-PCB layer is where an HBM/DDR5 capacity constraint would show up before it shows up at the DRAM makers — worth watching as an independent read on the supply-vs-de-spec argument above.**
- **The commitment, made at the JPM Asia Tech Tour (Seoul, Aug 20-21):** following the **W40tn share buyback and cancellation** announcement, *"management reiterated lifting the shareholder return pool from <50% to over 50% of accumulated FCF as a sign of prioritizing shareholder value and suggested further update at its 3Q26 earnings."* **LTA volume coverage: over 50%; contract duration up to 5 years.** (JPM · Jay Kwon w/ Sangsik Lee, Neelay Kamath, Mio Shikanai, "Key Takeaways from JPM Asia Tech Tour (Seoul Part): Memory Semiconductors", 2026-08-23)
- 🔴 **The rating split is the net-new information: SIG has [[SAMSUNG]] Positive, [[MU]] Positive, [[SNDK]] Positive — and SK hynix NEUTRAL, px KRW1,761,000.** SIG's preferred memory names are *"Micron, driven by HBM share gains, and SanDisk, where we see an inflection point in the adoption of 2Tb QLC-based NAND and eSSD solutions"* — Hynix is named in neither leg. (SIG · Mehdi Hosseini, "Technology Hardware — Memory Update: Intra-Quarter Pricing Update", 2026-08-24.) **This sharpens the standing divergence already on this page** where the two "bullish" houses (JPM, UBS) carry implied targets *below* the BBG consensus target — see Debate.
- **Cross-read that makes the W40tn look smaller than the headline:** BofA computes **50% of FCF as a high-single-digit % of market cap for BOTH SEC and Hynix**, i.e. it treats the two return programmes as *"broadly comparable"* in magnitude — Samsung's being dividend-centric and Hynix's buyback-centric — *"but consider both as broadly comparable in terms of returns."* (BofA · Simon Woo, 2026-08-23)

**🆕🔴🔴 2026-08-23 — THE PRIMARY BEHIND THE PACKAGING SLIDE IS NOW ON FILE, AND IT IS FAR RICHER THAN THE RELAY: SK HYNIX'S OWN HOT CHIPS TALK PUTS HARD NUMBERS ON THE HBM STACK-HEIGHT WALL — AND SAYS **HBM4 AT ~775 MICRONS IS ALREADY “CLOSE TO THE PRACTICAL LIMIT”**.** (**Jasik Lee, SK hynix USA** — Hot Chips 2026, as transcribed by **TMT Breakout** (Substack newsletter, AUG 23; **conference coverage, NOT broker research** — no rating, no PT, no estimate).) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Inbox_-_Felipe_Monteiro_-_Outlook.html)
- ➜ **Why this supersedes the relay below in EVIDENCE VALUE while leaving it in place: the @jukan05/@BenBajarin item is a photograph of one slide; this is the company engineer's own framing of the whole roadmap. Per the standing rule on this wiki, the primary wins and the relay keeps its place as the earlier timestamp.**
- 🔴 **THE CEILING, STATED BY THE SUPPLIER: “Through HBM3E, standard HBM thickness was roughly 720 microns. HBM4 moves to around 775 microns, which SK hynix described as CLOSE TO THE PRACTICAL LIMIT under current packaging approaches. Future scaling therefore cannot rely indefinitely on making the package taller and will increasingly depend on thinner dies, smaller gaps and eventually hybrid bonding.”** ➜ **This is the cleanest statement on this wiki of WHY the HBM roadmap has to change technology rather than just add layers — and it is the physical constraint underneath every HBM-content forecast the sell-side is running.**
- 🔴 **THE 16-HIGH COST, QUANTIFIED: in its 16-high HBM3E test vehicle SK hynix “still had to make individual DRAM dies roughly 10% THINNER than 12-high and reduce inter-die gaps by about 50%, even after increasing total cube thickness from 720 to 775 microns.”** That makes **die warpage and gap filling much harder**, and higher pin speeds **“increase power density around the PHY, adding localized hotspots.”** ➜ **Read directly against the HBM4/HBM4E yield and pricing debate this page carries: the incremental layer is being bought with thinner silicon and tighter gaps, i.e. with YIELD RISK, not with free headroom.**
- 🔴 **HYBRID BONDING — AND THE DATE IT IS *NOT* NEEDED: SK hynix expects HBM to move from micro-bump stacking to hybrid bonding, but “Lee does NOT expect it for HBM4E.”** The economics arrive **around 20-high**: removing the gaps between DRAM dies lets the silicon itself get thicker inside the same package-height limit — SK hynix estimates die thickness **could increase roughly 20–24%** in a hypothetical 20-high stack, **“improving manufacturing margin and thermals,”** with **~35% improvement in thermal conductivity at 20-high.** ➜ **A supplier-sourced date-stamp on the hybrid-bonding transition: NOT at HBM4E, economic at ~20-high. That is a direct input to the equipment debate (bonder demand timing) and cuts against any thesis that pulls hybrid bonding into the HBM4E generation.**
- **THE PROCESS ADVANTAGE SK HYNIX CLAIMS, WITH ITS OWN STATED DOWNSIDE:** its **molded** flow “places the full DRAM stack, joins it during a SINGLE REFLOW and then fills the gaps with molding material. Compared with bonding each die individually, this improves PRODUCTIVITY and allows materials with relatively good thermal conductivity between dies.” ⚠️ **“The downside is greater sensitivity to warpage because individual dies can be around 50 microns thick or thinner, while ever-smaller gaps become harder to fill reliably.”** ➜ **Carried with the caveat attached because it is the mechanism behind both the yield advantage and the yield risk on the same page.**
- **HOTSPOTS ARE NOT SOLVED BY HYBRID BONDING — AND THERE IS A NAMED PRODUCT RESPONSE: “Even if hybrid bonding improves overall thermal conductivity, the base die still contains small regions with extremely high power density. SK hynix is developing **IHBM**, which adds SILICON HEAT BLOCKS above hotspot regions to improve local heat extraction.”** Lee said this **“requires co-optimization from the beginning of the design and cannot simply be added late in a product cycle.”** ➜ **“IHBM” is net-new to this wiki. The co-design requirement is also a competitive moat argument — it favours whoever is engaged with the customer earliest.**
- 🔴 **THE CUSTOMER-PACKAGING DEPENDENCY, WHICH IS THE STRATEGIC POINT: “Advanced HBM experiences much more of the customer's subsequent packaging process than traditional memory. Hybrid-bonded HBM can be among the FIRST components attached to an interposer, exposing it to the thermal and mechanical stress of later integration. Customers are also evaluating more interposers, bridges and alternative packaging technologies, requiring deeper collaboration around process flows, materials and mechanical stress.”** ➜ **This is the commercial logic that makes the EMIB slide (relay below) coherent rather than surprising — the memory maker is now co-engineering with the customer's packaging choice, so an Intel bridge appearing in SK hynix's own roadmap is a consequence of the model Lee describes, not an anomaly.**
- 🔴 **AND THE ARCHITECTURE FORK THAT MATTERS MOST TO THE MEMORY-MIX DEBATE: “SK hynix does NOT assume every AI workload should simply use taller HBM. TRAINING often needs both more bandwidth and more capacity, supporting continued stack-height increases. INFERENCE may use a SMALLER POOL of very-high-bandwidth memory and place less bandwidth-sensitive data in LPDDR or another lower-cost tier. Lee said the industry still needs to determine the optimal architecture rather than assuming training and inference converge on the same solution.”** ➜ **The supplier itself is declining to underwrite “HBM content per accelerator grows forever.” That is a genuine ⚠ against the most aggressive HBM-TAM formulations on this wiki, and it comes from the market leader rather than from a bear.**
- **ONE MORE SCALING CONSTRAINT, FLAGGED AS UNRESOLVED BY THE COMPANY: “Higher signal speeds require tighter bump pitches, more shielding and potentially more metal layers in the interposer or bridge. Those layers are much thicker than in conventional logic, so adding more can materially change wafer thickness and mechanical behavior. SK hynix said it is NOT YET CLEAR how far the industry can push routing-layer counts before warpage and manufacturability become limiting.”**

**🆕🔴 2026-08-23 — HYNIX'S ADVANCED-PACKAGING ROADMAP NOW NAMES *INTEL EMIB* IN ITS OWN SLIDES, AND THAT IS A GENUINELY NEW SUPPLIER RELATIONSHIP FOR THIS PAGE.** *"EMIB mentioned in SK hynix's slides…"* (@jukan05, 2026-08-23, with the slide image; amplified @BenBajarin — **independent handle relaying a primary conference slide, not broker research**). Corroborated by the trade press the next day: *"SK Hynix charts advanced HBM packaging path: hybrid bonding, Intel EMIB and 3D integration"* — the framing being that *"competition in high bandwidth memory is shifting from raw memory performance to advanced packaging"*, with thermal management and power consumption as the binding constraints as stack heights climb (DIGITIMES, 2026-08-24). A second DIGITIMES piece the same day: *"Analysis: SK Hynix pushes beyond HBM with HBF and CPO."*
- **Why EMIB matters:** it puts **[[INTC]]** into Hynix's packaging supply chain at exactly the moment a separate (unconfirmed) report has NVDA evaluating an **all-Intel Rubin CPO variant using 18A, Intel OCI and EMIB** (@ShanghaoJin via @bubbleboi, 2026-08-23). Two independent EMIB datapoints in one week is worth flagging even though neither is broker-sourced. Filed also to [[INTC]] and themes/optical-cpo.
- **The CPO vendor question, speculatively:** *"SK Hynix is evaluating Celestial/Ayar"* — noting **Ayar Labs appears on SK hynix's own website in the context of optical↔memory links**, and that **[[MRVL]] "is actively working with SK Hynix in custom memory solutions"** (and now owns Celestial AI). Both are cited in SK hynix's linked *Nature* paper (@aleabitoreddit, 2026-08-21 — **the vendor evaluation is explicitly the author's own guess; the MRVL/SKH custom-memory work is stated as fact**).
- **Capacity datapoint from the trade press:** *"Samsung's DRAM capacity lead shrinks as SK Hynix expands faster"* (DIGITIMES, 2026-08-24).

**🆕 2026-08-23 — THE HBM SPEC CUT IS A SUPPLY PROBLEM, NOT A DEMAND PROBLEM, AND HYNIX SAID SO ALONGSIDE ITS PEERS.** *"The dominant message from DRAM makers was that the 12-Hi to 8-Hi HBM spec adjustment or SOCAMM content downgrade was driven by insufficient supply (availability issue) rather than the intent to de-spec"* — with **"limited changes to the total bit demand TAM"** and constructive commentary on **agentic-AI-led CPU demand driving server DRAM content growth to 30%+ versus a historical 15-20%.** (JPM · Kwon, 2026-08-23.) This is the supplier-side confirmation of the spec changes [[NVDA]]'s preview independently documents (JPM · Sur, 2026-08-24: Rubin at 288GB *or* 192GB HBM4; Rubin Ultra 16-Hi → 8-Hi/12-Hi HBM4E; Vera SOCAMM -50% to 768GB).
- 🔴 **On HBM pricing, Hynix is deliberately the LESS aggressive of the two Koreans, and JPM frames that as a choice:** *"Investors found SEC's HBM pricing and wallet share commentary more aggressive vs SKH prioritizing strategic partnerships and presenting a rather balanced approach."* Both have **2027E HBM ASP negotiations under review, at the later part of the process**, weighing market conditions (incl. the non-HBM DRAM margin gap), next-gen HBM cost structure, and HBM performance. **JPM sees upside risk to its +43% y/y industry HBM ASP growth assumption.**
- ⚠️ **HBF is being talked down by the suppliers themselves, three weeks after the FMS announcements:** *"we sensed a relative conservatism citing the lack of interest from customers and a clear value proposition… many view that the customers are mainly viewing the TLC-based solution to be the primary option in the foreseeable future."* Cost is named as the blocker — **TSV processing plus system-level upgrade** — despite the bandwidth advantage. JPM: *"We continue to view the commercialization of HBF as likely to remain distant."*
- **Supply-side constraint that is specific and dated: new Korean NAND cleanroom space arrives no earlier than 2029E — for Hynix that is M17** (vs Samsung's Pyungtaek Fab 5). Near-term bit growth is **migration-driven, not capacity-driven, for the next 12-18 months**, and **higher wafer allocation to HBM is itself a supply constraint via the wafer-to-die penalty.** (JPM · Kwon, 2026-08-23)


**🆕🔴 2026-08-20 — THE BUYBACK IS NOW SIZED AGAINST THE FCF POOL, AND THE ANSWER IS THAT W40trn IS LESS THAN HALF THE 2026 BUDGET: BofA PUTS 2026 FCF AT ~W170trn, WHICH LEAVES ~W45trn OF SHAREHOLDER-RETURN BUDGET STILL UNSPENT THIS YEAR. THE EXECUTION WINDOW IS ALSO NOW DATED: 20 AUG → 19 NOV 2026.** (BofA · Simon Woo, "Simon's tech check (20 Aug)", 2026-08-19 23:33, and "Global Memory Tech: Weekly theme — Hynix buyback implication, upbeat spot price, tech tour preview", 2026-08-20.)
- 🔴 **The arithmetic, which is the first FCF-based sizing of the new policy on this page:** BofA expects **~W170trn of FCF in 2026 results**; at the new **50%+ payout** that is a **~W85trn return budget**, so **after the W40trn buyback the remaining budget is ~W45trn**. If returns stay buyback-centric, **cash dividends can be sub-50% of the total budget — e.g. ~W30trn, or ~W40k per share, "close to our current estimate."** ➜ **Against a market cap of ~W1,200trn, BofA's point is that memory names can now "easily offer high-single-digit% shareholder returns."**
- **The execution window is dated and checkable: the board approved the W40trn buyback for the period 20 Aug – 19 Nov 2026** (BofA, 2026-08-20). ➜ **That makes the 3Q26 results call — where management said further measures would be detailed — land near the END of the buyback window, not before it.**
- 🔴 **The ADR mechanic gets a concrete trigger, and it is a SK Square stake threshold rather than a Hynix decision:** SK Square owns ~20% of Hynix and **must retain at least 20.0% to be recognised as a holdco**; BofA's read is that **if SK Square's stake reaches 21% or higher, Hynix can smoothly issue new ADRs** (vs the current ADR float of ~2.5% of outstanding shares). ➜ **Reconciles with the MS Sales read already on this page (that future buybacks may be converted to ADR float rather than cancelled) — same conclusion, different mechanism. Both imply the cancellation ratio is not 100% forever.**
- **Industry-level implication BofA draws, which is the part that matters beyond the single name:** the buyback is read as **"extra cash spend for shareholders instead of irrationally increasing capex"** — i.e. a discipline signal for the whole memory complex, alongside (2) buybacks securing the ownership structure / future ADR issuance, and (3) **5-10x higher 2026 cash dividends versus the long-term historical average even AFTER the W40trn buyback.** BofA expects the same 50%-of-FCF return ratio at [[SAMSUNG]].
- 🔴 **The pricing backdrop underneath the capital-return story, and it is running AHEAD of BofA's own forecasts:** **DRAM spot has strengthened for more than two months despite low season**, most chips (16Gb DDR5, 8Gb DDR4) up LSD% again this week, implying **~+30% q/q**; the **August contract price for commodity DDR5 is only up low-to-mid-20% q/q, but a rising September price could take 3Q ASP above +30% q/q**. **NAND spot +20% QTD** (above BofA's own sub-20% forecast). **LTA-based 3Q prices span a very wide 0-30% range, with the majority "close to 20% or higher."** BofA sees **no downside risk to its 3Q blended ASP assumptions (DRAM +23% q/q, NAND +15%)**.
- 🔴 **The independent corroboration from China customs, which is the cleanest third-party ASP evidence available:** July semis imports **+71% y/y in USD on only +9% units**, and **memory-only imports +200% y/y** — "far exceeded logic," implying **memory import ASP up 100%+ y/y** (BofA, 2026-08-20).
- **Labour cost, newly settled:** SK hynix **reached a tentative deal with its labour union — a 6.3% salary increase plus a significant change in how employee performance bonuses are apportioned** (Jefferies Tech desk · Favuzza, 2026-08-20). ➜ **Relevant to the MS Sales point already on this page that Hynix bonuses are 100% CASH (vs [[SAMSUNG]] partly in shares) — the cash-bonus structure is what makes Hynix's buyback fully EPS-accretive, and this settlement re-prices that cash cost.**
- ⚠️ **Unverified product datapoint, logged as a lead not a fact: "$SKHY unveils CPO roadmap?? Apparently they're doing something with photonic interposer that links to memor[y]"** (@aleabitoreddit, 2026-08-20). ➜ **Would be material if confirmed — a memory-side CPO/photonic-interposer roadmap would put Hynix into the [[optical-cpo]] debate directly. NOT ADOPTED pending a primary source.** ✅ **PRIMARY SOURCE ARRIVED SAME DAY — see the CPO block immediately below. The lead was directionally right; the specifics in the tweet (a "photonic interposer that links to memory") are NOT what the primary says, so the tweet is confirmed as a topic and not as a mechanism.**

**🆕 2026-08-20 — CPO GETS A COMPANY PRIMARY, AND IT IS A PEER-REVIEWED ROADMAP RATHER THAN A PRODUCT: SK HYNIX PUBLISHED A CO-PACKAGED-OPTICS ROADMAP IN *NATURE ELECTRONICS*, FRAMED AROUND "AI COMPETITION SHIFTING FROM CHIPS TO SYSTEMS."** (**COMPANY PRIMARY** — SK hynix Newsroom, "SK hynix's technology roadmap for co-packaged optics features in 'Nature Electronics,' as AI competition shifts from chips to systems", 2026-08-20.)
- **What the source actually says, and no more:** SK hynix **collaborated with leading global researchers to publish a roadmap for co-packaged optics (CPO)** — described as **a key technology for next-generation AI infrastructure** — **in the journal *Nature Electronics***. The company's own tags on the item are **AI Infrastructure, AI Memory, Co-Packaged Optics, HBM, Heterogeneous Integration, Photonics**, and its headline thesis is that **AI competition is shifting from chips to systems**.
- ⚠️ **DELIBERATELY NOT OVERSTATED. This is a DIRECTIONAL/STRATEGIC signal — a peer-reviewed roadmap publication — NOT a product launch, not a customer win, and not a quantified capex or revenue item. There is no date, no partner named, no bandwidth or power figure, and no attach assumption to take from it.** ⚠️ **Mechanical note on the source: the archived copy is a 14-page image-rendered PDF with NO extractable text layer, so nothing beyond the headline facts above could be read out of it. If more specifics are needed, the pages have to be rendered and read visually (same hazard class as the BBG live-transcript files).**
- ➜ **Why it still earns a line: it resolves the 08-20 unverified FinTwit lead above with a company primary, and it puts SK hynix's own name — not a broker's inference — on the [[optical-cpo]] debate from the MEMORY side. Read against the FMS three-tier slide already on this page (3D stacked DRAM, HBF, CXL Pooled Memory — FUNDA, 2026-08-11), the pattern is consistent: the company keeps publishing system-level answers to the "HBM is too expensive / the interconnect is the bottleneck" problem rather than defending HBM alone. Cross-ref [themes/optical-cpo](themes/optical-cpo.md), [themes/hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md).**
- 🆕 **READ-THROUGH ADDED 2026-08-21 — AN INDEPENDENT INTERPRETATION OF THE SAME PAPER, AND THAT IS THE *ONLY* THING BEING ADDED: THE PAPER ITSELF IS ALREADY LOGGED IMMEDIATELY ABOVE FROM THE COMPANY PRIMARY AND IS DELIBERATELY **NOT** RE-LOGGED HERE.** (**DAMNANG (Substack)** — "HBM Density Peak: Why AI Value Is Moving From Memory to Connectivity", **2026-08-20**. ⚠️ **INDEPENDENT SUBSTACK AUTHOR, NOT A BROKER — no rating, no price target, no house model behind it.**) **What the read-through adds that the company primary could not (the archived primary has no text layer): (i) the two physical scaling rates the paper puts side by side — compute throughput has TRIPLED roughly every two years while interconnect bandwidth advanced only ~1.4x over the same two-year cycle**, which is the quantified version of "the interconnect is the bottleneck"; **(ii) the paper's own integration ladder — pluggable → on-board optics → 2.5D CPO → 3D TSV / hybrid bonding → monolithic integration, with 2.5D framed as the manufacturability-vs-performance compromise and 3D as higher bandwidth density and lower energy-per-bit at the cost of thermal and bonding yield**; and **(iii) the long-dated Fig. 4b architecture — XPU pool and memory pool SEPARATED and linked by a photonic interposer plus optical fibre.** ⚠️ **THE AUTHOR HIMSELF DRAWS THE LINE THIS PAGE ALREADY DREW, WHICH IS WHY NOTHING IS RE-SCORED: the paper "does not treat this as a finished architecture" — it NAMES the unsolved problems (an optical memory interface that preserves coherence while lowering latency, low-power photonic switching, multi-hop signal regeneration) — and the drawing is therefore "a research direction for the optical architecture that separated memory and compute would require, NOT an SK hynix commercial product roadmap."** ➡ **The one genuinely NEW *commercial* fact in the piece is the electrical, shipping half: CMM-Ax — disclosed August 2026, a CO-DEVELOPED product combining [[MRVL]]'s Structera A with SK hynix DRAM and software stack (a CXL-PNM structure), attaching compute to CXL memory so long-context inference can hold a KV cache LARGER THAN GPU HBM. Per the author, "the actual link between SK hynix and Marvell shows up FIRST IN THE CXL MEMORY TIER, not in optical memory" — and he explicitly warns there is NO announcement of SK hynix/Marvell co-development in optical memory: "sharing a structure and having a joint product are two different things." So the business read is a WIDENING of the designed-with-the-customer scope from the DRAM die to controller, base die, packaging and memory placement — HBM plus custom base die in the local fast tier, CXL/CMM-Ax in the external capacity tier, the photonic memory pool as the long-dated optical tier. Cross-ref [[MRVL]], [themes/cxl-memory-fabric](themes/cxl-memory-fabric.md), [themes/optical-cpo](themes/optical-cpo.md).** [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/13_HBM_Density_Peak__Why_AI_Value_Is_Moving_From_Memory_to_Connectivity.html)
- ⚠️🔴 **THE SAME PIECE CARRIES A CLAIM THAT CUTS *AGAINST* HBM CONTENT PER GPU, AND IT IS EXPLICITLY UNCONFIRMED — LOGGED AS A RUMOUR WITH THE AUTHOR'S OWN DISCLAIMER ATTACHED, NOT AS A FACT, AND NOT SCORED IN THE SINAL TABLE.** DAMNANG: **Rubin Ultra's HBM configuration "is being reviewed downward… toward HBM4 8-Hi," with supply constraint the direct cause.** ⚠️⚠️ **THE DISCLAIMER, VERBATIM: "The 8-Hi configuration for Rubin Ultra is UNCONFIRMED information based on industry checks, and the final specification may change." It is third-hand — the piece cites TrendForce (Aug-4: multiple configurations under evaluation including 8-Hi HBM4E / 12-Hi HBM4 / 8-Hi HBM4, final spec NOT set), The Information (prototype testing of 192GB and 256GB configurations) and SemiAnalysis (main SKU moving to HBM4 8-Hi 192GB with compute held and bandwidth slightly higher). A downshift in Rubin Ultra stack height would be the FIRST genuine mix-DOWN in HBM content per GPU this page has had to price, which is exactly why an unconfirmed spec rumour must not be allowed to read as established.** ➡ **Why it earns a line anyway — and the author's own offset, which is the part that matters: under supply constraint the per-GPU bit decline does NOT pass one-for-one into HBM revenue. Lower stack height raises the number of stacks buildable from the same DRAM and stacking capacity, and [[NVDA]] can absorb that into additional GPU shipments. His sensitivity (restated from his own earlier work): 12-Hi → 8-Hi gives a stacking-time effect plus a **1.084x stack-yield improvement** on a 98% per-core-die yield assumption, ≈ **1.63x effective output ceiling**; assuming all of it is absorbed into extra NVIDIA GPU shipments, total HBM revenue lands at **~81.5% of plan if 8-Hi is priced at 50% of the planned HBM4E, and ~98% at 60%**. ⚠️ HIS WORDS: "these are sensitivities to stack count, not shipment forecasts" — they are NOT adopted as estimates here. The durable half is competitive, not P&L: if the life of 8-Hi extends, the axis of differentiation moves OFF high-stack qualification — where this page's HBM4 yield moat lives (~80-85% at SK hynix vs ~60% at [[SAMSUNG]], BTG expert 07-30) — and ONTO yield, capacity, supply stability and BASE-DIE functionality. That is the same direction as the custom-base-die and CXL-tier expansion in the bullet above, which is the one reason this rumour is worth carrying at all.** [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/13_HBM_Density_Peak__Why_AI_Value_Is_Moving_From_Memory_to_Connectivity.html)

**🆕🔴 2026-08-20 (/run-inbox) — THREE HOUSES NOW COVER THE SAME W40trn EVENT, AND THE TWO GENUINELY NEW FACTS ARE (i) THE SIZE VERSUS THE BAR — UBS HAD MODELLED Won12tn AND PUT BUY-SIDE EXPECTATIONS AT c.Won20-50tn, AGAINST W40trn DELIVERED — AND (ii) THE POLICY CEILING MOVING FROM "WITHIN THE RANGE OF 50%" TO ">50%" OF CUMULATIVE 2025-27 FCF. THE IMPLIED UPSIDE IS TIGHTLY CLUSTERED AT +84% TO +100%; THE QUOTED PRICE TARGETS ARE IN THREE DIFFERENT UNIT CONVENTIONS AND MUST NOT BE LADDERED.** (Barclays, "SK Hynix — Strong signal", 2026-08-19 · J.P. Morgan · Jay Kwon / Sangsik Lee / Neelay Kamath, "SK hynix — W40trn buyback has arrived sooner than expected; what's next for the stock", note header 2026-08-20 on the 19-Aug event · UBS, "SK Hynix — Shareholders return announcement at the upper end of expectations", 2026-08-19.)

- 🔴 **THE EXPECTATIONS BAR IS THE MOST USEFUL LINE IN THE SET, AND IT ALSO CLOSES A TENSION THIS PAGE DELIBERATELY LEFT OPEN. UBS states its own bar: "UBS had modelled Won12tn," and believes "buy-side expectations were c.Won20-50tn."** The print was **W40trn** — **>3x UBS's own model and at the upper end of the buy-side range** (UBS, 2026-08-19). ➜ **The 08-09 Intra-quarter row logged a "SIZING TENSION, LOGGED NOT NETTED: UBS (08-07) sizes a Won12tn 2H26 buyback; JPM models W29.3tn of buyback in 2026E — a 2-6x spread." It resolves ABOVE BOTH. When the low estimate and the high estimate are both exceeded, the correct read is that the whole desk was mis-sizing management's willingness, not that one house was wrong.**
- 🔴 **THE UNIT-CONVENTION TRAP — RECORD THIS BEFORE ANY PT COMPARISON. Barclays covers SK hynix from its EUROPEAN TECHNOLOGY HARDWARE desk and quotes the note in USD ON THE GDR LINE (ticker shown in-note as "SKHY"), not the KRW local line.** Its stated **share count is 7,304.92mn (~10x the local ordinary count)** and its stated **market cap is US$1,136,792mn, ~45% above the UBS local-line figure of US$785bn**. ➜ **So Barclays' US$300.00 PT is NOT comparable in absolute terms to the KRW PTs on this page. Only the implied upside is comparable. Do not put US$300 in a PT ladder next to KRW3.0m.**
- **The three marks, stated in their own units with the only cross-comparable field alongside:**

| House | Rating | Price target | Reference price | **Implied upside (the only comparable field)** | Unit basis |
|---|---|---|--:|--:|---|
| **J.P. Morgan** · Jay Kwon | Overweight | **W2,750,000** (Jun-27) | W1,491,000 (19-Aug-26) | **+84%** | KRW local line |
| **UBS** | Buy | **Won 3,000,000** (12m) | Won 1,500,000 (19-Aug-26) | **+100%** | KRW local line |
| **Barclays** | Overweight (unchanged) | **US$300.00** (unchanged) | US$155.62 (18-Aug-26) | **+92.8%** | ⚠️ **USD, GDR line** |

- **The size of the thing, three ways, all consistent:** **24.07M shares = 3.3% of issued shares as of 2Q26-end** and **3.7% of market cap as of 19 August** (JPM, 2026-08-20); **≈24.1m shares (3.3% of shares outstanding) based on the 18-Aug close of Won1,662,000**, **≈~45% of 2Q26 cash and cash equivalents**, to be **completed within three months**, with a **daily buyback limit of 2.4m shares** (UBS, 2026-08-19). ➜ **Derived, and it matters for the execution-risk question the Catalysts section raises: 24.07mn ÷ 2.4mn/day ≈ 10 TRADING DAYS of minimum execution against a ~3-month (≈62 trading day) window. The daily cap is not remotely binding — if the full W40trn is not bought and retired inside the window, it will be a choice, not a mechanical constraint.**
- 🔴 **THE POLICY UPGRADE, which all three houses read as the durable half:** SK hynix now plans to return **"more than 50%"** of cumulative FCF 2025-27, **previously "within the range of 50%"** (Barclays, 2026-08-19); JPM records the same as **"50% or higher" of accumulated FCF over 25A-27E** (JPM, 2026-08-20); UBS as **"more than 50% of cumulative FCF in the current policy period ('25-'27E)" via buybacks and dividends, details around 3Q26 results** (UBS, 2026-08-19). **Barclays sizes the total return at ≈15% of market cap.**
- **What it does to the models, and the UBS number is the cleanest single mark:** **this announcement ALONE takes the % of 2026E FCF returned to shareholders to 30% in UBS's model, versus current UBSe of 15%** — i.e. a doubling of the return assumption off one disclosure (UBS, 2026-08-19). **Barclays' 2027 model:** quarterly dividend rising to **KRW2,500/share in 2027** with a **final dividend of KRW10,000**, and the **buyback assumption increased to c.KRW200trn in 2027** (assuming the KRW40trn pace continues for 1Q-3Q27 with a step-up in 4Q27) → **c.51% of cumulative FCF 2025-27 returned by YE27, ≈ c.15% of market cap.** ⚠️ **The Barclays note renders the currency as "KWD" in two places — a typo for KRW; the KRW200trn figure is internally consistent with its own stated pacing assumption, so it is recorded as KRW with the typo noted.**
- ⚠️🔴 **THE ONE PLACE THE THREE HOUSES ARE NOT CLUSTERED IS THE 2027 BUYBACK RUN-RATE, AND THE SPREAD IS ENORMOUS. Barclays models c.KRW200trn of buyback in 2027; JPM's 08-09 model on this page has W73.3tn of buyback in 2027E. That is a ~2.7x spread on the same policy, from two houses that agree on the rating and are within 9pts on implied upside.** ➜ **Both land at a broadly similar cumulative-payout conclusion (Barclays c.51% of 25-27 FCF ≈ c.15% of market cap; JPM a 16.7% 2027E cash yield), which tells you the difference is mostly the assumed FCF base and the buyback-vs-dividend split rather than a different view of management. Do not treat either 2027 buyback figure as a forecast to trade — the disclosed facts stop at "additional returns within the same program period, detailed at 3Q26."**
- **The dividend-vs-buyback question, which is the live one into the 3Q26 call, and Barclays' framing of it is the sharpest on the page:** a **greater weighting to dividends** *"could infer greater confidence in cashflow durability,"* but buybacks are *"likely the more preferred (and sensible) option given industry cyclicality — even if we see tightness holding through 2027"* (Barclays, 2026-08-19). ➜ **Note what that concedes: Barclays is bullish enough to see tightness holding through 2027 and STILL prefers the reversible instrument. That is an honest statement about cycle risk from a bull, and it is the mirror image of the "peak-margin cash committed to capacity" bear this page carries in its Síntese.**
- **Stance and framing:** Barclays **"Stay OW"** — a number of debates remain but the shares are seen as inexpensive; **reported ROE TTM 92.68%**. JPM says the announcement is **consistent with its view that an announcement was imminent within the next two months**, views the progressive buyback as **positive for sentiment given size and duration**, expects the company to **pursue additional shareholder returns over the next 2 years**, and repeats *"we believe the worst is behind us"* — sentiment improving gradually from a medium-term horizon, **recommending investors accumulate**. UBS takes it as *"a strong positive for the shares,"* explicitly **considering implications beyond 2027**, and flags the setup: **shares have been on a volatile run since the June 22nd peak, now down 49%.**
- **UBS reported metrics, for the record:** market cap **Won1,092,004b / US$785b**; shares o/s **728m (ORD)**; **free float 66%**; **P/BV (12/26E) 3.0x**; common shareholders' equity (12/26E) **Won354,569b**. ⚠️ **The note's data block prints a "52-wk range Won2,919,000-245,000" — the low would imply a 12x range and is almost certainly a vendor glitch. NOT propagated; logged only as suspect.**
- ⚠️ **PARTIAL DUPLICATION FLAG: the JPM item here is the same house and same event as the 08-19 JPM row already on this page (OW, PT W2,750,000, "the worst is behind us", recommends accumulating, three named catalysts). Net-new from this note only: the W40trn = 3.7% of market cap sizing, the 19-Aug reference price of W1,491,000, the "50% or higher" policy wording, the "imminent within the next two months" prior view, and the explicit two-year forward expectation on further returns. - 🔴 **THE CY2026 EPS GAP IS THE ALPHA AND IT IS A *BELOW-THE-LINE* STORY, WHICH IS UNUSUAL AND WORTH A BRIDGE.** JPM is **−0.7% on revenue and −1.1% on EBIT** yet **+14.1% on EPS**. You cannot get there from operations — it has to come from **tax rate, non-operating income, or share count**. That is the assumption the whole complex now rests on.**
