- ⚠️ **Standing methodology note: the Korean CY-sum bias is NOT confined to CY2027 EPS.** Measured live 2026-08-20 — EPS: CY2026 **−8.3%**, CY2027 **+22.3%**; capex: CY2026 **+7.8%**, CY2027 **+4.0%**. It **inverts sign between years** and is present on capex too. ➤ **For Korean names place broker annual models against `1FY`/`2FY`, never against the CY-sum.**

_Source: `estimates.json` asof 2026-08-20 (98/98 live, 0 FAIL, 0 null prices, 0 byte-identical carry-overs) + ad-hoc `BEST_FPERIOD_OVERRIDE=1FY/2FY` pulls; JPM figures read directly from `000660 KS Equity_MODEL_JPM_Aug 06 2026.xlsx` (`Report-Consol`). See [reconciliation-2026-08-19](_meta/reconciliation-2026-08-19.md) §BBG resolution._


**🆕🔴 2026-08-19 (post-market KST) — THE BUYBACK LANDED, AND IT IS THE BIGGEST SHARE CANCELLATION EVER BY A KOREAN LISTED COMPANY: W40trn (~US$28.6-29bn), 24.07mn shares (3.3% of the 730,492,365 outstanding), TO BE FULLY CANCELLED. The policy change underneath it matters more than the headline: the shareholder-return framework moves from "WITHIN 50%" of FCF to "OVER 50%".** (SK hynix board resolution + company release, 2026-08-19; MS · Shawn Kim/Ryan Kim "PULSE"; JPM · Jay Kwon; Jefferies · Conor O'Mara; MS Sales · Amir Amerian.)
- **The mechanics, all disclosed:** buyback priced off the **prior day's close of KRW 1,662,000**; **on-market purchases run 2026-08-20 → 2026-11-19 (3 months)**, managed by **SK Securities**; shares are then **cancelled and retired**. Execution is a **dual-track** approach — repurchase/cancellation **alongside** cash dividends — and the company says it intends to pursue **additional** returns **within the same program period**, with scale and timing to be announced after board approval at the **3Q26 earnings release**.
- 🔴 **The framing that separates signal from headline (MS Sales · Amerian, 2026-08-19): this W40trn is being executed under the EXISTING Nov-2024 plan, not a new one.** That plan was to accumulate 2025-27 FCF and pay out ~50% afterwards, with the option to pay earlier if FCF generation was strong. **Hynix has generated ~W99trn of FCF from 1Q25-2Q26, so W40trn buyback + W3trn dividend = ~44% of CY25-1H26 FCF — "not far off from the initial plan." What actually changed is the TIMELINE (pulled forward under market/share-price pressure) and the CEILING (">50%").**
- **What is genuinely new, in order of durability:** (1) the **">50% of FCF" ceiling lift**; (2) **for the first time, an explicit statement that returns will be MOSTLY buybacks & cancellations** — previously the buyback-vs-dividend split was unspecified; (3) **non-operating gains are excluded from the FCF base** (Kioxia stake sales, M&A-related cash outflow, and buybacks for employee bonuses do not count) — JPM reads this as "a clear sign to revamp and improve its shareholder return policy"; (4) management is **reviewing increases to both regular AND special dividends**, with newly created **capital-reserve capacity potentially supporting tax-efficient dividends**; (5) **net-cash targets may rise alongside capex — management sees roughly TWO YEARS OF CAPEX as a reasonable buffer** (MS).
- 🔴 **The cumulative cancellation number is the one to carry forward, because it is the peer-relative argument: 39.4mn shares committed for cancellation in the last 8 MONTHS (15.3mn announced Feb-2026 + 24.07mn now) — "the most aggressive among memory peers" (JPM · Jay Kwon).** ⚠️ **The pushback JPM itself records: some investors call the plan "still relatively conservative compared to U.S. memory peers' guidance of returning 100% of EXCESS CASH" — though JPM notes the definition of excess cash there "is unclear."** Amerian adds the same caveat versus **[[SNDK]]**, whose promise is higher.
- 🔴 **The EPS-accretion asymmetry versus [[SAMSUNG]], which is the cleanest new relative-value argument on this page (MS Sales · Amerian, 2026-08-19): Hynix employee bonuses are 100% CASH payouts, whereas the market knows a portion of Samsung's future buybacks will be used for employee compensation and therefore NOT cancelled. So a step-up delivered mostly as buyback-and-cancel is more EPS-accretive at Hynix than at Samsung.** ⚠️ **Two caveats stated by the same author: (1) Samsung may positively surprise on total buyback size; (2) Hynix's own ADR comments imply part of future buybacks may NOT be cancelled.**
- **ADR:** management acknowledged to the sell-side that the current **~2.5% ADR headroom is too small to meaningfully enhance corporate value**, and said float **could be expanded in future WITHOUT issuing new shares** — fungibility remains the constraint. Amerian reads this as converting **treasury shares** into ADR float, i.e. the route the market originally expected before Hynix chose new-share issuance. ⚠️ **This cuts both ways: it lowers assumed long-run dilution versus a new-issue path, but it means some of the buyback may not be retired.**
- **Market context, which is the reason this arrived when it did:** the announcement came **moments after the KOSPI closed down 5.8%**, with **Hynix down 9.4% and [[SAMSUNG]] down 7.8%** on the session and **shares erasing an 8.3% loss in post-market trading** on the news (Jefferies · O'Mara; @zerohedge, 2026-08-19). **JPM frames the drawdown precisely: since the June 22 peak the stock is −49%, versus memory peers −26% and the KOSPI −29%**, driven by AI-capex-sustainability and open-source-model fears and **exacerbated by the 2Q26 earnings miss**. The same-day sell-off trigger cited across desks was the **US long-bond sell-off plus reports that [[ANTHROPIC]]'s ARR was BELOW expectations at ~$65bn at end-July**.
- **Sell-side verdict:** **MS OW, PT W2,600,000** — "management is raising the bar… **this is not the end of the program**." **JPM OW, PT W2,750,000** — "we believe **the worst is behind us**," recommends accumulating, expects further returns over the next two years. ⚠️ **Amerian's dissent is the honest one and worth keeping: "the ADR's response… rightfully reflects that market is accepting the need for MORE EARNINGS REVISIONS to drive stocks higher rather than just shareholder return."**

**🆕🔴 2026-08-18 (/run-inbox) — A SECOND HOUSE NOW HAS [[SAMSUNG]] TAKING THE HBM BIT-SHARE LEAD IN 2027, AND THE NUMBER PUTS SK HYNIX SECOND FOR THE FIRST TIME ON THIS PAGE.**
- 🔴 **THE CALL: UBS forecasts 2027 HBM bit supply share at Samsung 40% / SK HYNIX 38% / [[MU]] 22%** — Samsung "slightly ahead" and surpassing SK Hynix — with Samsung's HBM4 yields and performance improved, though thermal issues may persist (UBS · Nicolas Gaudois, "Key Call: Samsung Electronics — Implying ROE below pre-AI levels", 2026-08-07). **[[SAMSUNG]]'s own house broker puts it higher still: KB Securities has Samsung's HBM4 share specifically reaching 44%, "the largest"** (KB Securities · Jeff Kim, 2026-08-10). ➤ **Read the two together and the claim is no longer one house's forecast: two independent sell-side models, on two different share metrics (all-HBM bits vs HBM4 specifically), both put Samsung #1 in 2027. This page has carried SK Hynix's HBM leadership as a structural fact — it is now a contested one, on a dated horizon.**
- ⚠️ **BUT NOTE THE MECHANISM BEFORE ADOPTING IT: UBS reaches Samsung 40% while simultaneously CUTTING Samsung's 2027 HBM bit shipments to 22bn Gb (+95% y/y) from 24bn prior. A share gain delivered alongside a volume cut is a denominator effect as much as a Samsung ramp — it implies UBS cut the rest of the industry's 2027 HBM bits harder than Samsung's.** ➤ **The falsifiable version of this call is therefore not "does Samsung ship more HBM" but "does SK Hynix ship LESS than currently modelled in 2027." That is the number to watch, and it is checkable against SK Hynix's own capacity disclosures rather than against Samsung's claims.** **Supporting capacity context from the same note: Samsung retains more deployable new wafer capacity than peers (remaining P4 floor space, plus P5 pulled in to first clean-room readiness by 3Q27), and industry front-end DRAM capacity allocated to HBM reaches 500k/690k wpm by end-2026/2027 = 24%/30% of total.** [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/20260807_UBS_005930-KS_Key_Call-_Samsung_Electronics_-Implying_ROE_below_pre_AI.html)
- **For the record, a dated Bernstein mark swept late from P:: Outperform, PT KRW3,300,000 (ticker table, 17-Jul close KRW1,830,000)** — Bernstein · Mark Newman + Mark Li, "Global Memory: New Memory LTAs Part 3", 2026-07-20. **Same note flags the structural LTA asymmetry that cuts against the Korean names: Bernstein describes [[MU]] and [[SNDK]] as "the most proactive in pursuing agreements backed by financial guarantees, while Asia-based suppliers have taken a more SELECTIVE approach."** ➤ **In an upcycle that is upside participation; in a downcycle it is the absence of the earnings floor the US names have bought. Worth carrying as a downside-shape difference, not a quality judgement.** [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/20260720_Bernstein_SNDK_Global_Memory-_New_Memory_LTAs_Part_3_Bull_vs_Bear_Deb_1.html)

**🆕 2026-08-12 — THE KIOXIA STAKE IS CONFIRMED AS INDIRECT (VIA AN SPC), BOTH NAMES TRADED UP ON IT, AND KOREA IS ATTRACTING SOVEREIGN CAPITAL INTO THE MEMORY COMPLEX.** (Jefferies Asian TMT End of Day / Conor O'Mara, 2026-08-12 — sales & trading, not Research.)
- **Structure clarified: "Hynix has become the largest shareholder in [[KIOXIA]] via an SPC"** — i.e. **held through a special-purpose company, not on balance sheet directly.** ⚠️ **Worth carrying: an SPC structure changes the consolidation, governance and antitrust optics of the stake versus a direct holding. The 08-11 entry below logged the register flip; this dates and structures it.** **Both names traded up on the news.**
- **[[SAMSUNG]] up more, "on continued positive sentiment about more aggressive shareholder return plans."**
- **Sovereign capital inbound: Bloomberg reported Temasek is seeking to approach South Korea to invest in [[SAMSUNG]] and SK Hynix, and Korea will set up a new sovereign wealth fund with KRW 1trn to invest in AI and robotics.**
- **⚠️ THE CONTRARIAN FLAG, and it is worth logging precisely because the desk disagrees with it: Chosun Ilbo ran a piece asking whether this rotation into large-cap laggards "is a sign of another top as that's what happened before the peak 2 months ago."** Jefferies' Korea analyst **Shawn Park does not think so, "given we are nowhere near peak in all the tech shares that have sold off in Korea."** ➜ **A local-press top-call against a desk rebuttal; neither is evidence, but the fact that the question is being asked in Korean press two months after a peak is itself positioning information.**

**🆕🔴 2026-08-11 — TWO ITEMS, AND THEY SIT AWKWARDLY TOGETHER: SK Hynix said it will raise NAND output in China by 50%, and it became [[KIOXIA]]'s largest shareholder. SKHY +5%, partly on buyback anticipation — but it "continues to lag" [[SAMSUNG]].**
- **Capacity: "SKHY +5% as they announced they would increase NAND production in China by 50% as investors get excited about buyback"** (TMTB EOD Wrap, 2026-08-11). ⚠️ **Headline-level — no capacity base, no timeline, no wafer-start figure in the source. Do not model it; chase the disclosure.**
- **🔴 Register: "SK Hynix becomes Kioxia's largest shareholder as Toshiba trims stake"** (DIGITIMES, 2026-08-11). ⚠️ **Also headline-level — no stake percentages, transaction size or consideration disclosed.** ➜ **Filed in full on [[KIOXIA]]'s page with the three governance questions it raises (independent pricing/capacity, [[SNDK]]/Kioxia JV fab governance at Yokkaichi/Kitakami, and the exit path for the remaining Bain-led consortium holders). BofA hosts KIOXIA IR on 2026-08-18 — the venue to ask them.**
- **⚠️ THE TENSION IS THE POINT, and it is a NAND-discipline question rather than a governance one: announcing a 50% China NAND capacity increase in the same week you become the largest shareholder of a NAND competitor is a conflict, not a synergy.** It also cuts against the "NAND supply discipline" leg of the LTA bull case this wiki carries — and it lands while **Bernstein is raising NAND equipment spending to +58% in 2027 and +42% in 2028 on "NAND is recovering"** (Bernstein / Rasgon & Dai, 2026-08-11 desk distribution). **Equipment forecasts that aggressive are consistent with capacity being added, not with discipline.**
- **⚠️🔴 THE RELATIVE-PERFORMANCE PROBLEM GOT A NUMBER FROM A COMPETITOR'S ANALYST, and it is unflattering: Jefferies' Jeff Kim notes "Samsung up 4%… while Hynix continues to lag," and argues "the market cap of SEC, the global DRAM leader, stands at a 4% DISCOUNT TO [[MU]], the third largest player. This seems abnormal" — expecting SEC's HBM ASP to more than double in 2027 with HBM4 share reaching 44% (largest)** (Jefferies / Jeff Kim via Conor O'Mara, 2026-08-11). ➜ **A 44% HBM4 share for Samsung is only reachable at someone's expense, and on the existing splits that someone is primarily SK Hynix. ⚠️ NOT ADOPTED — it must be reconciled against the HBM4 share splits already on this page and on [hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md) before use, and it comes from the house that covers the competitor.**
- **Sector context that has not changed: the same Jefferies note reports "as of Aug 2026, only 60% of big techs' memory demand is being met… the shortage should persist for at least three years," with hyperscalers moving to 5-year LTAs from 3-year (5y base + 1y rollover). ⚠️ Against that, MS's prime-brokerage data shows memory still being SOLD / DE-GROSSED as investors weigh "peak rate of change and whether LTAs are 'good or bad'." Both are logged on [hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md); the tension is unresolved, and SKHY's lagging tape is the clearest expression of it.**

**🆕 2026-08-09/10 — the -15% week, and JPM's rebuttal (JPM / Jay Kwon, 2026-08-09; OW, PT W2,750,000 Jun-27; price W1,416,000 at 07-Aug).** SKH fell **15% in the week to 07-Aug (vs Kospi -5%, SEC -9%)** on three headlines, all of which JPM pushes back on:
- **HBM "50% discount" reports are inaccurate.** JPM's own assumption is **below +40% y/y HBM ASP from 2026E** — deliberately conservative — on three grounds: (1) suppliers prioritise **DDR5/LPDDR5/NAND LTAs, which carry a margin premium over HBM**; (2) NVDA is SKH's largest customer with multi-year sourcing leverage; (3) HBM re-prices annually, so a soft 2027 print is recoverable. Upside risk to EPS if SKH lands above.
- **W54tn ($38.1bn) infrastructure capex disclosed:** **W35.2tn for a DRAM plant at Yongin Y2** (vs W31tn for Yongin Phase 1; groundbreaking **July 2027**, mainly capacity **beyond 2031**) and **W19.1tn for NAND at Cheongju M17** (groundbreaking February, first cleanroom **end-2028**, investment through April 2031). JPM reads it as fab-space securing after the **1mn-wafer-by-2030E** target, not a demand call.
- **Shareholder return pulled forward:** per a 08-07 DART filing SKH will finalise and announce additional shareholder-return measures **by 3Q26-end** (previously "within year-end" at the 2Q26 briefing). JPM models **cumulative FCF >W800tn over three years** and flags a **16-20% two-year yield potential for SEC/SKH** (Global Memory note, 08-09).
- **Solidigm US IPO: "under review," reconfirm within one month** (DART, 08-05). JPM is cautiously negative on the logic — NAND margins top 70%, the balance sheet funds capex without dilution, and a listing may collide with **Korea's duplicate-listing rules** from the early-2026 commercial-code change.

**Next company-specific catalysts (JPM, 08-09):** (1) shareholder-return program update **by Sep-end**; (2) **HBM contract price update by Sep-end**; (3) **US subsidiary listing decision within one month**.

**🆕 2026-08-10 — Chongqing packaging stake: "no decision made."** Responding to a KRX inquiry, SKH said no decision has been taken on a reported **~W4tn (~US$3bn) stake sale in its Chongqing, China packaging plant**, that it is "reviewing various options to strengthen the competitiveness of its packaging business," and will disclose within one month (regulatory filing via Jefferies / Conor O'Mara, 2026-08-10; DIGITIMES, 2026-08-10). Separately, SKH faces **a second USITC investigation from MonolithIC 3D covering HBM and 3D NAND patents** (DIGITIMES, 2026-08-10).

**Q2 2026 (reported 2026-07-28) — first headline miss of the cycle, but in line with where the sell-side had already cut.** **Operating profit ₩60.54tn vs consensus ₩64.2tn**; **revenue ₩79.3tn vs ~₩83.9tn expected** (Jefferies · Conor O'Mara, 2026-07-28). Crucially, the "miss" is against a stale consensus: **local Korean brokers had already cut operating profit to ~₩60tn and Jefferies' own Jeff Kim was at ₩62tn**, so O'Mara's read is that the print "shouldn't be coming as much of a shock" — it is in line with the most recent numbers rather than with the screen consensus. **Profitability remains extreme: GM 83%, operating margin 76%, EBITDA margin 81%** — "all pretty healthy despite the perceived miss." Sequentially this is still a step up from Q1's ₩52.6tn revenue / ₩37.6tn OP, i.e. the *level* accelerated even as the *beat* disappeared.

**What management said (Q2 2026 call, 2026-07-28, via Jefferies):**
- **DRAM:** Q2 bit growth **up high-single-digits with ASP +30% q/q**; **2026 DRAM bit growth guided to mid-20s%**; **Q3 bit growth +10%, focused on server products.**
- **HBM4:** **shipments began in Q2** at customer-required speeds, **ramping further in 2H**; **1cnm-based HBM4E samples supplied to a major customer in 1H**. Jefferies attributes the revenue shortfall specifically to **"the delay in the ramp of HBM4."**
- **SOCAMM2** modules "grew significantly" in Q2 and are **fully commercialised on 1C**.
- **Market view:** the scope of memory demand is **broadening as AI evolves into agentic form** and spreads across services — AI-server sophistication driving structural demand across **HBM / server DRAM / eSSD**; improvements in models and software efficiency expected to *expand* AI usage and infrastructure demand rather than reduce it. Big-Tech customers are expanding infrastructure investment and memory procurement on surging AI service growth and a **shortage of computing capacity**. **PC and smartphone sales saw a temporary adjustment due to challenges securing memory volumes**, with recovery expected as supply tightness eases.
- **Capex:** full-year capex guided to rise **as much as 80% to the high-₩40tn range** (Culpium · Tim Culpan, 2026-07-28) — a very large step-up and, per Jefferies, being **pulled in**. **No buyback announced despite a large net-cash position** (Jefferies, 2026-07-28).

**Net-new from the full call transcript (Bloomberg FINAL, call 2026-07-28, ingested 2026-07-29)** — the primary source behind the relays above:
- **The bottom half of the P&L is a Kioxia story: net non-operating profit ₩62.2tn**, driven by **₩63.3tn of gains from the sale and valuation of investment assets** (the Kioxia stake sale, per the closing Q&A) + ₩1.1tn FX gains → **pretax ₩122.7tn, net profit ₩93.9tn, net margin 118%** (net > revenue). Balance sheet after it: **cash ₩88tn (+₩33.6tn q/q), net cash ₩69.4tn, D/E 7%**; D&A only ₩4tn; EBITDA ₩64.6tn (81%).
- **LTA framework, primary detail: negotiations CONCLUDED with ~10 customers including key customers**, more in discussion; terms "normally around **five years**"; pricing "not uniform" — a range of mechanisms designed to address price volatility; and **deposits incorporated** "to support contract fulfillment and enhance the visibility and reliability of customers' mid-to-long-term demand plans." Management won't say what share of sales LTAs will cover — "an appropriate level." (This is the demand-side half of the LTA/deposit chain the page already carries tool-side via ASML's prepaid EUV orders — BofA, 2026-04-15.)
- **NAND, the underreported half of the print:** ASP **+mid-50% q/q**; **eSSD revenue 2x q/q**, Solidigm 30TB+ eSSDs **3x q/q**; Q3 bit guide only **+low-single-digit** (vs DRAM +~10%); **321-layer to ~50% of domestic capacity by YE** as planned; industry demand high-10s% (vs DRAM mid-20s%). Strategy framing: AI storage is moving **inference-centric — eSSD as a core tier of the AI memory hierarchy**, with **KV-cache offloading and "near-GPU storage"** named as the new-tier products and QLC eSSD the HDD-replacement/AI-data-lake solution; "it will not be a single SSD that handles all the workload."
- **DRAM ASP mix, management's own explanation** (the +30% that disappointed): "shipments of some high-value-add products were pushed back into the second half" — 2H bit growth guided **above 1H**, with HBM4 ramp + 1c conventional DRAM lifting blended ASP; sales mix managed by "demand visibility, long-term customer relationships and supply-demand dynamics," not short-term price.
- **HBM roadmap specifics:** HBM4 yield/quality "**nearing the levels of HBM3E**, which is already in the maturity stage" — focus now on ramping capacity; **HBM4E volume production from 2027** (samples delivered 1H); beyond hybrid bonding, **iHBM for HBM5-class products integrates cooling elements in-package, cutting thermal resistance >30%**. **2027 HBM volume + pricing negotiations are underway and "progressing smoothly"** — and management concedes the conventional-DRAM price surge "may also have some influence on our HBM pricing discussions" (while listing wafer intensity, TSV/packaging capacity and opportunity cost as the real pricing inputs) — i.e. the commodity super-cycle is a tailwind, not a cap, to HBM pricing.
- **Capex drivers behind the high-₩40tn guide, stated:** M15X mass-production schedule **pulled forward** + investments to expand capacity fast after the **Yongin Fab1 clean-room opening in early 2027**; mid-to-long-term: **P&T7 (advanced packaging) + M17 (new NAND base)** + a **new domestic semiconductor cluster beyond Yongin** — staged against "customer demand visibility and investment efficiency." On overseas (US/Japan) expansion chatter: siting will be decided on power/water/talent/supply-chain/customer access, but "**at this time there are no further decisions made** in addition to the investment that has already been announced."
- **Demand-debate answer (to JPM's leasing/efficient-models question):** DC leasing + efficient models are "not a sign of AI investment slowdown but a transition toward **higher utilization of infrastructure already built at scale** and accelerated monetization"; efficiency "lowers price and adoption barriers," evidenced by "explosive demand for the recent high-efficiency AI models"; **"our major customers are still requesting more memory supply."** Project timing may slip on "physical constraints such as power availability and data center construction," but investment stays solid "beyond next year." **PC/mobile: "temporary sales adjustments" because device makers can't SECURE memory** — the shortage is rationing the consumer end.
- **Oversupply pushback (to the capacity-expansion question):** expansion is "based on visibility secured in partnerships with customers" (LTAs), executed "flexibly in alignment with confirmed customer demand" — "we do not believe our medium-to-long-term investment plans will lead to oversupply **right away**."
- **ADR mechanics:** the July-10 Nasdaq listing was "the **largest ever for a foreign company's IPO in the US**"; **from July 30 ADRs freely convert into Korean shares, but the reverse is limited** — stock→ADR conversion needs regulatory filings ("several weeks") and total ADRs are capped at the **17,790,000-share conversion limit** (= the offering size); raising the ADR proportion "no decisions made." (Confirms and details the @jukan05 07-14 fungibility item in Catalysts.) **Shareholder returns:** additional measures under review "from multiple angles," to be communicated **within the year** — with disclosure currently constrained by ADR-offering regulatory restrictions (the stated reason the print carried no buyback).

⚠️ **The tape around this print matters more than the print.** SK Hynix reported into the worst two sessions of the year for Asian semis: on 2026-07-27 the **KOSPI fell 10.7% with a sell sidecar and a circuit breaker (the eighth of the year), Samsung -12% and SK Hynix -13%**, on two things landing together — **CXMT's IPO debut at +466% (China's second-largest IPO ever) making the China memory threat feel immediate**, and Citadel flagging a live Fed hike with swaps pricing ~40% odds of 25bps (Jefferies "Silk Road," 2026-07-28). On the print itself the stock opened **-6% pre-market and recovered to -2.3%**, while **[[MU]] fell another ~4% after hours on top of an 8.9% decline during the session** (Jefferies, 2026-07-28). Culpium's framing of the day: *"SK Hynix Sales and Profit Miss, Bears Roar."*

📌 **The pricing-discipline question this print reopens.** Jefferies: the miss "is just going to fuel the view that **Hynix has been less aggressive than Samsung on pricing given its commitments to [[NVDA]] on HBM**" — i.e. the NVIDIA relationship that is this page's central moat argument is now also being read as a pricing constraint. That sits directly against the sector call from Jefferies research the same morning: **memory price hikes are tracking 15-20% versus the 25-30% expected**, 2027 consensus requires **~US$2tn of US AI capex to be achievable**, and **memory prices peak around 1H27** (Jefferies "Silk Road," 2026-07-28) — with **HBM4 costs jumping to US$31-32/GB**. See [themes/hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md).

🎯 **The pre-print bogey — and MS's explicit call that the reaction, not the number, was the test (Morgan Stanley webcast "2Q26 memory, Intel implications and ARM preview" · Sean, 2026-07-28).** Recorded ahead of the print ("we do have earnings for Hynix tomorrow. And we have Samsung on Thursday"), MS's Asia memory host laid out where expectations sat: **"for Hynix the numbers were anywhere between 60 to 65 trillion. Our number is currently at 65 trillion. We've seen some adjustment towards the 60 handle."** Against the **actual ₩60.54tn OP**, that puts the print **at the very bottom of the buyside range and ~7% below MS's own ₩65tn** — i.e. the drift toward "the 60 handle" that Sean flagged was directionally right and the houses that had not cut were the ones marked. More useful than the number is the framing he attached to it: **"I think the number itself will not be that meaningful. The reaction to any number would actually give us a better sense of whether this selloff is actually at the tail end. If tomorrow we do have numbers and stocks bouncing, I think that would be good news for the sector."** Read against what actually happened — SK Hynix **opened -6% and recovered to -2.3%** while [MU](MU.md) fell ~8.9% intraday and a further ~4% after hours — the reaction test came back **ambiguous-to-mildly-constructive for Hynix specifically** and clearly negative for the complex. On the buyback the print did not deliver, Sean's pre-print read was non-committal: **"For Hynix, my view is that it could happen, given the current volatility, but again, we have no visibility on that."** He also placed the name at the centre of the CXMT event: CXMT closed its Shanghai debut at a **US$492bn market cap, the 25th most valuable company in the world**, and **"you can probably say that that's quite proportionate to the amount of market cap that was taken away from stocks like Hynix"** — an explicit framing of the CXMT re-rating as a *transfer* from the incumbent, though he bounds the fundamental risk to the outer years ("probably doesn't impact this cycle, maybe in the outer years"). (Morgan Stanley webcast "2Q26 memory, Intel implications and ARM preview" · Sean, 2026-07-28) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_28_2q26_ms_memory_intel_implications_and_arm_preview.html)

⚙️ **The second-order read on the HBM4 delay: less HBM means MORE commodity DRAM, and it is now showing up in the industry bit number (Morgan Stanley webcast · Sean, 2026-07-28).** Sean attributes part of the upward revision in **TrendForce industry DRAM bit growth — from "a 25% type of grow to now reaching 31%" this year, and ~24% next year** — to **"Hynix probably HBM3E, where no HBM4 will actually reduce the amount of total HBM and more DRAM will be produced as a result"** (alongside [Samsung](SAMSUNG.md)'s P4 second-floor cleanroom). This is the supply-side mirror of the revenue miss already on this page: **the same HBM4 ramp delay that cost the top line is simultaneously freeing wafer into commodity DRAM at a moment when commodity-DRAM margins are competitive with HBM** — which is precisely the "HBM-vs-EPS paradox" this page carries from MS's Sean Kim (04-14: "it's better if you don't have HBM" because commodity-DRAM returns are now higher). Two-sided, and the page should hold both: the delay is a **share/mix negative on the NVDA franchise** and a **near-term bit/EPS cushion**, and it is also **incremental industry supply** feeding the cycle-durability bear. On NAND the analogous driver is YMTC (third line, **50,000 wafers** next year), taking NAND bit growth from high-teens to **20-25% next year**. (Morgan Stanley webcast "2Q26 memory, Intel implications and ARM preview" · Sean, 2026-07-28)

**Q1 2026 (reported 2026-04-23, English call):** Revenue **52.6tn KRW (+60% QoQ, +198% YoY)**, operating profit **37.6tn** (≈doubled QoQ), **OP margin 72%** (+13pp QoQ), net profit 40.3tn (77% net margin), EBITDA 41.3tn. Net cash 35tn KRW (cash 54.3tn). Management: AI-infrastructure demand drove "significant price increases for both DRAM and NAND"; customers "prioritize securing volume over price." HBM4 demand "far exceeding current supply capacity," supplying HBM3E + HBM4 simultaneously. NAND shipments ~-10% QoQ on a high prior-quarter base (SK hynix IR / Yahoo Q1 FY26; CNBC, 2026-04-23). **Trajectory across the 4 saved calls:** OP margin 41% (Q2'25) → 47% (Q3'25) → 58% (Q4'25) → 72% (Q1'26) — a vertical margin ramp on HBM/DRAM ASP.

**Capacity build-out — narrowing the DRAM gap, dominant in HBM (StoneX/TrendForce "Memory expert call — Avril", 2026-06-23):** SK Hynix exits 2026 the #2 DRAM maker at **~610K wafers/month** (vs Samsung ~740K) but reaches **710K by end-2027, narrowing the gap to Samsung** (which adds only ~45K). It remains the **dominant HBM supplier with the largest HBM wafer cap at ~200K end-2026** (vs Samsung 180K, Micron 100K). **Yongin FAB1 (200-250K, geometry-dependent)** is expected ready end-2027, but chip output only lands in 2028 given equipment install (~1-1.5 quarters) plus the 2-quarter wafer-in-to-wafer-out cycle; **Yongin FAB2 was just announced** and is "far along." Clean-room space — not equipment or demand — is the binding constraint through end-2028. **Risk:** Samsung's improving HBM4 raises a real possibility it overtakes SK Hynix as #1 HBM supplier in 2027 (same source; cross-ref SAMSUNG). **Anthropic** is pursuing a deal with SK Hynix (server DRAM + eSSD 2027 / HBM 2028), implying an in-house Anthropic ASIC (same source).

**HBM4 cadence / pricing (expert detail, Jefferies Techknowledge, 2025-12-17):** >70% of SK hynix's 2026 HBM bits are still HBM3 (12-high crossover hit in April); **HBM4 does not cross over in 2026 — blended ≤50% of mix**, ramping in 2H26. HBM pricing "finalized" and tracking at start-of-year expectations, with SK hynix guiding **HBM ASP +40-50% YoY in 2026**; cannot renegotiate HBM3E prices and there is "more upside in demand but limited capacity." Blended HBM margin can dip in 1H26 (less HBM4 in the mix), then hold once HBM4 is mainstream. SK hynix frames **~70% OP margins as the level "needed to maintain R&D," and "not high enough"** to fund the greenfield/hybrid-bonding capex step-up — pushing back on the peak-margin bear narrative. It is capacity-rationing non-NVDA: with limited M15X output it prioritizes HBM4 and "cannot increase non-NVIDIA above 20%" in 2026, having declined ASIC/AMD volume (Jefferies Techknowledge, 2025-12-17).

**Rubin (R200-class) HBM supply split; HBM4 engineering-sample specs; Yongin Phase 1 timing; 2026 DRAM WFE capex (SemiAnalysis "Memory Mania", 2026-02-06):** SemiAnalysis sizes the first-12-months Rubin HBM supply split at **SK hynix ~60% / Samsung ~30% / Micron ~0%** — SK hynix's HBM3/HBM3E execution has become the industry benchmark for supplier differentiation. On HBM4 engineering samples, SK hynix is running **~10 Gbps pin speed** (in line with Samsung, ahead of Micron), with **lower jitter and stronger package execution than Samsung's** competing part. On capacity, **Yongin Phase 1's cleanroom does not come online until Feb-2027**, with only limited wafer output starting 3Q27 — but the **M15X fab and Yongin Phase 1 schedules have both been pulled in/accelerated** versus prior timelines. On capex, SemiAnalysis models **2026 DRAM WFE capex growth of +34% at SK hynix — the highest of the three** (vs Samsung +26%, Micron +20%). SemiAnalysis notes it "turned bullish" on Samsung in its November note while maintaining its existing bullish view on SK hynix (SemiAnalysis "Memory Mania: How a Once-in-Four-Decades Shortage Is Fueling a Memory Boom", 2026-02-06).

**HBM4/HBM4E primer — SK hynix the lead supplier (Atlas Peak Research "HBM4 and HBM4E: The Memory Wall Becomes the System Architecture Problem," 2026-04-03):** JEDEC finalized HBM4 in April 2025 — **2048-bit interface / 32 channels / 2.048 TB/s per stack** at the 8 Gb/s baseline (~2x HBM3E's 1024-bit / 1.229 TB/s), up to 64 GB/cube; HBM4E extends to **16 Gb/s / ~4.096 TB/s**. SK hynix **shipped 12-layer HBM4 samples in March 2025 and completed HBM4 development in September 2025**, with **NVIDIA Vera Rubin the key platform** and HBM4E readiness targeted **~2027**. At **GTC 2026 SK hynix demonstrated a custom cHBM design with a "stream DQ" architecture** that continuously feeds data to compute units in a queue-based structure — the base die becoming a semi-custom integration layer is "the next competitive frontier." Corroborates SK hynix's HBM4/Rubin lead and the value-capture-into-base-die/packaging thesis this page tracks (Atlas Peak, 2026-04-03).

**MR-MUF packaging moat, all-around power TSVs, and the Hanmi/Hanwha bonding-tool drama (SemiAnalysis "Scaling the Memory Wall: HBM", 2025-08-11):** SK hynix's proprietary **MR-MUF** (mass reflow molded underfill, co-developed with NAMICS) gives better thermal dissipation than the NCF process Micron/Samsung use, and sidesteps thermal-compression bonding (TCB) entirely in favor of batch mass reflow — more productive at volume. On HBM3E, SK hynix moved to **all-around power TSVs (vs. two banks previously)** — roughly 6x more TSVs, cutting IR drop for VPP by up to 75%. This packaging lead is also SK hynix's single point of tool-supply fragility: **Hanmi held ~100% share of TC bonders at SK hynix until fall 2024**, when Hynix diversified and ordered from **Hanwha**; Hanmi responded by **pulling its field-service teams from SK hynix's fabs in early April 2025**. The Hanwha tools then went undelivered, and the **ASMPT bonders Hynix had ordered in fall 2024 turned out not to work for HBM3E 12-high** — forcing SK hynix into the position of placing a **placating order back with Hanmi** to keep HBM3E 12-high packaging running. SK hynix also **lacks in-house advanced-logic design capability** (like Micron), so it depends on TSMC plus design/IP partners for the HBM4 custom base die (N12/N3 variants). Its proprietary TSV layouts are framed as a core moat, with the potential to **charge licensing fees** for TSV-layout exposure going forward; NVIDIA's rising HBM content is described as **primarily benefiting SK hynix** (SemiAnalysis "Scaling the Memory Wall: The Rise and Roadmap of HBM", 2025-08-11).

**The HBM4 pin-speed bake-off — the supplier-side read from the AMD launch, and a two-tier HBM4 market (SemiAnalysis "Can AMD break the CUDA Moat? AMD Advancing AI 2026", 2026-07-24).** AMD's MI455X ships **12 stacks of HBM4 = 432GB per package** at **23.3 TB/s** vs Nvidia Rubin and Google at **8 stacks = 288GB**, Rubin at **22 TB/s** — a near-tie on bandwidth *despite AMD running a 50% wider bus*, because AMD clocks its HBM4 at a pin speed of **7.6 Gbps while Nvidia targets 10.7 Gbps, 40% faster**. SemiAnalysis is explicit this was deliberate and supplier-facing: "**Nvidia aggressively raised its targeted HBM4 pin speeds last year, well above the original JEDEC HBM4 specification. This change was precisely driven by the need to make up for the memory bandwidth deficit to AMD MI455X.**" The cost landed on the memory makers — the change "**was challenging for memory suppliers to keep up with. Memory suppliers had to rework their HBM4 to deliver the spec Nvidia was targeting. This also pushed out upstream output for Rubin but as of today these issues have been finally resolved**" — and the standing implication is that **Nvidia must use a much higher-quality bin than AMD** for the same generation. AMD and Nvidia are the only two shipping HBM4 this year. **Read-through for SK hynix, the #1 HBM supplier and Nvidia's primary source:** an above-JEDEC pin-speed target means **tighter binning, lower effective yield on the top bin, and a widening quality/price spread** between suppliers that can hit 10.7 Gbps and those that cannot — a **share-and-mix question, not a volume question**. It also means HBM4 is **two markets, not one**: the AMD 7.6 Gbps socket is a materially easier bin than the Nvidia 10.7 Gbps socket, so being short of Nvidia's spec locks a supplier out of the *premium* bin rather than out of HBM4. Two page-level cross-checks worth holding together, carefully: (1) this page already carries SemiAnalysis's own datapoint that SK hynix's **HBM4 engineering samples ran ~10 Gbps** with **lower jitter and stronger package execution than Samsung's** ("Memory Mania", 2026-02-06) — which sits just under the 10.7 Gbps Rubin target, i.e. the rework SemiAnalysis describes is precisely the kind of gap the industry had to close; and (2) the "**issues have been finally resolved**" language is the first explicit statement on this page that the Rubin HBM4 upstream-output delay is **behind** the supply chain. **SemiAnalysis does not say which supplier wins the 10.7 Gbps bin, and nothing here should be read as saying SK hynix does** — the useful conclusion is that the binning spread itself is now a live differentiator between the three suppliers, on top of capacity. (SemiAnalysis "Can AMD break the CUDA Moat? AMD Advancing AI 2026", 2026-07-24) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_24_ENC_Can_AMD_break_the_CUDA_Moat_AMD_Advancing_AI_2026_email.html)

## Debate / thesis
- **Bull — structural HBM/DRAM supercycle, sold-out book.** "Sold out 2026" confirmed at DRAM + HBM level (Q3'25 call); tight supply expected into 2027; HBM4 in mass production ahead of NVDA Rubin (initial HBM4 shipments with a TSMC-fabbed logic base die in 2H26, DeepResearch TSM). HBM4 itself is a step-up, not a commodity refresh: 2048-bit bus (2x HBM3E) lifting bandwidth ~1.5x to ~1.6TB/s, and a custom logic base die from HBM4 onward that strengthens vendor lock-in via proprietary TSV layouts and lets memory makers earn a higher GM (a "corkage" charge for customer-supplied base dies) — with only SK hynix and Micron viable suppliers (SemiAnalysis HBM). JEF (Conor, 2026-04-07) flagged a Jefferies KBI note arguing Korea memory can out-earn the hyperscalers; SK Hynix ~20% pullback called a buy opportunity (JEF Titu/Wong, briefing 2026-06-19). DRAM ASP +50-60% QoQ in 2Q26, +20% QoQ 3Q26; NAND +75-100% QoQ in 2Q (memory complex table, briefing 2026-06-19). @rwang07 (Ray Wang): "SK Hynix is the next 3 trillion-dollar company" (briefing 2026-06-19).
  - **Jefferies' "Korea out-earns Nvidia" thesis quantified (Conor, 2026-04-07):** Jefferies' KBI analyst (Jeff Kim) put out a note arguing **Samsung will earn more operating profit than Nvidia next year** yet trades at ~1/5 the market cap — and by read-across the same HBM-pricing economics flow to SK Hynix (Jefferies "Asia tech", 2026-04-07). The mechanism: **HBM4 is ~35% more expensive than HBM3E**, and (per a Dec-2025 Jefferies check) Hynix's HBM operating margins are flat rather than the −20% feared — i.e. NVIDIA/hyperscalers are absorbing the 35% HBM4 price hike (same call). The Jefferies' bull-vs-bear framing: there is "massive disagreement on memory," with one camp (incl. Jefferies) seeing a mid-cycle correction and another seeing "no cycle whatsoever" — both can be true on timing; the rally so far has been ASP-led and now needs to convert to **unit growth into 2027** since hyperscaler memory spend already runs ~30-35% of their operating cash flow (Samsung's own framing of the ceiling — Jefferies "Asia tech", 2026-04-07).
  - **HBM4 base-die supply chain (BofA TSMC expert, 2026-04-09):** the TSMC ecosystem supplies the **logic/communication base die under HBM** to SK Hynix (and Samsung joining the alliance); the expert framed NVIDIA's Jensen Huang as the supply-chain "firefighter" personally chasing Hynix capacity — corroborating Hynix as the primary HBM source into Rubin (BofA "Best ideas", 2026-04-09).
  - **Memory-led TAM expansion underpins the ASP thesis (BofA ASML callback, 2026-04-15):** BofA's Global Semis team raised its **2026 semiconductor market forecast from ~$1.0tn to ~$1.3tn**, "the vast majority driven by higher ASPs for memory" — explicitly lifting the **DRAM market size from just below $200bn to ~$400bn**, and introducing a **2030 semi market of ~$2tn** (vs ASML's own ~$1tn+ CMD-Nov-24 framing). For the litho read-through, ASML's analyst raised 2027/28 EUV-for-DRAM deliveries by 4 systems (2 Samsung, **2 Hynix**); **Korea was 45% of ASML Q1 revenue, +40% YoY** — the clearest tool-side confirmation of the Korean DRAM/HBM capex ramp (BofA ASML callback, 2026-04-15).
  - **Hynix is the gating item on the Rubin ramp (JPM/Gokul TSMC preview, 2026-04-02):** the Rubin output ramp (Q3 first output, big Q4 step-up) is "**limited by the availability of HBM4 from the three vendors, and Hynix is the one which is a little bit more of a limiting factor right now — especially the high-speed versions**." JPM caps **2026 Rubin at ~2–2.5mn units max**, with NVIDIA offsetting the HBM4 constraint by raising **Blackwell/GB300** orders — i.e. Hynix HBM4 yield/speed is the binding constraint on NVIDIA's flagship volume (JPM "TSMC preview", 2026-04-02).
  - **Longevity-of-cycle re-rate (GS, Junie Yoon):** GS argues the cycle's longevity is underestimated — Hynix at 5-6x earnings means the market disbelieves durability; GS's 9x target is "conservative" vs peers with similar margins/moats at high-teens-to-20x. Even a bear-case DRAM −30%/yr in 2027-28 leaves DRAM GM ~40%, supporting an earnings (not P/B) multiple; demand visibility lets suppliers spend capex efficiently, lowering the odds of an overbuild-driven crash vs past cycles (GS "Memory", 2026-06-08).
- **Bear — it's still a commodity, peak-margin valuation, share normalizing.** Ex-Micron VP procurement (2026-06-10 expert call): HBM becomes a commodity once 2-3 vendors are qualified; "your $900 stock becomes a $50 stock again" once capacity normalizes — risk inside CY26/early CY27. Jefferies (Conor, 2026-04-07): mid-cycle correction risk, rising data-center concentration. **Share erosion is now in the numbers, not just feared:** UBS models SK hynix HBM share at 56% in 2026 (vs Samsung/Micron 22%/22%), well below the historical ~90% (UBS 2026 overview, Dec-2025). Samsung "believes it has the best-performing HBM4" (1C, internally approved, samples to all customers, mass production/full-scale sales targeted 1H26) and SK hynix itself notes **NVIDIA is encouraging Samsung to ramp** to relieve tightness for Rubin Ultra (Jefferies Techknowledge, 2025-12-17; Samsung targeting NVDA HBM4 qual by 1Q26 — UBS 2026 overview). MS stays **EW on SK hynix "until the HBM margin-erosion debate gets fully priced in"** (MS AI NAND overview). Risk that elevated DRAM/NAND pricing destroys PC/mobile demand (Q1'26 call; Tim Cook WSJ "once-in-100-year flood," briefings 2026-06-18/19).
- **The HBM-vs-EPS paradox (MS, Sean Kim, 2026-04-14):** on the headlines that Hynix may reduce HBM4 volume/share, MS's call is that it **doesn't matter for EPS — "it's better if you don't have HBM" because commodity-DRAM returns are now higher** — but HBM matters for the *multiple* (DRAM trades ~4-5x peak earnings vs HBM ~10x). So lower HBM share is EPS-neutral-to-positive but multiple-negative (MS "Memory", 2026-04-14). NAND is framed as the more interesting industry than DRAM (two AI drivers — KV-cache offload + AI storage; early S-curve penetration; ~6-7yr NAND fab payback vs ~2yr DRAM, so little incentive to add NAND capacity) (same call).
- **Hynix-vs-Samsung flow dynamics (JPM, Jay, 2026-05-28):** JPM's call that Samsung should "catch up" with Hynix post-labor-strike "didn't work" — Hynix's recent outperformance is tied to **single-stock-ETF + passive index buying centered on Hynix**, plus the confirmed US listing/ADR catalyst and improving HBM4 progress; retail is positioned toward Hynix. (Jefferies, 2026-04-07, frames the US-listing valuation kicker as the main Hynix driver — but flags the offsetting "sell Micron, buy Hynix" arbitrage as a reason the PB gap may not be as wide as bulls think.)

- **2026-08-19 · Shareholder-return policy superseded.** The framework carried on this page as **"within 50% of 2025-27 FCF" (Nov-2024 Value-Up plan)** is replaced by **"over 50% of FCF"**, with returns now explicitly weighted to **buybacks & cancellations** (previously the split was unspecified) and **non-operating gains excluded from the FCF base**. Prior state on this page — the 2026-07-28 call at which Hynix declined to announce any new shareholder return, citing "regulatory requirements and procedural restrictions related to the ADR offering" — is superseded by the 08-19 board resolution. **Cumulative cancellation commitment: 15.3mn shares (Feb-2026) + 24.07mn (Aug-2026) = 39.4mn.** The 2024 'Value-Up' plan "may be revisited" (MS · Kim, 2026-08-19).
- **History corroborates the current share lead — SK hynix took HBM3 share from Samsung, and is staying conservative on HBM4 node choice (SemiAnalysis "Scaling the Memory Wall: HBM", 2025-08-11):** SK hynix **took HBM3 share from Samsung** as Samsung's front-end process shortfalls emerged during the Nvidia Hopper era — the origin of SK hynix's current dominant HBM position. For HBM4, SK hynix is **staying on the 1b node** rather than jumping to the riskier 1c node Samsung is attempting; SemiAnalysis describes SK hynix's yields as "progressing smoothly," at the same level as prior generations at the same development phase — a lower-risk, steadier path than Samsung's leapfrog attempt (SemiAnalysis "Scaling the Memory Wall: The Rise and Roadmap of HBM", 2025-08-11).
- **Chris Wood (Jefferies GREED & fear) initiates a new 4% position in three portfolios (2026-06-25).** Christopher Wood adds an initial **4% weighting** in SK Hynix to his global long-only, international long-only, and Asia ex-Japan portfolios simultaneously (selling Alphabet, Alibaba, PolicyBazaar to fund). Thesis identical to Samsung: DRAM structural P/E rerating ("valued on P/E not P/B, and by this measure still look cheap") + Jevons Paradox (cheaper tokens → more compute demand → higher DRAM prices). SK Hynix framed as a "picks and shovels" primary beneficiary of the AI capex arms race. Bull signal (Jefferies / Christopher Wood "GREED & fear: The mother of all cycles", 2026-06-25). - **ADS roadshow — LTA strategy is margin-not-floor, and the structural case for a higher P/E (SK Hynix ADS roadshow, 2026-07-06).** On the ADS roadshow management drew an explicit contrast with Micron: asked whether it would adopt Micron's floor-price LTAs, mgmt said "my main focus is margin… rather than the pricing arrangement," structuring each customer's LTA differently. The re-rating logic laid out directly: the new LTAs run **3-5 years with penalty provisions** (vs the old ~1-yr "gentleman's agreements" that were unenforceable — "you cannot sue basically") → lower demand/supply-shock volatility → a higher P/E deserved; a higher HBM mix (annual contracts vs quarterly/monthly commodity pricing) reinforces the lower-volatility case. **>50% of the 2028-30 capacity expansion will be LTA-covered** (its major LTA customers are >50% of revenue), with **LTA signing expected before year-end 2026**. On capacity, total wafer capacity is guided to **~2x by 2030, but customer demand for 2030 is "a lot bigger" than that potential supply**. Management **pushed back on any committed HBM-to-DDR margin parity** ("to my best knowledge, we haven't committed anything") — the current strong market is only a positive backdrop for next year's HBM price negotiation, framed around preserving core-customer relationships. On China, **CXMT has ~20% of DRAM wafer capacity but only ~6-7% of bits** (low yield → worse quality, usable only for low-end consumer), and China memory impact has been "minimal"; YMTC's expansion has repeatedly under-executed on Western-tool access. **ASIC share of HBM is "going up a little" but the majority of HBM still goes into GPU.** Bull on the durability/re-rating leg (SK Hynix ADS roadshow, 2026-07-06). [../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_06_sk_hynix_roadshow.html](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_06_sk_hynix_roadshow.html)
- **Chairman Chey Tae-won — the strategic case: capacity-doubling, partnership moat, and "memory-as-a-service" (Bloomberg TV — Chairman Chey Tae-won interview, 2026-06-10).** Around the ADR debut (priced **$149/ADR** vs an indicated **$177**, raising **$26.5bn**), SK Group Chairman Chey framed the AI memory cycle as a structural break from the old boom-bust: the LTAs were **proposed by customers, not SK Hynix** ("not SK… they actually require that"), so "it's not cyclical business anymore." SK Hynix has announced it will **double total capacity within five years**, but customers "said that's not enough… they want us to quadruple, five times, six times." Supply tightness lasts **until AI "settles" with human society** (no fixed date); the demand cycle is now driven by agentic-AI KV-cache, not device-unit counts. On the "AI bubble": **the technology is real (no bubble); only the stock market may overshoot**. Two durable moats: SK Hynix **works with TSMC on the HBM base die "which the others do not do,"** and **"doesn't compete with any customers"** — both enabling deeper partnerships. Also: a new **US fab in Indiana**; **SK Group ~$1T AI-datacenter investment over 10 years (~15GW in Korea + ~5GW outside Korea)**, with **~$35B already invested in the US** (bio, battery, Hynix, SKT AI startups); and a longer-term **"memory-as-a-service"** concept (memory delivered as a managed solution rather than just product). Bull/strategic framing (Bloomberg TV — Chairman Chey Tae-won interview, 2026-06-10). - **Price up, content down — the 2027 HBM-pricing call and the offsetting de-spec, sourced from the buyer's own BOM (SemiAnalysis "Can AMD break the CUDA Moat? AMD Advancing AI 2026", 2026-07-24).** The bull leg is unusually well-placed because it comes from the *accelerator vendor's* bill of materials rather than a memory maker: SemiAnalysis explicitly expects "**a significant increase in HBM pricing in 2027 as memory suppliers move to match the margins they now earn on commodity DRAM**" — commodity-DRAM margin is now the *anchor* HBM is being repriced toward, which is the same inversion this page tracks from the other side (Redburn: HBM 2027 revised down 60→50bn Gb-eq because conventional DRAM is more profitable; roadshow 07-06: management explicitly **refused to commit** to HBM-to-DDR margin parity). And it is modelled as strong enough to **compress the accelerator vendors themselves**: AMD's ASPs must rise but it cannot pass the full memory increase through, because "most of AMD's volume goes to large buyers with real negotiating leverage, and those buyers have more alternatives than they used to," so HBM cost passes through "**at a lower incremental margin than their corporate margins, dragging down gross margins on the MI455X package.**" That is a third-party estimate of HBM pricing power strong enough to move a customer's gross margin — the most direct external support yet for the page's "next year's HBM price negotiation" framing. Baseline: "**HBM, DRAM, and NAND prices alike have increased dramatically since our baseline in mid-2025**" (SemiAnalysis Memory Model / Accelerator and HBM Model). **The offsetting bear leg is in the same note and is the reason price × prior content is the wrong revenue model.** Tight supply is already destroying attach: the original 2025 MI455X BOM estimate carried "a staggering **138TB in DRAM across LPDDR5X and DDR5 MRDIMM**"; the current estimate has **LPDDR5X entirely removed** plus a **significant DDR5 de-spec to a far lower DDR5 RDIMM content** — specifically the roadmapped **up to 1TB of direct-attached LPDDR per MI455X accelerator on the EAM module has disappeared**, attributed directly to tight memory supply. SemiAnalysis's summary is the line to carry: "**much of the DRAM price increases are offset by aggressive de-specs.**" Net for SK hynix: **price-led revenue with content as a live offset** — bullish on ASP durability into 2027, but the bits-per-accelerator assumption underneath consensus volume is now the exposed variable. (SemiAnalysis "Can AMD break the CUDA Moat? AMD Advancing AI 2026", 2026-07-24) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_24_ENC_Can_AMD_break_the_CUDA_Moat_AMD_Advancing_AI_2026_email.html)
