It is NOT re-logged here.**
  - **HBF, dismantled from the SK hynix researchers' own paper — the most rigorous HBF scepticism on this page, and it comes from a source that is otherwise structurally bullish memory** (**DAMNANG (Substack)** — “HBF's Limits and Reality: Between Promise and Structural Obstacles (H³ Paper Review)”, **2026-03-18**). The promise being tested: **stack NAND with TSVs like HBM for ~16x HBM capacity (~3TB) at equivalent ~8TB/s bandwidth, at ~1/5 the cost of HBM**, SK hynix and SanDisk targeting **sampling in late 2026**. The H³ architecture in the paper is explicitly HYBRID (HBM + HBF), because the author's core objection is physical and unarguable: **NAND carries a 1-2 ORDER-OF-MAGNITUDE latency disadvantage that cannot be architected away**, and the paper's mitigation — **a 40MB SRAM buffer plus tens of GB of DRAM plus a complex FTL controller** — *“makes the total system anything but cheap.”* Second objection: **the paper assumes pure READ-ONLY workloads and deterministic access patterns**, which he calls *“extremely limited in actual production.”* Open items he lists: production yield, thermal management in a GPU environment, NAND read-disturb/endurance under those conditions. **His conclusion is a COMPLEMENT, not a substitute: “a niche solution effective in highly specialized CAG workloads… a complement targeting ‘the gap between HBM and SSD,’ NOT an HBM replacement.”** ➤ **Independently corroborated five months later from the commercial side by Micron's own management (Mizuho, 2026-08-09): HBF substituting HBM “unlikely… but will see adoption in the memory stack.” Two sources, one physical and one commercial, same destination — which is the reason to price HBF as a tier, not as an option on HBM's TAM.** The strategic framing is worth keeping regardless of the product: *“SK hynix dominates DRAM and HBM, while SanDisk dominates NAND. Their collaboration is… the memory industry's effort to transition from COMMODITY business to PLATFORM business.”* [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026-03-19_-_HBFs_Limits_and_Reality-_Between_Promise_and_Structural_Obstacles_H%C2%B3_Paper_Review.html)
  - **“Memory is no longer a cyclical industry” — the framing this page's structural leg rests on, sourced to an SK hynix executive** (**DAMNANG (Substack)** — “Memory is no longer a cyclical industry”, **2026-03-25**, written up from a ~20-minute seminar by **SK hynix's North America regional president** at the Silicon Valley K-ASIC Seminar on 2026-03-24, plus a 1-on-1 afterwards). The argument: the classic glut→price-collapse→investment-cut→shortage loop no longer runs because **all three players “paid for it” in the last cycle and have shifted priority from volume share to “profitability, capital efficiency, and customer mix”**; with HBM, allocation decisions are margin decisions, so **LTAs are “a signal that memory companies are managing supply far more STRATEGICALLY than before”** rather than merely locking volume. ⚠️ **THE AUTHOR'S OWN DISCLOSURE, CARRIED: “given that the underlying perspective comes from someone inside the memory industry, the content may lean BULLISH on memory.”** ⚠️ **AND ONE PASSAGE IS EXPLICITLY LABELLED SPECULATION BY THE AUTHOR AND MUST NOT BE READ AS COMPANY GUIDANCE: asked about an ADR, the executive deflected in public and, privately, answered with a joke — “just buy Micron.” The author then builds an unsupported thesis that [[MU]] has carried a structural premium as the only large-cap memory name accessible to US investors and that an SK hynix ADR would erode it. He calls this “entertainment, not signal.” RECORDED AS SUCH.** ➤ Cross-read to the 08-25 UBS line above, which is the company's first on-the-record ADR framing: regulatory approval to work through, liquidity to build over time — consistent with “not near-term.” [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026-03-25_-_Memory_is_no_longer_a_cyclical_industry.html)
  - **The captive-ratio framework — the cleanest statement of WHY this cycle's margins might hold, and of what would break them** (**DAMNANG (Substack)** — “Will the Memory Stock Rally Keep Going?”, **2026-05-11**). Opening marks (historical): **SK hynix posted a 72% operating margin in a single quarter; [[MU]] was locking 3-to-5-year supply agreements; [[SAMSUNG]] had a quarter where conventional-DRAM profitability EXCEEDED HBM.** Demand backdrop cited: **Big Tech 4 (Google/Microsoft/Meta/Amazon) 2026 capex ~$725bn, +~77% y/y**; **[[NVDA]] ~$68bn quarterly revenue guiding ~$78bn**. The argument: *“what matters in this memory cycle is not peak margin itself. It is HOW MANY QUARTERS this margin can hold. In the past, supply gluts answered that question. This time, THE CAPTIVE RATIO answers it — and the tools to lower the captive ratio are still unproven.”* His three-item quarterly dashboard is a genuinely usable checklist for this page: **(1) the pace at which platform owners deploy ‘bypass’ tools in production; (2) memory-vendor progress converting cHBM / HBF / PIM into revenue; (3) whether the MEMORY SHARE WITHIN hyperscaler capex keeps rising.** ➤ Read against 2026-08: item (1) is exactly what the de-spec debate is measuring, and item (2) is what the CMM-Ax/custom-base-die work above is. [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026-05-12_-_Will_the_Memory_Stock_Rally_Keep_Going-.html)
  - **CXMT — the SK-hynix-specific conclusion, and the double-edged sword inside it** (**DAMNANG (Substack)** — “Is CXMT a Threat or an Illusion?”, **2026-04-11**; the author cites separate conversations with **SK hynix's Americas head** and an **ASML employee**). *“SK hynix has the STRONGEST DEFENSIVE POSITION for now, thanks to HBM”* — even if CXMT produces HBM3, SK hynix competes at HBM4 with TSMC base-die collaboration locked in, and its commodity book has been restructured toward higher-value product, so CXMT's commodity expansion hits [[SAMSUNG]] harder. 🔴 **THE WARNING IS THE PART TO KEEP: “the variable is the 3D DRAM transition… THE STRONGER THE HBM MOAT, THE LOWER THE PERCEIVED URGENCY TO INVEST IN THE 3D DRAM TRANSITION, and that could become a double-edged sword over the long run” — with the Americas head himself described as taking the speed of China's 3D DRAM pursuit seriously.** Scale context: **CXMT went from ~40kwpm in 2020 to ~720,000 wafers per QUARTER by end-2025 across three 12-inch fabs, reaching a ~16nm G4 node (TechInsights) on DUV multi-patterning with NO EUV** — a level the Big 3 reached in 2018-19. ➤ **This is a DIFFERENT piece from the 2026-07-27 “After CXMT's Listing” note already on this page and it argues a different thing: that one tests the price-war claims, this one identifies the technology transition where the rules could change. Both retained.** [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026-04-11_-_Is_CXMT_a_Threat_or_an_Illusion-.html)
  - **Hybrid bonding — SK hynix is the CONSERVATIVE one, and that is a position with a shelf life** (**DAMNANG (Substack)** — “Hybrid Bonding, Fully Dissected”, **2026-03-28**, based on the author's interviews with engineers at SK hynix, Samsung and Micron). **“SK Hynix appears to be the MOST CONSERVATIVE of the three”: 12-layer HBM4 development and MP-readiness completed on its existing MR-MUF approach, 16-Hi HBM4 showcased, hybrid bonding targeted at HBM4E.** Against that, **“Samsung is the most aggressive,” signalling adoption at 16-layer HBM4E with SEMES having produced working 16-layer hybrid-bonded HBM samples — the author's read being that hybrid bonding is “a RESET OF THE GAME BOARD” for a company that could not catch SK hynix on TC/NCF bonding. [[MU]] has disclosed no timeline and is “most likely to be last.”** Timing mechanics: **JEDEC relaxing HBM4 package thickness from 720μm to 775μm let 16-layer HBM4 be built with conventional TC bonding and pushed hybrid bonding out one generation; from HBM4E at 20 layers, TC bonding's “economics and technical feasibility deteriorate sharply.”** ⚠️ **The engineers' own caveat, carried: they target HBM4E for initial adoption but “given the cost challenges, it may realistically not happen at scale until HBM5” — while also saying “technically, they can no longer afford to delay.”** ➤ **The relevance in August 2026 is that Mizuho/SemiAnalysis now call 16-20Hi HBM AND hybrid bonding “uncertain for future gens” — which, if right, EXTENDS the life of SK hynix's MR-MUF advantage rather than shortening it. That is the under-discussed second-order read of the de-spec debate for this name.** (From the same author's “Why HBM Is So Hard to Make”, **2026-03-20**: the current split is **SK hynix on MR-MUF — best thermal performance, most production-proven — vs Samsung on NCF + thermocompression, better precision at fine pitch**; HBM4 at 16 layers pushes bump pitch to ~16-18μm from ~25μm, with **no bump-to-bump redundancy in the HBM PHY signal path**.) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026-03-29_-_Hybrid_Bonding_Fully_Dissected.html)
  - **Two smaller archive marks.** (i) **The base-die asymmetry, stated plainly from the Samsung side** (**DAMNANG (Substack)** — “How Samsung Overtakes SK hynix in HBM4 — An ISSCC 2026 Paper Review”, **2026-03-20**): Samsung's HBM4 moves the base die to its own **4nm FinFET logic** process while jumping the core die 1a→1c, and the author's structural point is that this *“only makes sense if you have memory and foundry under the same roof — SK Hynix would need to SOURCE THAT LOGIC PROCESS EXTERNALLY (e.g. from TSMC)”*, with the offsetting note that **“SK Hynix will almost certainly pursue its own logic base die strategy through its partnership with TSMC.”** ⚠️ Historical; the TSMC base-die collaboration is already established on this page from better sources. (ii) **SOCAMM2 and the NAND leg** (**DAMNANG (Substack)** — “Vera Rubin's DRAM Cut in Half”, **2026-06-08** — ⚠️ **the ingest router mislabelled this piece “SemiAnalysis”; it is DAMNANG, discussing a SemiAnalysis report**): **SK hynix announced 192GB SOCAMM2 mass production** (as did Micron), which is the author's reason for treating the reported 96GB Vera Rubin standard build as *“a supply chain account, not an official confirmation”*; and **SK hynix's own 1Q results said AI is growing NAND demand ALONGSIDE DRAM, not just DRAM.** Framework worth carrying: he splits inference state into **CAPTIVE (must stay in HBM), ABSORBED (LPDDR/SOCAMM), and LEAKED (storage)** — the same taxonomy the 2026-08 de-spec debate is implicitly arguing over. [Sources](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026-03-20_-_How_Samsung_Overtakes_SK_hynix_in_HBM4-_An_ISSCC_2026_Paper_Review.html) · [Vera Rubin](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026-06-08_-_Vera_Rubins_DRAM_Cut_in_Half-_Is_the_Memory_Cycle_Really_Over-.html)
  - **NAND layer-count standing, as of March 2026 — historical, kept only as the baseline the Dalian 240L upgrade is measured against** (**DAMNANG (Substack)** — “Is SanDisk Done?”, **2026-03-26**): **SK hynix in mass production at 321-layer QLC — the highest layer count of the group** (Samsung V9 ~290L-class TLC; [[MU]] 276L G9 QLC; [[SNDK]]/[[KIOXIA]] BiCS8 at 218-232L). ⚠️ **The author's caveat cuts against SK hynix and is carried: “SK hynix at 321 layers is APPROACHING THE LIMITS of how much additional efficiency it can extract, while SanDisk/Kioxia still have steps left to climb.”** Also in that piece and relevant here: **Solidigm (SK hynix subsidiary) took a $500M / 2.0% stake in Nanya Technology at NT$223.9 alongside SanDisk ($1B / 3.9%), Kioxia ($500M / 2%) and Cisco ($500M / 2%) in a single ~$2.5bn placement, each SSD maker signing a separate multi-year DRAM supply agreement** — three SSD makers locking DRAM sourcing simultaneously, which the author reads as the industry conceding a **DRAM (FTL cache) bottleneck** in enterprise SSD. [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026-03-26_-_Is_SanDisk_Done-.html)


## Catalysts / what to watch
- **🆕🔴🔴 THE "SHORTAGE THROUGH END-2030" CLAIM IS NOW THE CENTRAL FALSIFIABLE THESIS ON THIS PAGE, AND JPM NAMES THE TEST: LTA PRICING DISCLOSURES.** The CEO's position is that oversupply risk is low *"since memory chips are no longer 'just commodities' in the AI era"* (@dnystedt, 2026-08-27). JPM's four catalysts for the sector are **(a) shareholder-return policy update at the 3Q26 earnings conference, Oct-end; (b) CSP capex update; (c) LTA pricing disclosures; (d) China competition update.** ➜ **(c) is the one that adjudicates the commoditisation argument: if LTA pricing holds as supply loosens in 2028, the CEO is right and TrendForce's late-2027 oversupply call is a commodity-framework error. If LTAs re-price down, he is not.**
- **🆕 3Q26 EARNINGS CONFERENCE, OCT-END — shareholder-return policy update expected, and JPM flags it as the sector's first named catalyst.** *"We expect to hear more on a transparent shareholder return policy update from both US/Asian memory makers within the next 3-4M."*
- **🆕🔴 THE INTEL HBM4E BASE-DIE EVALUATION — the highest-consequence unconfirmed item on this page.** *"SK hynix Reportedly Weighs Intel for HBM4E Base Dies amid TSMC Cost Pressure and Supply Diversification"* (UBS TMI, 2026-08-31). **Watch for: any confirmation, the generation it would apply to, and whether [[TSM]] responds — TSMC publicly claimed HBM base-die silicon from N12 (HBM4) to N3 (HBM5) at SEMICON Taiwan the same week. Cross-filed [[INTC]], [[TSM]].**
- **🆕 THE JAPAN GREENFIELD JV — a chairman's stated EVALUATION, nothing more.** *"SK Group's chairman Mr. Chey mentioned evaluation of a JV in Japan for a new greenfield fab where power and water supply are sufficient"* (JPM, 2026-08-31). **JPM's base case is that the majority of production stays in Korea (Yongin cluster 1 at 390-420K wfpm by 2030, three further Yongin clusters after it) and that any ex-Korea capacity is non-leading-edge — packaging or NAND, on the Solidigm/Dalian template. Watch for a named partner or site before treating it as capacity.**
- **🆕⚠️ THE NAND NODE-TRANSITION LAG IS A CAPEX OBLIGATION, NOT JUST A MARGIN GAP.** *"176-layer NAND still accounted for more than half of SK hynix's production [in 2Q26], while Samsung had already completed the transition… to 236-layer"* (@jukan05, 2026-08-27). ➜ **Read against the BofA 08-27 tour line that SK hynix has *"no interest in conventional DRAM/NAND capacity expansion"* and against the M17 Cheongju NAND fab base logged 08-28: migration capex is required regardless of appetite for net-new bits. Watch the 3Q26 capex split between migration and greenfield.**

- **🆕🔴 3Q26 EARNINGS — management "suggested further update" on the shareholder-return pool THEN.** The specific commitment to be tested: lifting the pool **from <50% to over 50% of accumulated FCF** (JPM · Kwon, 2026-08-23). Note [[SAMSUNG]] deferred its own equivalent decision to **January 2027**, so Hynix's 3Q26 print is the earlier of the two Korean capital-return events.
- **🆕 BY END-SEPTEMBER 2026 — HBM contract price update** (the industry-wide catalyst JPM names first for the complex); **2027E HBM ASP negotiations are already "at the later part of the process."** (JPM · Kwon, 2026-08-23)
- **🆕 2026-08-26, 09:00 HKT — Nanya Tech President call (BofA-hosted).** Nanya is the cleanest independent read on the CXMT question — at the JPM tour it argued **CXMT "has now been present in the market for well over a decade without materially disrupting global S/D, in large part because AI has been absorbing the very supply that would otherwise serve consumer end-markets."** (BofA · Woo, 2026-08-23; JPM · Kwon, 2026-08-23)


- **🆕🔴 By 2026-09-30 — HBM CONTRACT PRICE UPDATE.** Named by JPM (Kwon, 2026-08-19) as the #2 company-specific catalyst. **This is the one that moves earnings rather than the share count**, and it is the live test of the "HBM repricing in 2027" narrative the 2Q26 call half-confirmed.
- **🆕 Next month (~Sep 2026) — US SUBSIDIARY LISTING PLAN UPDATE** (JPM · Kwon, 2026-08-19).
- **🆕 By end-Oct 2026 — 3Q26 earnings call: the SECOND tranche of shareholder return.** The company said explicitly that it intends to pursue **additional** returns **within the current program period**, with scale/timing announced after board approval at the 3Q26 release, and that it is reviewing **both regular and special dividend** increases. MS: "this is not the end of the program."
- **🆕 2026-08-20 → 2026-11-19 — the buyback executes on-market** (SK Securities). Daily execution is observable; **the falsifier for the "most aggressive among peers" claim is whether the full W40trn actually gets bought and retired inside the window, and whether any of it is diverted to ADR float rather than cancelled** (management said float could expand without new-share issuance).
- **Q2 2026 earnings:** late July 2026 (English call; prior Q2 was 2025-07-24) — HBM4 ramp progress, DRAM ASP, capex number.
- **HBM4 / NVDA Rubin ramp** through 2026; HBM4 blended ≤50% of mix in 2026, 2H26 crossover (Jefferies Techknowledge, 2025-12-17); whether SK hynix holds ~56% HBM share (UBS 2026 overview) vs Samsung's pursued NVDA slot.
- **Samsung/Micron HBM4 qualification at NVDA** — Samsung targeting NVDA HBM4 qual by 1Q26 (UBS 2026 overview); the single biggest share-erosion risk, with NVDA itself nudging Samsung to add capacity (Jefferies Techknowledge, 2025-12-17).
- **Rubin (R200-class) HBM supply-share split** — whether SK hynix holds SemiAnalysis's modeled ~60% of first-12-months Rubin HBM supply (vs Samsung ~30%, Micron ~0%); confirmation would validate the current HBM3/HBM3E-execution lead carrying into HBM4 (SemiAnalysis "Memory Mania", 2026-02-06).
- **2026 capex disclosure & funding** — M15X (Cheongju) ramp delayed by Korea labour-law/industrial conflicts (fab structure targeted mid-2026, fab start ~later; Daifuku via Jefferies Techknowledge, 2025-12-17); first Yongin cluster fab delayed to next year; Indiana advanced-packaging expansion; dollar HBM capex rising sharply (greenfield + hybrid bonding) — SK hynix is "open to customer deposits" to help fund it. **Roadshow use-of-proceeds detail (SK Hynix ADS roadshow, 2026-07-06):** the ~$26.5bn ADR proceeds fund **Cheongju (₩19tn, plant PNT7) + Yongin (₩21tn) + EUV scanners**; net cash since Q3'25, management **targets ₩100tn cash by year-end 2026** ("easily") and, having hit that position earlier than planned, will **announce a shareholder-return measure on the Q3 conference call** (executable this year, vs the original 3-yr plan). New **M17 (Cheongju) NAND capacity contributes from 2029** (the reason NAND expansion in the roadshow deck footnotes read as ~2030 — decided/disclosed after the deck was produced). **Yongin Y1 acceleration (@jukan05, 2026-07-14):** SK hynix has begun placing equipment orders for Yongin Y1 and **pulled the Phase-1 pilot ("one-pass") line forward to Feb-2027 (from May-2027)**, with full install toward ~20K wpm following in Mar/Apr on **1c DRAM** (the most advanced node in commercial production, also feeding HBM4E, which could commercialize as early as next year); it also **moved equipment price-negotiations forward a quarter (to 3Q)** to speed installs. Y1 Phases 2&3 cleanroom construction is set to start 2H-2026; the Yongin cluster (KRW600tn / 4 fabs) has its 4th fab pulled forward 12yrs to 2033 — concrete execution on the capacity pull-forward.
- **ADR/local arbitrage "malfunctioning" until July 29 (@jukan05 / Korea Securities Depository, 2026-07-14):** two-way conversion between SK Hynix's Korean-listed common shares and the Nasdaq-listed SKHY line **only becomes possible after July 29**, when the newly issued Korean common shares backing the ADR offering list domestically. Until then the fungibility mechanism is inoperative — a technical explanation for the ADR-vs-local premium dislocation in the interim window.
- **Korea national AI mega-plan follow-through** — Samsung + SK Group's **KRW4,755tn (~US$3.1tn)** long-term capex plan through 2040 (JPM · Jay Kwon, 2026-06-30, revised up from the $880B/$1.3T framing reported just 48hrs earlier); watch for confirmation of the implied doubling of installed Korean DRAM capacity within 5 years and the 7-12yr Yongin pull-forward, plus whether the 60-70% front-end-wafer / 20-30% infra-cleanroom capex split (JPM, 2026-06-30) holds as disclosures firm up. Near-term signal to watch: whether the "hyperscaler curse" disconnect (capex-beneficiary basket +5% while memory names sell off — Rothschild, 2026-07-01) resolves toward the memory names as NPS rebalancing/DRAM-export-data noise fades.
- **US Nasdaq listing — expected Friday 2026-07-10** (fresh capital raise on top of the KRX:000660 line); investor reception is a read on both the IPO-market strength and the US-investor bid for the name (only MU among the big-3 is US-listed today). Firms up the "US listing/ADR" re-rating catalyst previously flagged by JPM (05-28) / Barclays (05-03) with a concrete date. On the valuation the listing highlights: 3.6x fwd sales vs MU 4.6x / NVDA 10.8x, P/B discount to MU (The Information, 2026-07-05). **Ticker confirmed SKHY** and the listing **raises capital** as part of the offering; rationale is to make the stock easier for US investors to buy; context cited: Hynix revenue **+200% between 2023 and 2025, then +200% again in Q1 2026** (The Information, 2026-07-05; multiple bookrunner IPO notices, 2026-07-06). **Size now put at ~US$29bn — potentially the largest-ever US IPO by a foreign issuer**, with proceeds "largely earmarked for expanding manufacturing capacity in Korea and securing additional EUV lithography machines from ASML" — "not financial engineering… a direct bet that the AI memory shortage is far from over" (Jefferies · Cristina Titu, 2026-07-07). Trading setup: several desks recommend **buy the ADR / sell the Seoul-listed shares** (ADR expected to command a premium + a cleaner route for global HFs), and Korean authorities are reportedly preparing for significant FX flows ahead of settlement. Valuation framing: Hynix trades ~**7x this year's / 5x next year's earnings** vs AI stories at 30-80x sales — "extraordinarily difficult to describe as anything other than cheap" as long as AI capex is alive; the biggest AI winners so far have been the "picks-and-shovels" suppliers, "and among those nobody is better positioned than SK Hynix" (Jefferies · Cristina Titu, 2026-07-07). **Deal size marked at ~US$28.2bn** (~17.79M new shares / ~2.5% of TSO; 1 ADR = 0.1 common share) by Morgan Stanley (S&T) on the 07-08 marketing detail — consistent with Jefferies' ~US$29bn (rounding/pricing-window difference, not a supersession); see the dedicated ADR-mechanics bullet below for premium/flow/index detail.
- **US Nasdaq ADR listing — mechanics & premium/flow view (Morgan Stanley (S&T) "SK Hynix ADR Listing", 2026-07-08).** MS Equity S&T (Pankaj Mataney, Asia Platform Sales & QDS — *note: S&T commentary, not MS Research, no rating*) details the deal: SK Hynix began marketing the proposed **Nasdaq ADR (ticker SKHY)** on **2026-07-06**; offering ~**US$28.2bn** via ADRs representing ~**17.79M new common shares (~2.5% of TSO)**; **1 ADR = 0.1 common share (10 ADRs = 1 ordinary)**; bookbuild Jul 6–9, pricing Jul 9, **ADRs begin trading 2026-07-10**, subscription-proceeds payment ~Jul 14, new underlying shares list on KRX Jul 29. Proceeds fund domestic expansion (Yongin, Cheongju packaging, advanced equipment). **Liquidity:** MS estimates the ADR line at **~10-20% of aggregate turnover** near-term (foreigners ~30% of local turnover × ~50% US-based); plus leveraged ETFs **SKHU/SKUU (2x long), SKDD (-2x)** expected ~Jul 13 adding dealer-hedging/rebalancing flow (Asia-listed SK Hynix levered ETFs, ~$18.7bn AUM, already run ~18% of cash turnover). Most Korean ADRs stay <10% of turnover (ex-KT/SK Telecom at 31-61% on prior FOL breaches). **Premium:** MS expects the SKHY ADR to trade at a **~5-10% premium** to the local line (higher in event-driven windows like index/ETF inclusion) — between typical Korean ADRs (parity to low-single-digit) and TSMC ADR (double-digit; ~18% as of Jul 6 vs 13.3% 5-yr avg). Driver = limited ~2.5% issuance headroom + supportive narrative; caveat: headroom alone doesn't guarantee a premium (TSMC traded near parity in stretches), and weaker memory fundamentals/sentiment could compress it toward parity. **Fungibility is one-way:** ADR→local seamless (from Jul 29); local→ADR constrained by the ~2.5% headroom (expected fully used at launch), so new ADRs need existing holders to cancel/convert back. Further primary issuance is limited near-term — **SK Square must hold ≥20% of SK Hynix (currently ~20.5%)**, capping dilution; any expansion likely via secondary/buyback structures or onshore approval. MS also flags **KSD headroom (not SEC Form F-6, which shows max program size of 1.78bn ADS / 25% TSO) as the right benchmark** — some investors are mis-sizing US index-inclusion market cap off the F-6 figure. **Index events:** MSCI ~**$1.9bn** inflow (Nov-2026 review, local line); FTSE ~**$1.28bn** (Sep-2026, local line); **KOSPI 200 ~$955mn** (ad-hoc, effective ~Jul 29); **VanEck SMH ~$3.8bn** (earliest Sep-2026 if treated as an index IPO, else Sep-2027); **SOX ~$114mn** (earliest Sep-2027); **Nasdaq-100 near-term inclusion unlikely** (eligibility on full ADR market cap ~$30bn vs the ~$200bn fast-entry / ~$70bn top-75 thresholds). Options expected ~2 trading days after listing; SEC Rule 105 / Reg M short-sale constraints may reach the local line given fungibility (Morgan Stanley (S&T) "SK Hynix ADR Listing: Key Investor Questions and Our Views", Pankaj Mataney, 2026-07-08). [../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/MS_-_SK_Hynix_ADR_Listing_Key_Investor_Questions_and_Our_Views_1.html](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/MS_-_SK_Hynix_ADR_Listing_Key_Investor_Questions_and_Our_Views_1.html)
- **SKHY book heavily oversubscribed** — demand for the ~$28bn US share sale was **more than 7x available shares** (Reuters, 2026-07-09), a strong read on the US-investor bid ahead of the 2026-07-10 trading debut. (bull)
- **✅ RESOLVED — Nasdaq ADR debut (2026-07-10): priced at $149, raised ~$26.5bn.** SK Hynix priced its ADRs at **$149**, raising **~US$26.5bn** (regulatory filing) — the largest-ever first-time share sale by a foreign company on US markets, **pushing aside Alibaba** for the record (Bloomberg @business; Reuters, 2026-07-10). The book was **7x oversubscribed with 500+ investment firms**, and even key/anchor investors (incl. Leo/Philippe allocations) had **final allocations cut on the excess demand** (FT via @firstadopter, 2026-07-10). ADRs priced at a **~3% premium** to the local line (below the ~5-10% MS S&T range and the 15-20% some desks were comfortable with — @zerohedge, 2026-07-10; Barron's "set to trade at a premium"). Realized raise (~$26.5bn) came in a touch below the ~$28.2-29bn marketing size (MS S&T 07-08 / Jefferies 07-07). Korean-listed line still trades **~4.5x forward earnings** (FT via @firstadopter). ETF issuers have **≥6 products ready to launch next week** (incl. leveraged SKHU/SKUU/SKDD) (Bloomberg @business, 2026-07-10). Cramer: SK Hynix analysts likely to raise numbers Monday to "keep this tight" (@jimcramer, 2026-07-10). (bull — the US-listing/ADR re-rating catalyst is now realized with record-setting demand)
- **Intel Ohio fab — US-manufacturing option (@jukan05 / Korean media, 2026-07-21; SK Hynix official, 2026-07-22):** Korean media reported SK Hynix in talks / a "bold move" to acquire Intel's Ohio fab for US memory production. Both parties denied a sale/acquisition (07-21), without denying a possible JV; SK Hynix's official statement (07-22) says it "has neither pursued nor decided" on the Ohio plant but "continuously reviews various investment/acquisition opportunities." Watch item on US-footprint pressure — likely rumor for now, but a live channel for the US-localization push (cross-ref the Indiana packaging build).
- **HBF (High Bandwidth Flash)** — SK hynix signed an MOU with SanDisk (Aug-2025) to standardize HBF for AI inference (NAND augmenting HBM); first SanDisk HBF samples 2H26 (MS AI NAND overview).
- 🆕 **HBF + TWO MORE TIERS — SK HYNIX PUT A THREE-TIER PROPOSAL ON A SLIDE AT FMS, AND HBF IS ONLY ONE OF THEM (FUNDA, "Takeaways from the Future of Memory and Storage Conference 2026", 2026-08-11).** *"SK Hynix proposed **3D stacked DRAM, HBF, and CXL Pooled Memory** as three new tiers of memory."* ➜ **This is the first time this page can say that SK hynix is attacking the hierarchy at three levels rather than defending HBM plus one flash option. Two of the three are NOT HBM: HBF is NAND-based and CXL Pooled Memory is commodity-DRAM-based — i.e. the company is positioning to monetise the tiers that exist BECAUSE HBM is too expensive, which is the tiering logic already on this page from the former-SK-Hynix-executive expert call ("because of high cost, so they rather use TIERED MEMORY"). Watch which of the three gets a product date first; only HBF has one (2027 samples). Cross-ref [themes/cxl-memory-fabric](themes/cxl-memory-fabric.md), [themes/hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md).**
- 🆕 **HBF customer confirmation and its conspicuous omission, same source:** *"The Sandisk / SK Hynix announcement **confirmed [[GOOG]]'s interest in HBF**. Investors noted **[[NVDA]]'s absence from the HBF presentations and panels**, which suggests **Nvidia prefers an architecture built on high-IOPS SSDs**."* ➜ **The Google half corroborates the 08-04 consortium row below (Google + Tenstorrent as key ecosystem partners). The Nvidia half is the harder datapoint and it belongs with @bubbleboi's "who is the actual customer" objection already logged here: NVDA is not a consortium member, its own named storage paths are SCADA (GPU-direct-to-eSSD) and CMX (GPU→DPU→eSSD), and it sent nobody to the HBF panels. So the HBF demand anchor is the ASIC/TPU camp, not the largest accelerator buyer on the tape.**
- 🆕 **And the supply mechanic, which is the leg that pays SK hynix even if it never sells an HBF stack:** *"If HBF ramps, it may **consume NAND wafers at premium pricing the same way HBM did inside DRAM**. Every wafer diverted to HBF then **tightens commodity flash supply**"* (FUNDA, 2026-08-11). ➜ **Stated three days BEFORE Bernstein quantified the same mechanic at 3-4x wafer capacity per exabyte (08-14 row below). For a supplier that is simultaneously expanding China NAND output ~50%, an industry-wide NAND wafer sink is a tailwind to the part of the book this page has been most worried about.**
- **MU print** as the cleanest near-term memory demand/pricing tell; UBS sees MU DDR GM surpassing HBM GM for the first time in early CY26 on commodity-DRAM tightness (UBS 2026 overview).
- **Consumer demand elasticity** — PC/mobile cuts (NVDA SOCAMM2 cut, AAPL prepayment/balance-sheet talk; briefings 2026-06-18/19).

## Risks
- **Commodity cyclicality / ASP reversal** — peak ~72% OP margin is not a through-cycle level; capacity additions (own + China CXMT/YMTC) normalize pricing. CXMT aggressively expanding HBM (HBM2 8-high mass production from 1H25, TSV capacity nearing Micron's by YE); Huawei building its own HBM supply chain (SemiAnalysis HBM).
  - ⚠️ **COUNTER-EVIDENCE ON THE CXMT LEG OF THIS RISK, logged 2026-08-21 from an independent source that TESTS the claims rather than asserting them** (DAMNANG (Substack), "After CXMT's Listing, Do the Memory Three Have Further to Fall?", **2026-07-27** — ⚠️ **NOT a broker; ~25 days stale, its price marks are historical, the framework is the value**). The three load-bearing planks of the CXMT price-war bear all weaken on inspection: **(i) the "sells 30% cheaper" figure is a company-average artefact — two thirds of CXMT revenue is low-price mobile; like-for-like the prospectus gap is 5-10%, and in SERVER product the sign FLIPS (a 64GB DDR5 module on CXMT chips at RMB18,999 vs RMB18,595 for the same spec on Samsung/SK hynix chips, July 2026 = CXMT 2.2% ABOVE)**; **(ii) it cannot undercut — >30% higher cost for the same bits, ~40% larger die, yield below the 85-90% industry standard**; **(iii) the "8% share" is REVENUE (7.7% of 1Q26 world DRAM revenue on US$7.31bn) while 13% of wafer starts convert to just 6% of capacity, and its 1Q26 +71% y/y was ASP +57% on volume +11%.** On HBM the direction actually INVERTS: **<2% of wafer starts, ~25% 8-high yield vs 70-90% incumbent, and stacking consumes ~3x the wafer area per GB — so CXMT moving into HBM pushes ordinary DRAM prices UP, not down.** IPO-funded supply lands **2028 at the earliest**. ➡ **The SK-hynix-specific conclusion is the one to hold: earnings concentrated in HBM, a segment "CXMT cannot enter," make this the MOST INSULATED of the three — and "what moves SK hynix shares is [the capex plans of a handful of AI-accelerator customers], not CXMT."** ⚠️ **This does NOT retire the risk line above: the 2028+ capacity build, the CXMT HBM-localisation vector, the DRAM price-fixing litigation and the China-substitution legs already logged on this page are untouched. It re-weights the NEAR-TERM CXMT price-war fear specifically — and note it agrees with the two sell-side CXMT initiations already on the page (UBS 08-07: "LIMITED price discounts for CXMT's DRAM vs global peers"; Nomura 07-26 bullish on CXMT while still forecasting demand outrunning all-maker supply). Full detail in the archived 07-27 row: [## Changelog](#changelog).**
- **HBM share loss / customer concentration** — UBS 2026 HBM share of 56% (vs ~90% historically); NVDA encouraging Samsung to ramp; Samsung's HBM4 self-described as best-in-class (Jefferies Techknowledge, 2025-12-17; UBS 2026 overview).
- **HBM tool dependency** — concentrated TC-bonding reliance on Hanmi for HBM3E 12-high; ASMPT/Besi/Hanwha not yet fully qualified (SemiAnalysis HBM). Concrete case study: Hanmi held ~100% share of SK hynix's TC bonders until fall-2024 diversification into Hanwha; Hanmi then pulled its field-service teams from SK hynix's fabs (early April 2025), the Hanwha tools went undelivered, and the ASMPT bonders ordered fall-2024 didn't work for HBM3E 12-high — forcing SK hynix to place a placating order back with Hanmi to keep packaging output flowing (SemiAnalysis "Scaling the Memory Wall: HBM", 2025-08-11).
- **Demand destruction** — elevated DRAM/NAND prices crimp PC/mobile bit demand.
- **De-spec / content destruction in the accelerator platforms themselves — a distinct risk from consumer elasticity (SemiAnalysis, 2026-07-24 and 2026-07-21).** The platforms are cutting DRAM and HBM attach *because* memory is scarce and expensive, so bits-per-accelerator (which most models hold flat or growing) is the exposed variable rather than price. On AMD's MI455X the **up-to-1TB per-accelerator direct-attached LPDDR tier has been removed outright** and DDR5 de-spec'd to a far lower RDIMM content vs an original 2025 BOM of **138TB of DRAM per platform across LPDDR5X + DDR5 MRDIMM** — "much of the DRAM price increases are offset by aggressive de-specs" (SemiAnalysis "Can AMD break the CUDA Moat?", 2026-07-24). In the custom parts it is a direct HBM-content cut: **Meta's custom MI450/MI455 halves the compute silicon (8 dies → 4), cuts HBM from 12 stacks to 6 per package, and downgrades the HBM4 from 12-Hi to 8-Hi** — at one of AMD's two largest GPU customers, on a program SemiAnalysis argues "is going to nuke AMD's volume at Meta because TBD will vastly prefer Rubin," making the read **doubly negative: fewer stacks per package on a program that may itself shrink** (SemiAnalysis "Meta's Infrastructure Team Needs A Culture Reset", 2026-07-21; restated 2026-07-24). Read against the page's LTA thesis this cuts both ways — take-or-pay locks volume for the covered book, but the *un*covered ~33-50% of the market is exactly where de-spec bites. [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_21_ENC_Metas_Infrastructure_Team_Needs_A_Culture_Reset_email.html)
- **HBM4 top-bin qualification, not just capacity** — Nvidia's above-JEDEC **10.7 Gbps** HBM4 pin-speed target (vs **7.6 Gbps** on AMD's MI455X) forced suppliers to rework HBM4 and means Nvidia "must use a much higher-quality bin than AMD"; that concentrates the risk in **yield on the premium bin** rather than in wafer output, and widens the quality/price spread between the three suppliers (SemiAnalysis "Can AMD break the CUDA Moat?", 2026-07-24). Relevant given this page's core swing factor is HBM4 allocation at NVIDIA; SemiAnalysis names no bin winner.
- **Capex intensity / funding** — large greenfield fab + packaging build-out (Yongin, M15X, Indiana) into a possible cycle peak; rising hybrid-bonding/R&D cost; Korea construction delays (Daifuku via Jefferies Techknowledge, 2025-12-17). Funding mechanism corroborated tool-side: ASML's DRAM customers only placed (prepaid) EUV orders **after receiving hyperscaler prepayments** — i.e. the LTA/deposit chain is what de-risked the capex (BofA ASML callback, 2026-04-15).
- **Litho/tool intensity & EUV bottleneck risk** — Hynix's DRAM expansion is increasingly EUV-layer-intensive (1C ~6-7 EUV layers, a prospective 1D ~10 layers ~2028), but legacy capacity adds still pull **immersion**: e.g. **SK Hynix's Wuxi (China) fab runs no EUV**, so capacity growth there is immersion-driven — a China-exposure/tool-mix nuance (BofA ASML callback, 2026-04-15).
- **Korea/macro & FX** — KRW reporting, geopolitical/export-control exposure, China memory entrants.
- **Antitrust / price-fixing litigation** — a 17-plaintiff DRAM price-fixing class action filed in N.D. Cal. (2026-06-30) names Samsung, SK Hynix and Micron for allegedly conspiring to keep DRAM artificially scarce; early-stage legal/headline overhang (media report via @dnystedt, 2026-06-30).
- **Solidigm/NAND** — fragmented, structurally less profitable than DRAM, though MS sees Solidigm as a key NAND-upcycle beneficiary on high QLC eSSD exposure and fully-depreciated, low-cost fabs (MS AI NAND overview); HBM-driven DRAM mix-shift squeezing NAND wafers cuts both ways.

_Q2 2026 print (2026-07-28) → intra-quarter · Jul 28 → present. Prior window (Q1 2026, Apr 23 → Jul 28) archived in [## Changelog](#changelog). Timeline visual: [timeline.html](timeline.html)._

**Sinal vs gestão** — what management said on the Q2 2026 call × what the flow is saying (✓ confirma · ⚠ nuança · ✗ contesta):

_**Síntese — update 2026-07-30 (two days post-print, after the expert call and the Korea unwind):** the mechanical explanation for the "miss" is now settled and it is **mix, not execution**. The BTG former-SK-hynix executive: SK hynix runs **~40% of wafer capacity on HBM vs Samsung's ≤25%**, and HBM pricing is set roughly a year ahead, so when conventional DRAM spiked SK hynix's blended DRAM ASP printed **+30% against Samsung's +mid-40%** — Samsung captured the spot spike, SK hynix was contracted through it. Same source: **operating margin still went 72% → 76%**, "exceeding margins seen in companies like TSMC or Apple." The offsetting fact is the moat: **HBM4 yield ~80-85% at SK hynix (already at mature-generation levels) versus ~60% at Samsung**, which is what lets SK hynix supply Nvidia's Rubin volumes and is worth ~40% of potential output to Samsung. So @firstadopter's contrarian read on print night — "SK Hynix missing numbers is **BULLISH** for SK Hynix … a Q2 miss caused by more HBM mix actually means SK Hynix is BETTER positioned in the long run" (2026-07-28) — is directionally supported by the expert work, with the caveat that HBM's 40-60% stable margin is *structurally below* the >80% conventional server DRAM can earn in a spike. **Where the Street landed: KIS (the Korean axe that called the Q2 mechanics correctly) RAISED its target 24% to KRW 4.7m** two days after the print (@jukan05, 2026-07-29), while **BNK cut to KRW 1.48m** (from 1.85m) — an extraordinary 3x spread. Jefferies is BUY at **TP KRW 4,200,000** ("Overblown Fears Present Opportunity"). Price action for the record: **-16.2% on 07-28, -7.6% on 07-29**, ~-20% on the week; **Chairman Chey Tae-won bought 3,620 shares** on 07-30 (@jukan05). Forward pricing to watch: the same expert expects **Q3 DRAM increases to moderate to +13-18% q/q** from Q2's +30%._

Three houses on the same event, all constructive, implied upside clustered +84% (JPM) / +92.8% (Barclays, GDR basis) / +100% (UBS).** ⚠️ **BUT THE TENSION THIS CELL WAS BUILT ON IS NOT RESOLVED, ONLY RELIEVED — and Barclays says so from the bull side: buybacks are "likely the more preferred (and sensible) option given industry cyclicality — EVEN IF WE SEE TIGHTNESS HOLDING THROUGH 2027." A house that bullish still choosing the reversible instrument is a statement about the capex-into-a-price-peak risk this page's Síntese flags, not a dismissal of it. Management's original "~70% OP margin is not high enough to fund the capex step-up" argument now has to coexist with a >50%-of-FCF payout commitment — the two are only compatible if the FCF base is as large as the bulls model.** | **➕ 08-25 — THREE COMPANY CLARIFICATIONS THAT CHANGE HOW THE W40tn BUYBACK SHOULD BE READ (UBS Korea Summit, 2026-08-25).** 🔴 **(a) THE BUYBACK IS INSIDE THE POLICY, NOT ON TOP OF IT: the Won40tn programme “is PART OF the overall >50% FCF return.”** ➤ A mild negative for anyone who had modelled it as incremental — and it re-frames the 08-19 “upper end of expectations” headline. **(b) THE FLOOR IS NOW EXPLICIT: the change from “up to 50%” to “more than 50%” FCF return “does imply 50% IS THE FLOOR for the 2025-2027 period.”** **(c) NET-NEW AND TRADEABLE: “SK Hynix MAY BE SUPPORTING THE STOCK PRICE WITH ADDITIONAL BUYBACKS, WITHIN THIS POLICY, WHEN NEEDED and announced as such”** — the first acknowledgement on this page of discretionary price-support buying, which is what makes the same-day Jefferies desk observation (650K shs/day ≈ 15% of KRX-only volume) a policy fact rather than a curiosity. **Further detail promised AT THE 3Q26 RESULTS CALL** — which confirms the catalyst already on this page. **On capex the direction is unchanged and now has a 2027 number and a tool-in date: UBSe Won61tn in 2027 (+30% y/y) and “elevated for some time”; YONGIN 1 STARTS TAKING EQUIPMENT IN FEBRUARY 2027, second cleanroom later that year, first phase focused on DRAM.** ⚠️ **AND THE ADR QUESTION FINALLY HAS A COMPANY LINE, THOUGH NOT AN ANSWER: SK Hynix “will work through regulatory approval but may aim to increase over time the liquidity of the US listing” — no date, no size.**
| **PC / mobile** | **Temporary** sales adjustment from difficulty securing memory volumes; recovery as tightness eases | Consistent with the Q1 flag of PC/mobile softening on high prices — now explicitly attributed to *allocation*, not weak end-demand | **✓ confirma** |

The focus for the first phase will remain on DRAM.” Overall capex up y/y in 2027, UBSe Won61tn (+30% y/y), and “stay ELEVATED FOR SOME TIME.”** ⑦ **PROCESS ROADMAP — net-new and specific: “SK Hynix MAY CONTINUE 6F2 TIL 0a NM (we believe still TBC vs. 4F2), then move to 4F2 for several nodes TIL 3D DRAM.” NAND: capacity “could be added in DALIAN on FLOATING GATE process tech, which over time may move to 240L FROM 144/192L”; M17 (NAND) “expected to start MP in 1H29.”** ➤ The Dalian line corroborates the 08-24 @jukan05 relay and the 08-25 DIGITIMES report below with the process detail neither had. ⑧ **VALUATION AND THE ESTIMATE GAP, which is the real reason to hold the note: UBS values SK Hynix at 4.32x NTM P/BV on a LONG-TERM ROE of 49.2% and CoE of 11.4%. UBS is 12% ABOVE '27E CONSENSUS, "which we believe HAS NOW FACTORED IN LTA ADJUSTMENTS" (UBSe '27 OP +11.7% / EPS +14.4% vs cons).** ⚠️ **Valuation marks for the drawdown record: shares DOWN 49% FROM THEIR 22 JUNE PEAK, trading 1.80x NTM P/BV "discounting a LONG-TERM ROE OF 20.5% VS OUR ESTIMATE OF 41.1%"; UBS values the shares at 3.61x NTM P/BV on a 41.1% LT ROE and 11.4% CoE. The manager "acknowledged that 2X LEVERAGED ETFs WILL CONTINUE TO CONTRIBUTE TO STOCK-MARKET VOLATILITY (with inverse/gamma effects), but the government's NEW REGULATIONS ON LEVERAGED ETFs, together with ongoing pension fund inflows, should help REDUCE VOLATILITY… and support a RE-RATING (for undervalued memory/financial)."** ⚠️ **That is a structural-flow argument for why the July mechanism (retail leverage + 2x single-stock ETFs) should be less violent next time, from a participant rather than a strategist. Read alongside [themes/macro-cycle](themes/macro-cycle.md) and the Goldman margin-call data (>1.2mn Korean leveraged accounts margin-called by 13 July, ~320-360k fully liquidated).** |

Culpan: such stop-starts are "also bad for memory makers like [[MU]] and SK Hynix because they are COMMODITY PLAYERS THAT SUFFER WILD PRICE SWINGS WHICH CAN TURN PROFIT TO LOSS IN THE BLINK OF AN EYE."** **His week-in-review framing of this page's own print is worth keeping for the record because it is the outside view: "SK Hynix… posted revenue and earnings which SKYROCKETED WHILE STILL MISSING ESTIMATES. The company's outlook for the rest of the year was also a disappointment. Seoul-traded shares sank even as delusional perma-bulls tried to argue that 'missing earnings was actually a good thing for SK Hynix.'"** **The counterweight sits in the row above: DRAM contract prices rose 30-50% q/q in July and spot is expected to rally further in August, which is not the price behaviour of a market whose largest buyers are slowing purchases. The honest reading is that late-binding is an option the hyperscalers have described, not a decision they have made — and memory is the part of the chain where that option, if exercised, does the most damage fastest. Watch hyperscaler capex MIX (short- vs long-lived) rather than headline capex.** (Culpium, 2026-08-02, opinion; Hood quotes are from the MSFT 4Q26 call transcript.) |
| 08-01 | **Susquehanna · Mehdi Hosseini (Tech Hardware weekly) + UBS positioning — node migration over QLC, the CXMT defence, and the month's drawdown quantified** | produto | **mixed** | **① NAND STRATEGY: SK hynix "is similarly FOCUSED ON NODE MIGRATION, targeting its 321-LAYER TECHNOLOGY TO REACH 50% OF DOMESTIC PRODUCTION BY YEAR-END to support surging eSSD demand"** — i.e. it is playing the middle path between [[SAMSUNG]]'s aggressive 2Tb QLC ramp (bits to more than double in 2H26; V10 V-NAND mass production August 2026) and [[KIOXIA]]'s explicit refusal to chase QLC as "too costly for broad deployment." ⚠️ **Layer count is a cost-per-bit lever that works across TLC and QLC alike, so this is the lowest-regret of the three positions — but it also means SK hynix is not making the density bet that captures the AI-storage share if Samsung is right about the requirement.** **② THE CXMT DEFENCE IS BEING FOUGHT ON PRODUCT, NOT POLICY: "SK hynix plans to DIMINISH CXMT'S GOLDEN PERIOD with the only way it knows — TECHNOLOGICAL SUPERIORITY, as it WINS OVER CHINESE CUSTOMERS WITH LPDDR6."** ⚠️ **Read alongside the counter-reports in the same link flow: "CXMT is betting on CUSTOMIZED DRAM to circumvent U.S. sanctions on HBM; KOREAN SUPPLIERS PLAYING SAFE COULD MISS OUT ON A MAJOR OPPORTUNITY," and "Innosilicon's LPDDR6 memory IP lands at a major Korean DRAM maker." The first frames SK hynix's caution on custom DRAM as a strategic risk rather than prudence; the second suggests external IP is being licensed into the LPDDR6 effort. Both are press relays with no company confirmation — logged as the shape of the China-competition narrative this week, not adopted. Also: "CXMT EVICTED HUAWEI-LINKED ENGINEERS from its R&D facility," and "[[SAMSUNG]], SK hynix chase DIVERGING AI DEALS" (BusinessKorea).** **③ THE DRAWDOWN, QUANTIFIED — and it is the largest in the memory complex: SK hynix −34% m/m, against [[MU]] −28% and [[SAMSUNG]] −22%, with the KOSPI then +17% ON FRIDAY ALONE** (UBS · Robert Ruple, "Tech Musings", 2026-08-01). ⚠️ **A −34% month on a company whose operating margin printed in the 72-76% range is not a fundamentals move, and the mechanism is now documented: SK hynix was one of the two positions "the entire hedge fund community knew" Situational Awareness was massively long, and the name traded through the liquidation ahead of the news (see [themes/macro-cycle](themes/macro-cycle.md) and [[ANTHROPIC]]). The +17% KOSPI Friday is the other side of the same flow. Hosseini carries 000660.KS at NEUTRAL, KRW1,718,000 — the only Neutral among his memory names, with Samsung at Positive and [[MU]]/[[SNDK]] Positive** (2026-08-01). **UBS hosted a Korea Tech update call with Nick Gaudios and Jimmy Yoon on memory and MLCCs; replay available.** (Susquehanna · Mehdi Hosseini; UBS · Robert Ruple — both 2026-08-01) |
| 07-30 | Bernstein · David Dai / Carmine Milano / Juho Hwang ("ASML: Best Idea 3Q26") | produto | **bull** | **Read-through only — an [[ASML]] Best-Idea note with no SK hynix rating or PT. The relevance is that Bernstein names SK hynix as the industry's LEAD High-NA adopter, ahead of every logic player except Intel, and argues DRAM — not logic — is where High-NA economics work best.** Verbatim: **"SK hynix has the clearest early signal: the company announced in September 2025 that it had assembled ASML's TWINSCAN EXE:5200B, the first production-oriented HNA system, at its M16 fab for next-generation DRAM development."** The economic argument behind that lead is specific and structural: **"the strongest economic case for HNA is in DRAM, as DRAM dies are smaller than logic dies and therefore do not require stitching. This translates into higher throughput and a lower cost per exposure, making HNA adoption easier to justify economically."** Bernstein's adoption roadmap (Exhibit 10) has **DRAM HNA beginning with SK hynix and [[SAMSUNG]] at the 1d node**, while [[TSM]] waits until ~A10 at the end of the decade. **The node-migration arithmetic underneath is the part to hold, because it is a capex-intensity claim, not a tool-vendor claim: DRAM EUV exposures per node run ~2 at 1a → 3 at 1b → 5 at 1c → 8 at 1d, rising into the low teens at 0b/0c; DRAM litho intensity moves from <20% at 1Y/1Z → 26% at 1c → ~30% at 1d; and total DRAM EUV exposures compound 4.5x from 3.4 MWPM (2025) to 26.3 MWPM (2030).** ⚠️ **Read honestly, that is a rising cost-per-bit-of-capacity headwind for SK hynix as a litho BUYER, at the same time as it is a demand tailwind for ASML** — the note is written from the vendor's side and does not cost it from the memory maker's P&L. The offset Bernstein offers is process simplification: HNA single-exposure replaces LNA multi-patterning, eliminating masks, litho-etch loops and cut steps, with **litho capex/opex ~1.2x but non-litho cost declining**, so total process cost can fall even as the tool bill rises. Also relevant to the 4F² debate: Bernstein frames the 6F²→4F² transition around 0a as **"a temporary easing of scaling requirements rather than a departure from the long-term trend"** of shrinking features — i.e. it does not read 4F² as a break in the EUV-layer trajectory. Demand context: ASML guided FY26 memory system sales **+75% y/y vs +25% for advanced logic**, with memory now ~50% of system sales (from ~30% historically) — management's word for the DRAM setup was a **"perfect storm"** of node migration and volume capacity adds at once. (Bernstein · David Dai, 2026-07-30) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/BERN_258544.html) |
| 07-31 | **BofA · Simon Woo / Dai Shen / Vivek Arya — Global Memory Tech weekly** | supply | **bull** | **★ The LTA mechanics, and they invert the common bear reading of high contract coverage. "LTA does not fix memory chip price… lot of room to raise contract price every quarter but there is LIMIT FOR PRICE CUT." Specifically: Samsung said 60-70% OF ITS MEMORY SALES WILL BE UNDER LTA, but with ASYMMETRIC terms — a price-cut FLOOR of sub-5% down q/q against price INCREASES broadly open at 10-20%+. And on tenor: Samsung's LTAs (mainly for US Big Tech) are mostly 5-YEAR ROLLING, so a 6th year can be added right after or before the 1st year completes.** ⚠️ **So LTAs here are not price locks — they are DOWNSIDE COLLARS WITH UNCAPPED UPSIDE on a rolling tenor. High LTA coverage is therefore bullish for realised ASP stability rather than a cap on it, which is the opposite of how rising contract coverage is usually read at this point in a cycle.** **Spot: sentiment positive into August on three drivers — (1) chip order increases for inventory restocking; (2) many Tier-2 OEMs resuming smartphone/PC launches for Sept/4Q DESPITE high memory cost; (3) China domestic smartphone shipments bottoming out — only 17MN UNITS IN JUNE vs a normal 25-30mn, with lean channel inventories. ⚠️ Note the DRAM/NAND divergence in the confirmed July contract prints: TrendForce July DRAM contract price UP LOW-TEENS % M/M = 30-50% Q/Q, but FLAT FOR NAND.** **On China supply, the reason it is not showing up in spot: "minimal impact from China local chipmakers' capacity expansion — China-branded memory NOT COMMONLY TRADED IN THE SPOT MARKET; even Korea/US/Japan chips not much available for white-box OEMs."** Adjacent confirmation that the AI-server pull is broad: **SEMCO and LG Innotek both guided bullishly on AI MLCC and FC-BGA, with 2Q OP more than doubling y/y, stronger LTA orders, LARGE PREPAYMENTS funding new fab construction, and contract price increases through 2H and 2027 — and all three top MLCC makers (SEMCO, Murata, Taiyo Yuden) have raised prices.** |

BofA hosted a Korea ETF expert call the same week: Korean ETF AUM ~W500tn across 1,000+ funds = **30%+ of KOSPI trading value**, with 2x leveraged ETFs acknowledged as an ongoing volatility contributor, partly offset by new leveraged-ETF regulation and pension inflows into active ETFs. |

And we have Samsung on Thursday" — so read every figure here as an expectation, not a result.* **The bogey: "for Hynix the numbers were anywhere between 60 to 65 trillion. Our number is currently at 65 trillion. We've seen some adjustment towards the 60 handle."** Against the actual **₩60.54tn OP**, the print landed **at the bottom of the buyside range and ~7% below MS's own ₩65tn** — the drift Sean flagged was directionally right, and the houses that had not cut were the ones marked. **His framing is the more valuable part: "the number itself will not be that meaningful. The reaction to any number would actually give us a better sense of whether this selloff is actually at the tail end. If tomorrow we do have numbers and stocks bouncing, I think that would be good news for the sector."** What the tape then delivered — SK Hynix **opening -6% and recovering to -2.3%** while [[MU]] fell ~8.9% intraday plus ~4% after hours — reads as an **ambiguous-to-mildly-constructive** result on Sean's own test for the name and a clearly negative one for the complex. **On the buyback: "For Hynix, my view is that it could happen, given the current volatility, but again, we have no visibility on that"** — against a stated buyside view that "valuations are clearly an attractive entry point for memory **with the potential buyback as near-term catalyst**." It did not come; that catalyst is now a miss, not a pending item. **The net-new analytical point is on supply: Sean names "Hynix probably HBM3E, where no HBM4 will actually reduce the amount of total HBM and more DRAM will be produced as a result" as one driver of TrendForce industry DRAM bit growth moving from "a 25% type of grow to now reaching 31%" this year and ~24% next** (alongside [[SAMSUNG]]'s P4 second-floor cleanroom) — i.e. the same HBM4 slip that caused the revenue miss is *adding commodity DRAM bits to the industry*. **On CXMT he puts the re-rating in transfer terms: the debut closed at a US$492bn market cap, "the 25th most valuable company in the world upon the IPO. You can probably say that that's quite proportionate to the amount of market cap that was taken away from stocks like Hynix"** — while bounding the fundamentals: "probably doesn't impact this cycle, maybe in the outer years," with CXMT "**currently at about 30,000 wafers capacity**" running DDR5 at "slightly lower on the clock speed versus mainstream DRAM, but nevertheless good enough for some of the mid to low end products in China," and the real concern being "**the expansion into 2030 could be more aggressive**." ⚠️ **Data-integrity flag: the "about 30,000 wafers capacity" figure is irreconcilable with the ~290-350k wspm CXMT capacity this page and [[MU]] carry from Wells Fargo (07-14) and BofA (07-12) — treat it as a transcription or sub-segment artifact and use the WF/BofA numbers.** Sector frame: his three-prong debate (AI spend robust; LTA re-rating still an *upside* story; the cycle) resolves by elimination onto the cycle — **y/y DRAM pricing peaks in 3Q, 3Q hikes ~15-20%, and 4Q would need +60-70% sequential to sustain the second derivative, "a bit tough to achieve."** Also: TrendForce inventory up since 2Q, **first time since 4Q last year, and at module makers not producers**, concentrated in non-AI NAND. (Morgan Stanley webcast "2Q26 memory, Intel implications and ARM preview" · Sean, 2026-07-28) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_28_2q26_ms_memory_intel_implications_and_arm_preview.html) |

WHATEVER THEY PRODUCE, THEY BUY. That's the current situation. This is a SUPPLY SIDE MARKET. But once supply is big enough compared to demand, then it will be different. They will choose better product or cheaper price."** **(5) WHY HE THINKS 2028 IS THE HINGE AND WHY HE STILL DOESN'T FEAR IT: the three players' new fabs land "around this 28 time period," so 2028 is when the balance is tested — but hyperscaler capex is effectively pre-committed ("they PREPAID for some power company or some cooling system… so they cannot reduce now," with the construction realised around 2028), and LTA coverage (Samsung 60-70% of wafer capacity ≈ 80% of revenue) plus CUSTOMISED product means "over supply situation will not be easy. It's not like a prior cycle." Price increases "will be moderated after 28, but still it will grow."** **(6) 3D DRAM commercialisation targeted "maybe in 2032 or 2033," because below the 1D node "it is practically difficult to scale down further"; hybrid bonding replaces TCB from HBM4E/HBM5 for speed, energy efficiency and heat dissipation.** (BTG Pactual-hosted expert call · Hongkyo Jung, former SK Hynix executive, "Beyond the Earnings: What SK Hynix & Samsung Tell Us About the Memory Cycle", 2026-07-30) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_30_btg_former_hynix_expert_30_07_26.html) |

Pricing ladder: **HBM3E $300-400 → HBM4 $500-600** on double the I/Os. Margin structure: **HBM 40-60% stable, vs conventional advanced server DRAM sometimes >80% during sharp price spikes** — i.e. HBM mix is a near-term drag and a long-term moat. **Síntese do quarter:** the first headline miss of the cycle — **OP ₩60.54tn vs ₩64.2tn, revenue ₩79.3tn vs ₩83.9tn** — and the most important qualifier is that it is a miss against a **stale screen consensus**: local brokers were already at ~₩60tn and Jefferies' analyst at ₩62tn. Margins did not break (**GM 83%, OM 76%, EBITDA 81%**) and the sequential level still rose off Q1. **The genuine negative is mechanical and specific: the revenue shortfall is attributed to the HBM4 ramp being late**, even though HBM4 shipments did begin in Q2 and 1cnm HBM4E samples are already with a major customer. Two things make this print more consequential than its numbers. **(1) The moat is being re-read as a constraint.** Jefferies expects the miss to feed the view that **SK Hynix has been less aggressive than [[SAMSUNG]] on pricing precisely because of its HBM commitments to [[NVDA]]** — the anchor relationship that underwrites this page's supplier-differentiation thesis is now the reason it may be leaving price on the table. **(2) An ~80% capex increase to the high-₩40tn range, with no buyback despite large net cash**, lands in the same week Jefferies research calls **memory prices to peak ~1H27** with hikes already tracking **15-20% vs 25-30% expected** and **2027 consensus requiring ~US$2tn of US AI capex**. That is the bear case in one sentence: peak-margin cash being committed to a capacity step-up into a price peak. Set against it, management's demand framing got *broader*, not narrower — **agentic AI expanding demand across HBM/server DRAM/eSSD**, PC/mobile softness explicitly a **volume-allocation** problem rather than end-demand — and DRAM ASP was still **+30% q/q**. The unresolved question for the next two quarters is whether **HBM4 catches up before pricing rolls**, with **CXMT's +466% IPO debut** now supplying a permanent sentiment overhang on the China supply question. **Two additions from MS's pre-print webcast (Sean, 07-28) that change how the print should be scored.** First, the **bogey**: MS was at **₩65tn** with the buyside range **₩60-65tn** and "adjustment towards the 60 handle" already underway — so the **₩60.54tn** print landed at the *bottom* of the range and ~7% below MS, which makes the "stale screen consensus" defence above accurate but narrower than it reads: the sell-side had cut, MS had not. Sean's own test was explicit and better than the number — **"the number itself will not be that meaningful. The reaction to any number would actually give us a better sense of whether this selloff is actually at the tail end"** — and the reaction (**Hynix opening -6% and recovering to -2.3%**, while [[MU]] fell ~8.9% intraday plus ~4% after hours) scores as **ambiguous-to-mildly-constructive for the name and negative for the complex**, not the clean bounce he was looking for. Second, and analytically the more useful: **the HBM4 delay is not only a revenue event, it is a supply event.** Sean names **"Hynix probably HBM3E, where no HBM4 will actually reduce the amount of total HBM and more DRAM will be produced as a result"** as a driver of TrendForce industry **DRAM bit growth moving 25% → ~31% this year (~24% next)** — so the freed wafer lands in commodity DRAM, where returns are currently competitive with HBM per this page's own HBM-vs-EPS paradox. Hold all three legs: **share/mix negative on the [[NVDA]] franchise, near-term bit/EPS cushion, and incremental industry supply feeding exactly the cycle-durability bear the Jefferies note is pressing.** Finally, the **buyback that did not come was an expected catalyst, not an absent question** — Sean, pre-print: "it could happen, given the current volatility, but again, we have no visibility," against a buyside "of the view that valuations are clearly an attractive entry point for memory with the potential buyback as near-term catalyst." Contrast [[SAMSUNG]], where the same webcast argues the buyback window is structurally *year-end* rather than simply absent. See [themes/hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md), [themes/china-export](themes/china-export.md). **Update 2026-08-03 (Fubon · Sherman Shang / Arthur, hosted by Jefferies — the primary supply-chain call): the first leg of the unresolved question now has an answer, and it is the favourable one. The question this Síntese posed was whether "HBM4 catches up before pricing rolls." Fubon dates the catch-up: "Rubin with Hynix HBM4 will start to ramp up at LATE AUGUST OR SEPTEMBER WITH A BIGGER VOLUME, which is why in Q4 we will see a higher volume of Rubin" — with only "a small volume of Micron and Samsung's HBM" in the chain so far. Rubin production of 200-300K units in Q3 stepping to 700-800K in Q4 is therefore PACED BY SK HYNIX, which reframes the "moat as a constraint" reading: the NVDA anchor relationship is what the platform's volume curve is built on, and the Q2 miss reads as timing inside a socket the company still owns rather than share loss to the peers. Against it, three offsets to keep live: (i) the HBM4 $/GB step-up to $30-32/GB from $17-18/GB HBM3-class is being captured across all three suppliers, so it is a cycle fact rather than a Hynix edge (⚠ the transcript dates it "in 2026" in one place and "in 2027" in another; ambiguity carried); (ii) the surviving Rubin Ultra SKU is LIGHTER on HBM — the four-compute-die ">1,024 GB/chip" part is cancelled and the two-die part is "around 384 GB" (B200 ~192GB / B300 ~288GB) — so content per package is a genuine headwind even though ODMs reject any 2027 memory de-spec "including the Google, AWS" and are raising purchasing capex against a $7-8m Rubin rack; (iii) "the HBM base die issue" is one of the two named causes of the ~two-month Rubin push-out, so the volume vendor also carries the execution risk. Net: the ramp-timing leg of the bear case weakens materially from late August; the pricing-peak and content-per-package legs are untouched.** **Update 2026-08-17 (/run-inbox) — the pricing-peak leg now has its most explicit dated bear, and it comes from the same house this page already quotes on the other side.** TrendForce, presented at FMS and relayed by FUNDA (2026-08-11): **the DRAM supply/demand gap bottoms and begins to NARROW in 2027, with the sufficiency ratio turning POSITIVE at +4.0% in 2028; NAND turns positive at +0.4% in 2027 and reaches 2% oversupply in 2028.** ⚠️ **Log the source's own inconsistency rather than adopting either version: TrendForce on 07-30 (already on [themes/hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md)) had the DRAM sufficiency gap WIDENING in 2027. Twelve days later, at FMS, it has the gap narrowing from 2027 and positive by 2028. Same house, opposite direction on the 2027 DRAM inflection — and this is a relay, not the TrendForce publication.** Against it, FUNDA explicitly disagrees: the ASP-growth flattening is *"a process of structural normalization,"* not a signal of 2027-28 oversupply, and it expects **memory prices to hold at elevated levels through 2027** on a rising LTA share and a mix shifting further to data centers, *"rather than peaking and collapsing."* ➜ **For this page the relevant asymmetry is that a 2028 DRAM oversupply call lands squarely on the ~80% capex step-up into the high-₩40tn range that this Síntese already flags as the bear case in one sentence: peak-margin cash committed to capacity arriving into a possible sufficiency flip. Unresolved and deliberately not netted.** Separately, the company's own FMS slide (**3D stacked DRAM, HBF, CXL Pooled Memory as three new tiers**) broadens the product answer to exactly that risk — but only HBF has a date, and [[NVDA]] was absent from every HBF panel. **Update 2026-08-20 (/run-inbox — three houses on the W40trn event, plus two company primaries): the "no buyback despite large net cash" clause in the bear sentence above is now DEAD, and it died above the bar rather than at it.** The buyback landed on 08-19 at **W40trn with full cancellation**, and the only house that published its own bar shows how far above expectations that is: **UBS "had modelled Won12tn" and put buy-side expectations at "c.Won20-50tn"** (UBS, 2026-08-19) — against JPM's **W29.3tn of 2026E buyback** already on this page (08-09), so the print **cleared every estimate the desk had**. The policy ceiling moved with it, from **"within the range of 50%"** to **">50%"/"50% or higher"** of cumulative 2025-27 FCF (Barclays 2026-08-19; JPM 2026-08-20), which UBS models as taking **2026E FCF returned to shareholders from 15% to 30%** off this one disclosure. **Three houses, one event, tightly clustered upside: JPM +84% (W2.75m vs W1.491m), Barclays +92.8% (US$300 vs US$155.62), UBS +100% (W3.0m vs W1.5m)** — ⚠️ **and note the trap: Barclays quotes the name in USD on the GDR line from its European Technology Hardware desk, so its US$300 is NOT ladderable against the KRW marks; only the upside is.** ➜ **BUT THE BEAR SENTENCE ONLY LOSES HALF ITS SUBJECT, AND THE SURVIVING HALF IS THE ONE THAT MATTERS. "Peak-margin cash committed to a capacity step-up into a price peak" was never really about the absent buyback — it was about the ~80% capex step-up landing into the possible 2028 sufficiency flip. A >50%-of-FCF payout commitment ON TOP of that capex path does not relieve the tension, it TIGHTENS it: both claims on the same cash are now contractual-ish, and they are only jointly affordable if the FCF base is as large as the bulls model (Barclays c.51% of 25-27 FCF returned by YE27 ≈ c.15% of market cap; JPM a 16.7% 2027E cash yield). ⚠️ The clearest tell is that Barclays — bullish enough to write that it sees "tightness holding through 2027" — STILL prefers buybacks to dividends "given industry cyclicality," i.e. it wants the reversible instrument. And the two houses disagree wildly on the run-rate: Barclays models c.KRW200trn of 2027 buyback against JPM's W73.3tn, a ~2.7x spread on the same policy. Neither is a forecast to trade; the disclosed facts stop at "additional returns within the same program period, detailed at 3Q26."** **On the demand side, two company primaries added this run and both cut the same way — broader, not cheaper.** Solidigm's co-CEO gives the agentic claim its mechanism and its buyer-side evidence: agentic workflows need **"active storage"** for multi-agent hand-offs on a common dataset, and **NVIDIA's own "STX" server exists because "memory alone is not enough to store all the context and key-values (KV cache)"**, with Solidigm supplying into that stack via the **BlueField DPU** (Bloomberg Intelligence "Tech Disruptors" · Xin Guo, 2026-07-27) — ⚠️ **"STX" is NVIDIA's product name here, not [[STX]]'s ticker; the router mis-routed the file on that collision.** That reconciles a loose end this Síntese left open: SK hynix's **HBF anchor is the [[GOOG]]/ASIC camp with [[NVDA]] absent from every panel, but its eSSD franchise sits INSIDE NVIDIA's preferred DPU-mediated storage path** — the group is on both branches of the storage-tier debate via different products. Alongside it, **SK Telecom + NVIDIA plan up to 2GW of AI DC capacity on Vera Rubin systems AND SK hynix HBM via a new entity "SK Hyper"** (MS "AI Sovereignty", 2026-08-19) — intra-group demand that need not be won against [[SAMSUNG]] or [[MU]], though ⚠️ **with no timeline and only 100MW dated (AWS, late 2027) against a 1GW site cap, treat 2GW as ambition** — and the company published a **co-packaged-optics roadmap in *Nature Electronics*** under the framing that **"AI competition is shifting from chips to systems"** (SK hynix Newsroom, 2026-08-20), ⚠️ **a peer-reviewed roadmap with no date, partner or spec — a fourth system-level vector alongside the FMS three tiers, not a product.** ➜ **Net for the quarter: the capital-return leg of the bear case is closed and closed favourably; the demand-breadth leg keeps strengthening from primary sources; and the two legs that actually decide the name — the price the breadth clears at, and whether an ~80%-higher capex path plus a >50% payout both survive a 2028 sufficiency flip — are exactly where they were. Nothing netted.** 🆕 **UPDATE 2026-08-21 (/run-inbox) — one addition and one deliberate NON-addition, and the non-addition is the more important discipline.** **The addition:** an independent read-through (DAMNANG (Substack), "HBM Density Peak: Why AI Value Is Moving From Memory to Connectivity", 2026-08-20 — ⚠️ **not a broker**) supplies the *mechanism* under the CPO primary already logged on this page — **compute throughput 3x per ~2 years vs interconnect bandwidth ~1.4x** — and names the one *shipping* commercial artefact of the memory→connectivity shift: **CMM-Ax with [[MRVL]] (Structera A + SK hynix DRAM and software stack, disclosed Aug-2026)**. Read together with the FMS three-tier slide (08-11) and the *Nature Electronics* roadmap (08-20), the pattern is that the company is **monetising the ELECTRICAL disaggregation tier now while the OPTICAL tier stays a research direction** — which is a scope-widening story (die → controller, base die, packaging, memory placement), not a near-term revenue line. **The non-addition:** the same piece claims Rubin Ultra is being de-spec'd to **HBM4 8-Hi**, and the author himself calls it **UNCONFIRMED** — so it is logged in the HBM4 Sinal cell and **NOT scored**. If it were ever confirmed it would be the first genuine **mix-DOWN in HBM content per GPU** this page has had to price, against a franchise whose moat is high-stack qualification. That is exactly why it is held at arm's length rather than folded into the thesis — and why the CXMT work archived from 07-27 matters more for the near term: the China price-war leg of the bear case weakens on inspection (see Risks), while the leg that actually moves this stock is unchanged — **"what moves SK hynix shares is [the capex plans of a handful of AI-accelerator customers], not CXMT."**

**🆕 08-09/10 — the bear-headline week, and what actually changed:** three of the four drags (HBM discount, Solidigm IPO, Chongqing stake) are **headline risk that management has now put on a ≤1-month disclosure clock**, not confirmed facts; the fourth (W54tn capex) is confirmed but back-end loaded to 2031. The genuinely new bear datapoint is competitive rather than corporate: **Samsung HBM4 yield "nears 80%"** (DIGITIMES, 2026-08-10) — i.e. the second source is qualifying, into a 2027 HBM price negotiation SKH is already being reported as discounting. Against that, **JPM prefers SEC > SKH in the near term** inside an OW-on-both memory call (JPM / Kwon, 2026-08-09), and **SIG prefers Micron specifically on "HBM share gains against SK Hynix"** (SIG / Hosseini, 2026-08-10). Two of the three houses that touched Korean memory this week are routing incremental money *away* from SKH within a bullish memory view — that is the read, not the tape. `demanda/valuation · mixed`
**🆕 Síntese — addendum 2026-08-25 (the UBS Korea Summit note, the competitor's own IR call, and three supply-side models — eight sources, and the quarter's question has changed shape).** When this window opened the debate was **“is the HBM4 ramp late, and does the capex step-up land into a price peak.”** It is now **“how many HBM layers ship, who pays for share, and does the 2027 bit-supply number break the tightness story.”** Four things moved. ① **THE CAPITAL-RETURN STORY IS BETTER SPECIFIED AND SLIGHTLY WORSE: the Won40tn buyback is INSIDE the >50%-of-FCF policy rather than additive, but 50% is now an explicit FLOOR for 2025-2027 and the company has effectively acknowledged DISCRETIONARY PRICE-SUPPORT BUYING — “SK Hynix may be supporting the stock price with additional buybacks, within this policy, when needed”** (UBS, 2026-08-25). With the Jefferies desk sizing daily buyback at ~15% of KRX-only volume the same day, **the tape and the policy are now the same object, and that is a reason to discount price action as evidence, not to celebrate it.** ② **THE LTA BOOK LOCKS VOLUME, NOT PRICE — AND TWO OF THE THREE MAJORS SAID SO IN THE SAME 48 HOURS.** UBS: >50% of bits LTA-covered by year-end, *“the main focus is on VOLUME VISIBILITY WITH NO PRICING FLOOR/CEILING.”* Samsung IR, relayed by BofA: ASP *“remains negotiable on a quarterly basis even under LTAs.”* ➤ **This is symmetric and the page should stop treating it as one-sided: it removes the downside floor the LTA bulls implied AND the upside cap the Jefferies bear implied.** ③ **THE DE-SPEC RUMOUR HAS BEEN UPGRADED TO A CONTESTED BROKER VIEW, NOT TO A FACT.** UBS now states as its own view that **NVIDIA moved to HBM4 8-Hi from HBM4E 12-Hi for VR/R300**, Mizuho/SemiAnalysis go further (**cap at 4-8Hi**; TPUv8 cut to 8-Hi), and Samsung's DS spokesperson confirms customers are evaluating lower stack heights **because of SUPPLY CONSTRAINT, not weak demand**. **Against all three, Morgan Stanley's 08-24 HBM demand model still carries Rubin R200 at HBM4 12-Hi.** ⚠️ **Left OPEN. The DAMNANG sensitivity already on this page — ~1.63x effective output ceiling, HBM revenue ~81.5-98% of plan — remains the right frame and remains a sensitivity, not a forecast.** ④ **THE GENUINELY NEW BEAR IS COMPETITIVE, AND IT IS THE COMPETITOR'S OWN WORDS: Samsung says it will hold mid-to-high-30s% HBM share in 2027 and will buy it with capacity “REGARDLESS OF THE PROFITABILITY.”** That is the one mechanism capable of breaking the pricing discipline underpinning the “memory is no longer cyclical” leg of this page — and it arrives the same day Jefferies' Sherman claims Hynix is **late at NVIDIA**. ⚠️ **An internal inconsistency inside Samsung's own call (mid-to-high-30s% for all-HBM vs “mid- to high-twenty percent” in the HBM4E answer) is logged, not reconciled.** **Net:** UBS's estimate gap to consensus widens every year (**+11.9% / +29.0% / +49.0% in 12/26E-28E EPS**) on **78-82% EBIT margins** and net cash building to **Won462tn by 2028E** — i.e. the bull case is now almost entirely a *durability* call, and every one of the four items above is a durability test with a date on it. **The nearest is UBS's own named catalyst: ~18 September 2026, pricing-negotiation finalisation plus a further shareholder-return update.**

| HBM cycle | HBM4 mass production in Sept; ships Q4; "sold out since 2023" | HBM rev >2x YoY; HBM3E + HBM4 supplied simultaneously | HBM4 ramp prep; demand "far exceeding supply capacity" | **HBM4 shipments BEGAN in Q2** at required speeds, ramping 2H; **1cnm HBM4E samples to a major customer in 1H**; HBM4 ramp delay cited as the revenue shortfall |
| DRAM | 128GB+ DDR5 shipments >2x QoQ; "sold out 2026" | 1cnm full mass production; 256GB DDR5 RDIMM | "Significant price increases"; customers prioritize volume | Q2 bits +HSD, **ASP +30% q/q**; 2026 bit growth **mid-20s%**; Q3 bits **+10%**, server-focused; SOCAMM2 fully commercialised on 1C |
| NAND / eSSD | AI server eSSD at price premiums; 321-layer ramp | 321-layer QLC done; highest annual NAND rev on record | Shipments ~-10% QoQ on high base; group eSSD ~$4.64bn | eSSD named part of the structural AI-server demand expansion (not separately quantified in the first-take flow) |
| Capex / footprint | 2026 investment to increase vs 2025 | Capex up "significantly YoY"; M15X, Yongin, Indiana | — | **FY capex up to +80% to the high-₩40tn range**, being pulled in; **no buyback despite large net cash** |
| Demand / supply balance | 2026 contracts finalized; tightness into 2027 | Could not fully meet HBM demand; 2026 locked | Tight supply; flags PC/mobile softening on high prices | Demand **broadening as AI turns agentic**; Big Tech expanding procurement on a **compute-capacity shortage**; **PC/mobile softness = difficulty securing volumes**, recovery as tightness eases |

_Source: SKHYNIX earnings calls (dates above); management commentary, paraphrased._
