## Changelog
- **2026-08-25 (/run-inbox — 8 current sources + 7 DAMNANG archive backfills). Added: 8 Full-log rows, 3 Sinal cells EXTENDED (`HBM4`, `Capex / capital return`, `Demanda` — nothing deleted), 1 Síntese addendum, 8 `## Debate / thesis` archive bullets, 10 Sources entries, this record.** 🔴 **NOTHING SUPERSEDED: UBS reiterates BUY at PT Won 3,000,000 UNCHANGED (px Won1,678,000, 25-Aug), and no other source in this run carries an SK hynix rating or PT (Mizuho has suspended coverage; both MS notes are on other subjects; the Samsung IR call, DIGITIMES and WSJ are not broker research). The standing PT ladder — Mirae W4,200,000, BofA W3,590,000, UBS W3,000,000, JPM W2,750,000, Barclays US$300 on the GDR line — is untouched.**
  🔴 **THREE COMPANY CLARIFICATIONS THAT CHANGE HOW THE W40tn BUYBACK READS, and one of them is mildly negative:** (a) the **Won40tn programme is PART OF the >50%-of-FCF return, not additive to it**; (b) the “up to 50%” → “more than 50%” wording change **makes 50% an explicit FLOOR for 2025-2027**; (c) net-new and tradeable — **“SK Hynix may be SUPPORTING THE STOCK PRICE with additional buybacks, within this policy, when needed and announced as such.”** ➤ (c) turns the same-day Jefferies desk observation that **650K shs/day ≈ 15% of KRX-only volume** into a policy fact, and is a standing reason to discount price action as evidence on this name. Further detail promised **at the 3Q26 results call** — confirming the catalyst already on the page. **The ADR also gets its first company framing: regulatory approval to work through, US-listing liquidity to build “over time.” No date, no size.**
  🔴 **THE LTA TERMS ARE NOW ON THE PAGE AND THEY ARE SYMMETRIC, WHICH RETIRES A ONE-SIDED READ: UBS — >50% of bits LTA-covered by year-end, “the main focus is on VOLUME VISIBILITY WITH NO PRICING FLOOR/CEILING.” [[SAMSUNG]] IR the same 48 hours — ASP “remains negotiable on a quarterly basis even under LTAs.”** Two of the three majors say the books lock **volume, not price**. ➤ **That removes the downside floor the LTA bulls implied AND the upside cap the Jefferies ‘LTAs limit pricing optionality’ bear implied. Recorded as symmetric; the page should stop using it in one direction only.**
  ⚠️⚠️ **THE DE-SPEC ITEM IS UPGRADED IN STATUS BUT NOT RESOLVED, AND THIS IS THE MOST IMPORTANT BOOKKEEPING DECISION OF THE RUN.** On **2026-08-21** this page logged the Rubin-Ultra 8-Hi claim from DAMNANG as an **EXPLICITLY UNCONFIRMED rumour with the author's own disclaimer attached**, deliberately unscored. It is now: **stated as its own view by a Buy-rated house** (UBS, 08-25: *“we believe Nvidia has moved to HBM4 8-Hi from HBM4E 12-Hi for VR/R300… this is not affecting overall demand (more SiPs, less content per SiP)”*); **pushed further by Mizuho/SemiAnalysis** (08-24: OEMs could cap content at 4-8Hi, 16-20Hi and hybrid bonding “uncertain for future gens”, GOOGL cut TPUv8 HBM3e to 8-Hi); and **indirectly supported by Samsung's own DS spokesperson**, who confirms customers are evaluating lower stack heights *because of supply constraint, not weak demand*. 🔴 **AND CONTRADICTED OUTRIGHT BY Morgan Stanley's 08-24 bottom-up HBM model, which still carries Rubin R200 at HBM4 12-Hi and Rubin Ultra at HBM4E 12-Hi.** ➤ **STATUS: unconfirmed rumour → CONTESTED BROKER VIEW. It is still NOT a confirmed platform spec. The `HBM4` Sinal was NOT moved. First test: UBS's own named catalyst, ~18-Sep-2026 pricing-negotiation finalisation.**
  ⚠️⚠️ **A SECOND CONTRADICTION OPENED AND LEFT OPEN — THE 2027 BIT-SUPPLY NUMBER.** UBS (08-25) says industry undersupply “should WORSEN” on a 60-70% fulfilment rate; Samsung IR independently says the same on the same day and extends it “after twenty eight.” **Morgan Stanley (08-25) models DRAM bit supply +32%/+38% in 2026/27** (up from 29%/33%, vs 24% in 2025) on **$53bn/$70bn of DRAM WFE and 283/493kwpm of greenfield — with the biggest 2027 greenfield revisions AT SAMSUNG AND HYNIX** — and adds that SPE companies imply **$75bn+** of 2027 DRAM WFE against MS's own $70bn, i.e. MS thinks the risk to its number is to the UPSIDE. **Neither side adopted; recorded as the most testable disagreement on the page.**
  🔴 **THE NET-NEW COMPETITIVE BEAR IS THE COMPETITOR'S OWN WORDS, AND IT IS THE ONE THAT COULD BREAK THE STRUCTURAL LEG: Samsung says it will hold mid-to-high-30s% HBM share in 2027 and buy it with capacity “REGARDLESS OF THE PROFITABILITY,” because “we had to be the major supplier for HBM” to keep deep relationships with NVIDIA, AMD, Broadcom and AWS.** An explicitly profit-insensitive share push by the #1 DRAM maker is the mechanism that would undo the “memory is no longer cyclical” discipline argument. ⚠️ **An internal inconsistency inside Samsung's own call is logged and NOT reconciled: “mid-thirty to high-thirty percent” (all-HBM answer) vs “at least mid- to high-twenty percent” (HBM4E answer ~5 minutes later).** ➤ It lands the same day as the Jefferies/Sherman claim that Hynix is *late* at NVIDIA — possibly the same event seen from opposite ends.
  **ARCHIVE BACKFILL — DAMNANG, 8 pieces (2026-03-18 → 2026-06-08), filed as 8 compact bullets under `## Debate / thesis`, all marked HISTORICAL.** Net-new to this page: **the H³/HBF teardown** (1-2 order latency gap unarguable; the 40MB SRAM + DRAM + FTL controller “makes the total system anything but cheap”; read-only workload assumption; conclusion = complement not substitute) — **now independently corroborated from the commercial side by Micron management via Mizuho, 2026-08-09**; **the SK hynix NA regional president's “no longer cyclical” framing** (with the author's own bullish-bias disclosure and his ADR passage explicitly flagged as speculation, not guidance); **the CAPTIVE-RATIO dashboard**; **the CXMT double-edged sword** (“the stronger the HBM moat, the lower the perceived urgency to invest in the 3D DRAM transition”) plus CXMT scale (~720k wafers/quarter, ~16nm G4 with no EUV); **the hybrid-bonding standing** (SK hynix most conservative on MR-MUF; JEDEC 720→775μm pushed adoption out a generation; engineers say HBM5 may be the realistic scale point) — whose 2026 relevance is that a **de-spec that stalls 16-20Hi EXTENDS the MR-MUF advantage**; and **321L QLC leadership with the author's own caveat that SK hynix is “approaching the limits” of layer-count efficiency**.

- **2026-07-28** — **/wiki-ingest · Q2 2026 PRINT (2026-07-28) — new-print reset.** New `## Current state` for the June quarter (the Q1 2026 block plus all the standing capacity/HBM4/MR-MUF/packaging deep-dive material is retained inline below it); archived the Q1 2026 intra-quarter window (Apr 23 → Jul 28, **130 lines** — the largest window on the wiki) into this Changelog; opened a fresh Q2 2026 window; rolled the management-evolution table forward (Q2 2025 drops off, **Q2 2026 added**). **Superseded per the thesis-drift rule** — prior values preserved: *Current state* previously led with **Q1 2026 revenue ₩52.6tn (+60% q/q, +198% y/y), operating profit ₩37.6tn, OP margin 72%, net profit ₩40.3tn, EBITDA ₩41.3tn, net cash ₩35tn.** **What printed: revenue ₩79.3tn vs ~₩83.9tn expected and operating profit ₩60.54tn vs ₩64.2tn consensus — the first headline miss of the cycle.** ⚠️ **The qualifier matters as much as the miss: local Korean brokers had already cut OP to ~₩60tn and Jefferies' Jeff Kim was at ₩62tn**, so the print is in line with the most recent numbers and only misses the stale screen consensus. Margin structure intact — **GM 83%, OM 76%, EBITDA margin 81%** — and the *level* still rose sequentially off Q1. **The margin trajectory this page has tracked (41% → 47% → 58% → 72%) therefore breaks its ascent at 76%.** **Cause of the shortfall, per Jefferies: the HBM4 ramp is late** — HBM4 shipments did begin in Q2 at customer-required speeds and **1cnm HBM4E samples went to a major customer in 1H**, but the ramp slipped relative to plan. Other management detail logged: **DRAM Q2 bits +HSD with ASP +30% q/q, 2026 bit growth guided mid-20s%, Q3 bits +10% server-focused**; **SOCAMM2 fully commercialised on 1C**; demand **broadening as AI evolves into agentic form** across HBM/server DRAM/eSSD; **PC and smartphone softness explicitly attributed to difficulty securing memory volumes, not weak end-demand.** **Two developments change the shape of the debate rather than the numbers.** **(1) The [[NVDA]] anchor is being re-read as a pricing constraint.** Jefferies expects the miss to fuel the view that **"Hynix has been less aggressive than [[SAMSUNG]] on pricing given its commitments to NVDA on HBM"** — i.e. the supplier-differentiation moat this page underwrites may also be a price ceiling. **(2) Capex is stepping up ~80% to the high-₩40tn range and is being pulled in, with no buyback despite large net cash** (Culpium; Jefferies) — landing in the same week Jefferies research published **"HAS MEMORY PEAKED?": price hikes tracking 15-20% vs 25-30% expected, memory prices peaking ~1H27, and 2027 consensus requiring ~US$2tn of US AI capex to be achievable**, with **HBM4 costs jumping to US$31-32/GB**. That is the bear case in one line — peak-margin cash committed to capacity into a forecast price peak. It is also consistent with management's own long-standing argument that ~70% OP margins are "not high enough" to fund the greenfield/hybrid-bonding step-up. **Tape context (not a company event, but it dominated the reaction):** on 2026-07-27 the **KOSPI fell 10.7% with a sell sidecar and the year's eighth circuit breaker, Samsung -12%, SK Hynix -13%**, driven by **CXMT's IPO debut at +466% — China's second-largest IPO ever — "making the memory threat feel suddenly very real"** plus a live-Fed-hike scare (~40% odds priced). On the print the stock opened **-6% pre-market and recovered to -2.3%**, while **[[MU]] fell a further ~4% after hours on top of -8.9% intraday**. Also logged: an unverified @zerohedge claim that SK Hynix slumped below a US IPO price — **flagged, not adopted**, as it is uncorroborated elsewhere in this ingest; and a Korean-press exclusive (@jukan05) that **LPDDR6 mass production starts in 2H with Xiaomi as first customer**, which sits in tension with the same-day comment that PC/mobile volumes are being rationed. Cross-filed [[MU]], [[SAMSUNG]], [[NVDA]], [themes/hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md), [themes/china-export](themes/china-export.md).

| 07-27 | **Bernstein · Mark Li / Stacy Rasgon / Edward Hou / Yipin Cai — "Global Memory, NVIDIA & Broadcom: Quick thoughts on strategic partnerships" (PRIMARY PDF, 16pp; ingested 2026-08-11)** | supply | **bull** | 🔴 **THE PRIMARY OF THE DESK RELAY IMMEDIATELY BELOW — ingested two weeks late (the file was locked on P: for three consecutive runs). Everything the relay carried was verified and is NOT repeated here; what follows is what the relay left out.** **① THE RATING MARK, absent from the relay: Outperform, PT KRW 3,300,000** (px KRW1,781,000 at the 24-Jul close, +542% relative performance), on **6.2x forward 5Q-8Q EPS of KRW536,158** — the same 6.2x multiple Bernstein applies to [[SAMSUNG]], so the relative call between the two Korean names is pure EPS, not multiple. **EPS: KRW60,341 (2025A) → 395,677 (2026E) → 568,862 (2027E)**, i.e. 29.5x / 4.5x / 3.1x reported P/E — a 3.1x 2027 P/E is the sharpest statement of the "market does not believe these earnings" problem this page tracks. **② THE DEAL DETAIL THE RELAY COMPRESSED TO "$500B+": the LOIs cover memory AND "a 2GW VERA RUBIN DSX AI FACTORY PLANNED TO BE ONLINE FOR SK TELECOM IN 2027."** ⚠️ **That is a dated, sized, named-offtaker datapoint and it was missing from this page — a 2GW facility for the affiliate telco, live in 2027, is both an [[NVDA]] revenue line and a captive demand sink for SK hynix's own HBM. The memory leg is described as "long-term technical developments & stable supply of next-gen AI memory (HBM4 & later?)" — the question mark is Bernstein's — "to enable SK hynix to 'expand the foundation for growth.'"** 🔴 **③ NET-NEW AND NOT ON THIS PAGE IN ANY FORM: "SK Group reportedly will ADDITIONALLY collaborate with other global tech companies for memory supply worth US$250B over the next 5 years."** ⚠️ **Sourcing is "reportedly" with two press links, not disclosure — carry it as a press report. But if it stands, the group's contracted memory book is $500B+ (NVDA) plus $250B (others) across five years, and the second leg has no named counterparties at all. That is the single largest unattributed demand commitment on this page.** **④ The rating logic across the complex, which is where Bernstein differs from the Street: Outperform on Samsung, SK hynix AND [[MU]], Underperform on [[KIOXIA]] — and the discriminator is explicitly the China threat by product. "We however worry about the long-term threat from China in NAND and thus rate KIOXIA Underperform. The threat is much lower in DRAM as CHINA EVENTUALLY WILL FIND IT DIFFICULT TO COMPETE IN DRAM MARKET WITHOUT EUV."** ➜ **That is a litho-access argument, not a capacity argument, and it is the cleanest available test of the CXMT debate on this page: it says CXMT's wafer ramp is not the binding question — its node ceiling is. Hold it against the CXMT capacity marks already here (UBS 240K→466K wpm; JPM 16% capacity / 11% bit share by 28E off a reported 600K wfpm ambition), which measure the thing Bernstein says does not decide the outcome.** **⑤ Industry frame: consensus memory revenue ~US$0.9T (CY2026) → US$1.3T (CY2027) → US$1.3T (CY2028) — the relay carried the $1.3T; the CY26 base and the FLATNESS from '27 to '28 are new, and the flatness is the more interesting half. Exhibit 3 splits it across Samsung Memory / SK hynix / Micron / SanDisk / KIOXIA / CXMT / YMTC, with CXMT and YMTC on Bernstein's own estimates.** **⑥ Risks as stated: "an earlier end of favorable pricing environment, which can be a result of weaker demand or higher supply," investor sentiment/valuation, and China's memory progress "especially in NAND."** (Bernstein · Mark Li / Stacy Rasgon / Edward Hou / Yipin Cai / Alrick Shaw / Arpad von Nemes / Eva Zhang, 2026-07-27; first published 26 Jul 2026 21:30 UTC) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/20260726_Bernstein_NVDA_Global_Memory-_NVIDIA_-_Broadcom-_Quick_thoughts_on_stra.html) |

| 07-27 | 🆕 **DAMNANG (Substack) — "After CXMT's Listing, Do the Memory Three Have Further to Fall?"** · ⚠️ **INDEPENDENT AUTHOR, NOT A BROKER; ingested 2026-08-21, ~25 days late** | competicao | **bull** | **THE MOST SYSTEMATIC TEST OF THE CXMT BEAR CASE THIS PAGE CARRIES — it takes each circulating claim and checks it against the disclosed data rather than asserting a view. ⚠️ The price marks are the 07-27 listing day and are HISTORICAL; the value is the framework.** ① **THE "CXMT SELLS 30% BELOW" CLAIM IS AN APPLES-TO-ORANGES ARTEFACT:** it compares company-wide average ASPs, and roughly **two thirds of CXMT's revenue is low-priced MOBILE memory**, which drags the company average "for reasons that have nothing to do with competitiveness." Like-for-like the gap is **5-10% per CXMT's own IPO prospectus — and in SERVER products the SIGN FLIPS**: in July 2026 a **64GB DDR5 server module built on CXMT chips sold for RMB18,999 in Chinese online retail vs RMB18,595 for the same specification on Samsung/SK hynix chips — CXMT 2.2% HIGHER** — and in the contract market CXMT was reported to have **quoted above Samsung's supply price of US$1,240 per module**. ② **IT HAS NO ROOM TO UNDERCUT:** at its current node CXMT spends **>30% more to produce the same amount of memory**, its **chips are ~40% larger in area**, and its yield falls **short of the 85-90% the industry treats as standard**. ③ **THE "8% SHARE" IS A REVENUE NUMBER, AND REVENUE RISES WITH PRICE:** CXMT booked **US$7.31bn of DRAM revenue in 1Q26 = 7.7% of the world market**, but it puts **13% of global DRAM WAFER STARTS into production** and converts them into only **6% of memory CAPACITY** — "half of what goes in disappears on the way from wafers to capacity." ④ **ITS GROWTH SO FAR IS PRICE, NOT VOLUME:** 1Q26 revenue **+71% y/y**, but measured q/q **memory capacity shipped grew only +11% while ASP rose +57%**. ⑤ **THE NODE GAP HAS STOPPED CLOSING:** CXMT took **18-24 months** per generation from its third to its fourth, **its fifth has already run past 24 months** and slipped toward 2H26, while the incumbents also turn a generation in ~24 months and are ramping 1c and moving to HBM4 → **the ~three-generation gap holds**. It did bring **16nm-class DDR5 to volume on DUV multi-patterning with no EUV option**, so the constraint is development PACE, not capability. ⑥ **HBM IS A PRICE-POSITIVE FOR THE INCUMBENTS, NOT A PRICE-NEGATIVE:** CXMT allocates **<2% of wafer starts to HBM**, its HBM3 schedule slipped on thermal problems and **8-high combined yield is estimated ~25% vs 70-90% at the incumbents**; because stacking consumes **~3x the wafer area per GB of ordinary DDR5**, if CXMT converts 10% of its DRAM lines to HBM the ordinary DRAM capacity off those lines **falls to one third**. The same mechanic on the incumbents' side is this page's own tightness explanation, now quantified: **in 2026 HBM consumes 22% of global DRAM wafer capacity while producing only 9% of the memory capacity — "that gap is the underlying reason ordinary DRAM is short today."** ⑦ **IPO-FUNDED SUPPLY IS A 2028 EVENT AT THE EARLIEST** (proceeds → equipment orders → production = 18-24 months). On the circulating "CXMT doubles DRAM capacity by 2030" story (said to have come out of a pre-listing broker conference call — the author states he **cannot verify it**, and warns that pieces "written by press outlets and institutions without the technical background" are circulating as established fact and reaching share prices): **what doubles is WAFERS, not capacity — 13% of wafer starts / 6% of capacity doubled still lands near 12% on a capacity basis, while DRAM demand over those four years grows far faster** → he reads the story as **good news for Chinese equipment makers (Naura, AMEC) rather than bad news for the memory three.** ⑧ 🔴 **THE SK HYNIX-SPECIFIC CONCLUSION, WHICH IS THE ITEM FOR THIS PAGE:** *"SK hynix has its earnings concentrated in HBM. CXMT cannot enter that segment, so of the three SK hynix is the most insulated from it."* The offsetting half is the exposure this page already owns: *"those earnings ride the capital spending plans of the handful of customers buying AI accelerators. **What moves SK hynix shares is that, not CXMT.**"* (For contrast, in the same piece [[SAMSUNG]] "is hit first and hardest" because it sells the largest share of its output to Chinese smartphone and PC makers and meets CXMT's commodity mobile/PC DRAM competition first, with a 4.32x forward multiple already discounting it; [[MU]] "has the least left to lose in China" — mainland revenue fell from ~11% of total to the 7% range after the 2023 purchase ban and it exited the Chinese server-memory business in Oct-2025.) ⑨ **The listing-day marks, HISTORICAL:** CXMT opened **RMB49.50 against an IPO price of RMB8.66 (+472%)**, a market cap of **RMB3.31tn (~US$489bn) — the most valuable company listed on the A-share market** — reaching **RMB54.65 by midday (+531%, ~RMB3.7tn)**; **Samsung Electronics and SK hynix were both weak the same day, with foreign investors net sellers**. Forward multiples that day: **CXMT ~30x this year's expected earnings, Samsung 4.32x, SK hynix 4.69x, Micron 6.42x.** ⚠️ **Nomura's CNY116 initiation is already logged on the 07-26 row above — the net-new here is the reverse-engineering of it: RMB5.80 of 2028 EPS at 20x → RMB7.76tn market cap, which backs out 2028 net profit of RMB388bn, "3.9 times what the market expects CXMT to earn this year, and more than Micron earns at the peak of a cycle."** **His path call, for the record:** the SMIC precedent (opened RMB95 in Jul-2020 +246%, never traded above that price again, −48% ten weeks later and −32% a year later, but never broke its IPO price and was +136% above it a year on); he expects **day one to mark the high**, a possible correction around the **first lockup release in January 2027**, and no break of the IPO price. **On the complex: "the recent memory correction is MORE LIKELY TO END after the CXMT listing than to continue"** — further downside needs memory earnings to turn down or AI demand to reverse outright, and neither price nor volume shows it. [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/13_After_CXMTs_Listing_Do_the_Memory_Three_Have_Further_to_Fall_.html) |

| 07-24 | SemiAnalysis "Can AMD break the CUDA Moat? AMD Advancing AI 2026" | produto | **mixed** | **The HBM4 pin-speed bake-off, the 2027 HBM-price call, and the de-spec that offsets it — all read off the buyer's BOM.** **(1) Pin speed / binning:** AMD's MI455X carries **12 HBM4 stacks / 432GB / 23.3 TB/s** vs Nvidia Rubin and Google at **8 stacks / 288GB**, Rubin **22 TB/s** — a near-tie despite AMD's **50% wider bus**, because AMD runs **7.6 Gbps** pin speed vs Nvidia's **10.7 Gbps (40% faster)**. "Nvidia aggressively raised its targeted HBM4 pin speeds last year, well above the original JEDEC HBM4 specification… precisely driven by the need to make up for the memory bandwidth deficit to AMD MI455X." That "was challenging for memory suppliers to keep up with. Memory suppliers had to rework their HBM4 to deliver the spec Nvidia was targeting. This also pushed out upstream output for Rubin **but as of today these issues have been finally resolved**" — and **Nvidia must use a much higher-quality bin than AMD**. AMD + Nvidia are the only two shipping HBM4 this year. Net for the #1 HBM supplier: HBM4 is **two markets** (easy 7.6 Gbps AMD bin vs premium 10.7 Gbps Nvidia bin), so the differentiator shifts toward **yield on the top bin** and the quality/price spread between suppliers — a share/mix question, not a volume one. Cross-check, held carefully: this page's own SemiAnalysis datapoint has SK hynix's **HBM4 engineering samples at ~10 Gbps with lower jitter / stronger package execution than Samsung's** ("Memory Mania," 02-06), just under the 10.7 Gbps Rubin target — **SemiAnalysis names no bin winner and neither do we.** Also the first explicit statement on the page that the Rubin HBM4 upstream-output delay is *behind* the chain. **(2) Bull — 2027 pricing:** expects "a significant increase in HBM pricing in 2027 as memory suppliers move to match the margins they now earn on commodity DRAM," strong enough that AMD "can't pass the full memory increase on to its customers" → **gross-margin drag on the MI455X package**; "HBM, DRAM, and NAND prices alike have increased dramatically since our baseline in mid-2025." Direct external support for management's refusal to commit to HBM-DDR margin parity (roadshow 07-06). **(3) Bear — de-spec:** original 2025 MI455X BOM carried **138TB of DRAM (LPDDR5X + DDR5 MRDIMM)**; current estimate has **LPDDR5X removed entirely** (the up-to-**1TB** per-accelerator direct-attached LPDDR tier is gone on tight supply) plus a significant DDR5 RDIMM de-spec — "**much of the DRAM price increases are offset by aggressive de-specs.**" [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_24_ENC_Can_AMD_break_the_CUDA_Moat_AMD_Advancing_AI_2026_email.html) |
| 07-21 | SemiAnalysis "Meta's Infrastructure Team Needs A Culture Reset" | demanda | **bear** | **A quantified HBM-content cut at one of AMD's two largest GPU customers — the sharpest instance of the de-spec risk.** Meta's custom MI450/MI455 variant **halves the compute silicon (8 dies → 4) and cuts HBM from 12 stacks to 6 per package**, and **downgrades the HBM4 itself from 12-Hi to 8-Hi**. SemiAnalysis argues the design "is going to nuke AMD's volume at Meta because TBD will vastly prefer Rubin if the half MI455 design is chosen," so the memory read is **doubly negative: fewer HBM stacks per package on a program that may itself shrink**. Logged for this page because the swing factor for 2027 HBM revenue is **bits per accelerator**, which most models hold flat — and because it is the uncovered (non-LTA) portion of the book where content cuts bite. | 07-25 | Susquehanna · Mehdi Hosseini (Technology Hardware weekly, Jul 19-25) | supply | mixed | **"US Negotiating Leverage Bears Fruit" — the sharpest articulation yet of the political mechanism behind Korean memory supply, and it has a bearish tail.** SIG (Neutral, ₩1,781,000) argues the US administration's negotiating strategy is working: **Korean memory suppliers are now expected to unveil major US memory-fab investment plans** (Korean president in Silicon Valley for meetings and an AI summit; Tom's Hardware reporting Samsung and SK Hynix set to announce large deals with leading US tech firms). Hosseini says the leverage rests on two points: **(1) the majority of Korean suppliers' memory demand comes from US-based customers, >50% of revenue mix**; and **(2) the rise of CXMT and YMTC as a viable supply alternative**. Both Chinese makers are **currently barred from supplying US-based OEMs** and both sit on the **Pentagon's Section 1260H list**, with **YMTC also on the Commerce Entity List** — so the implicit threat is that Washington can "unlock" that supply. **SIG's sizing, net-new: CXMT and YMTC reach 17% and 25% of DRAM and NAND supply respectively by YE28.** The consequence he draws is the part that matters for the cycle debate on this page: **new US greenfield memory fabs cannot come online before 2029** given construction and tooling lead times, so **in the interim CXMT/YMTC "could still offer a valuable short-term remedy to a tight memory supply-demand balance"** — the US "essentially killing two birds with one stone" by easing the imbalance and reducing memory-price inflation. Read as a **policy-driven downside scenario to the pricing curve** that is independent of demand: the tightness that underwrites current DRAM/NAND ASPs is partly a function of an export-control regime that the same administration has an incentive to relax once the fab commitments are banked. Cross-ref [SAMSUNG.md](SAMSUNG.md), [MU.md](MU.md), [themes](themes/). (Susquehanna · Mehdi Hosseini / Bastien Faucon-Morin, 2026-07-25) |

Though, yes, they're the LEAD FOR HBM4."** (@ParadisLabs, 08-18.) **The demand backdrop: NAND spot +~5% w/w, +11.6% over three weeks (DRAMeXchange), attributed to Vera Rubin expansion; much of NVDA's NAND is pre-secured via LTAs so the pressure lands on spot.** ⚠️ Corpus post, not a disclosure; "CMX" unverified against an NVDA document. 🔴 **Read: qualified-without-volume on the fastest-growing NAND socket is the same pattern this page has tracked on the other side of the ledger for MU in HBM — and it is a share question, not a price question.** **(2) 🔴 CUSTOMER CONCENTRATION IS SHIFTING: "SK hynix's No. 2 customer closes in on Nvidia in 1H26 sales" (DIGITIMES headline, 2026-08-18)** ⚠️ **headline only — the customer is NOT named in what was read, and the body was not read.** **Why it matters and why it should be chased: if the No.2 buyer is approaching NVDA in SK Hynix revenue, the HBM customer mix is diversifying toward custom-ASIC programs — exactly what Meritz's planned 2028 HBM split implies ([[AVGO]] 35–40bn Gb ahead of NVDA's 30bn Gb, with [[GOOG]] at 20bn Gb, via @jukan05, 08-18). Flagged as the highest-value follow-up on this page.** **Tape: Hynix −50bps on 08-17 then down mid-to-high-single-digits overnight; Samsung −2.2%; Kioxia −7.6%; KOSPI −1.3/−1.55%. | 07-23/25 | Trade press via Susquehanna weekly (TrendForce, BusinessKorea) | capital | neutral | Two items on the **Intel Ohio thread** the page already tracks, and they conflict: TrendForce reports **"SK hynix reportedly eyed as potential partner to operate Intel's Ohio fab after denying acquisition plans,"** while BusinessKorea runs **"SK Hynix Denies Intel Ohio Plant Deal."** Consistent with the 07-23 read already logged on [INTC.md](INTC.md) that both companies dismissed the *acquisition* rumour "but the story may not end there — new collaboration paths are reportedly emerging beyond a potential sale." Net: an **operating/partnership structure is the live version of this story, not a purchase**; unresolved. Also in the same weekly: **Nanya Tech sees the memory market reaching a record ~US$800bn in 2026, nearly half of total semiconductor revenue** — a competitor's TAM claim, useful as an upper-bound marker on how far the industry is extrapolating the current pricing cycle. **SK Hynix reports Tue 2026-07-28, 8:00 PM ET.** (TrendForce / BusinessKorea, 2026-07-23, via Susquehanna · Hosseini weekly, 2026-07-25) |

**Quarter synthesis:** with HBM demand and ASP already confirmed by the flow, the debate has shifted entirely to the multiple — cycle durability (GS bull, Jefferies "out-earn the hyperscalers") vs HBM commoditization once 2-3 suppliers qualify (ex-Micron VP). New from 06-27: (1) **Samsung/SK Hynix "huge AI spending push"** (JPM) — the upcoming CAPEX disclosures are framed as the near-term catalyst for the sector, following MU's template of $100B LTA RPO + 16 SCAs; (2) **BofA ASML Flow & Tell** confirms ASML as the DRAM-exposure proxy, with US LO inflows already building ahead of the Korean memory capex announcements. **06-29: Korea $880B AI mega-plan confirmed** (Bloomberg/JPM) — government-mandated Yongyin pull-forward (7–12 yrs) is the most explicit government backstop on memory capacity in the cycle; Samsung + SK Hynix combined $1.3T over 10 years. **06-30: figure revised sharply higher within 48 hours — Korea AI mega-plan now $3.1tn (JPM · Jay Kwon via Joshua Meyers)**, ~3.5x the 06-29 framing: Samsung + SK Group jointly announced KRW4,755tn (~US$3.1tn) through 2040 for semis/AI datacenters/robotics, doubling installed Korean DRAM capacity within 5 years, with the same 7-12yr Yongin pull-forward now sized against a much larger capex base; JPM sees a $12-20tn cumulative revenue opportunity for Korean memory makers, and estimates 60-70% of the $3.1tn flows to front-end wafer spend (bullish SPE/power-infra/EPC read-through). Hynix +3% on the news (Rothschild, 06-30). CXMT capacity confirmed insufficient for domestic demand (IPO prospectus), neutralizing the near-term China oversupply concern. **07-01: the stock has not followed the capex-plan news** — SK Hynix -2% (Jefferies) to -3.4% (Rothschild) even as US semi-cap beneficiaries (AMD/AMAT/LRCX/KLAC) hit 12-month highs on the same narrative; JPM's "hyperscaler curse" framing attributes the disconnect to NPS rebalancing and a June DRAM-export-data dip, not a change in fundamentals. Read-through support from Jefferies/KB's Samsung note (Jeff Kim, 07-01): memory shortage 2+ years, DRAM +308%/NAND +256% in 2026, memory's AI-capex share 14%→50% by 2027. **06-29/30:** KIS/@jukan05 details that **SK Hynix's LTA carries a floor with no ceiling** (vs Samsung/Micron), keeping full upside while locking a floor — and that customers, not suppliers, first proposed the 2028+ LTAs (shortage-runs-long signal). Offsetting: a 17-plaintiff DRAM price-fixing class action (N.D. Cal., 06-30) is a new legal overhang, and SemiAnalysis flags CXMT as clearly the No. 4 DRAM player expanding on strong cash flow (low-end competitive creep, not yet a server/HBM threat). **Folded in from two SemiAnalysis deep-dives** (Memory Mania 02-06; Scaling the Memory Wall 08-11, backfilled 2026-06-30): the Rubin HBM supply split (SK hynix ~60% vs Samsung ~30%/Micron ~0%) is the most concrete quantification yet of SK hynix's HBM4/Rubin lead, reinforced by the historical precedent of SK hynix having taken HBM3 share from Samsung outright and its lower-risk 1b-node choice for HBM4. The Hanmi/Hanwha TC-bonding tool dispute (fall-2024 diversification attempt, Apr-2025 Hanmi field-service pullback, incompatible ASMPT tools, placating re-order) is a concrete instance of the page's generic "HBM tool dependency" risk and shows the fragility is real, not hypothetical — though it evidently didn't dent SK hynix's 2025-26 output or share. **07-07:** UBS's SpaceX/SPCX-initiation "Terafab" note adds a long-dated competitive read-through — Terafab explicitly targets memory, and while UBS sees limited incentive for incumbents to license IP, a new at-scale memory supplier (if successful) could pressure Korean incumbents and prompt Korean US front-end (DRAM) investment; a monitor item, not a near-term thesis-mover. **07-08:** Morgan Stanley (S&T) — the ADR listing catalyst previously flagged with a date now has full mechanics: ~**US$28.2bn / ~2.5% TSO**, 1 ADR = 0.1 common share, **trades 07-10**, with MS expecting a **~5-10% ADR premium** to the Seoul line (below TSMC's ~18%), one-way fungibility (the ~2.5% headroom + SK Square's ≥20% floor cap further primary issuance), and index inflows led by **SMH ~$3.8bn** / MSCI ~$1.9bn (Nasdaq-100 near-term inclusion unlikely on the ~$30bn ADR mcap). The ~$28.2bn size is consistent with Jefferies' ~$29bn (07-07) — a rounding/pricing-window difference, not a supersession; net-net the S&T note quantifies the flow/premium mechanics behind the "buy-ADR/sell-Seoul" trade the desks had been recommending, but is technical listing color rather than a fundamental thesis-mover. **07-08/07-09:** into the ADR debut, demand signals stayed firm — @illyquid reports the **$28bn ADR bookbuild closing Wednesday US time with the book already covered multiple times** (MS flagged its "SKHY ADR FAQ" as helpful), corroborating Reuters' >7x oversubscription, and JEF (Madeleine Falkiner) included Hynix in its EU/Asia semis bus tours. Industry pricing/demand corroboration held: Bernstein's Memory tracker (Newman & Li, 07-08 — mild MoM contract-price rises, major 2QCY26 increase still indicated, server demand absorbing supply vs some consumer demand destruction), Goldman's ~$1T 2027 hyperscaler capex (~40-50% to memory, @ParadisLabs), @QQ_Timmy's ~50%-over-two-quarters move (AI broadening HBM→general DRAM/NAND) and BofA's HBM $/rack ~quintupling Blackwell→Rubin (via @firstadopter); Newman also published a Memory-LTA deep dive + structure call (07-09). Offsetting: CXMT's ~CNY29.5bn (~US$4.1bn) STAR Market IPO (China's biggest 2026 IPO) funds 17nm/DDR5/HBM with HBM3 mass production targeted end-2026 (still in qual — @zephyr_z9) — low-end competitive creep now reaching into HBM3. **07-10: the ADR listing catalyst RESOLVED — SKHY priced at $149, raising ~US$26.5bn**, the largest-ever first-time share sale by a foreign issuer on US markets (past Alibaba), 7x oversubscribed with anchor allocations cut on demand and a ~3% ADR premium (Reuters/Bloomberg/FT). The realized ~$26.5bn came slightly below the ~$28-29bn marketing size, and the ~3% premium landed below the ~5-10% MS range / 15-20% some desks flagged — but the record-setting foreign-IPO demand validates the US-investor bid thesis; Cramer expects sell-side number-raises Monday. Offsetting on the same day: Bloomberg profiled CXMT building a top-to-bottom China memory supply chain to challenge the big three (structural low-end threat, long-dated). **Two primary-source management datapoints now anchor the durability leg:** the **SK Hynix ADS roadshow (07-06)** put management's own words on the LTA re-rating thesis — LTAs are margin-focused (explicitly *not* Micron's floor-price approach), 3-5yr with penalty provisions (vs old unenforceable 1yr gentleman's agreements) → lower volatility → higher P/E; >50% of 2028-30 capacity to be LTA-covered (signing before year-end 2026); proceeds fund Cheongju (₩19tn/PNT7) + Yongin (₩21tn) + EUV, with a ₩100tn cash target and a Q3-call shareholder-return measure; total wafer cap ~2x by 2030 but customer demand bigger; HBM-DDR margin parity explicitly NOT committed; CXMT ~20% wafer / only ~6-7% bits. The **Chairman Chey interview (06-10)** framed the AI cycle as a structural break ("not cyclical anymore" because customers proposed the LTAs), with the TSMC-base-die partnership and "doesn't compete with customers" as durable moats, a 5-year capacity double (customers want 4-6x), and SK Group's ~$1T/10yr AI-DC plan (~15GW Korea + ~5GW ex-Korea, ~$35B already in US). Against these, Bernstein's "Memory LTA effectiveness" note (07-09) is the skeptic's bound — the ~$3.33B industry deposit secured so far is <1% (~0.6%) of what an LTA book would need to protect, and coverage tops out at ~50-67% of the market — expecting SK Hynix/Samsung to announce *more* deposits but doubting they reach a level that "de-risks" for investors. **07-14:** Mirae Asset (Young-gun Kim, "Take a broader view") maintains Buy and **the street-high TP W4,200,000** (tying Jefferies) even while cutting 2026/27 earnings ~12% — 2026F OP W266,817bn lands just *below* consensus W275,793bn — framing the pullback as an attractive entry with the 2Q26/ADR-optimism unwind already priced in; the note reinforces the durability/re-rating leg (LTAs ~50% of revenue, DRAM spot up 40+ straight days) while widening the page's PT dispersion to ~W2m (W4.2m Mirae/Jefferies high vs JPM W2.221m low). _(Also corrected this run: the Bernstein 07-06 SK Hynix PT is KRW 3,300,000, not the stale body-figure 1,150,000 previously logged — see Changelog.)_ **07-13→15: a sharp ASP-miss selloff, quickly rebutted and reversed.** The KIS/local 2Q26 OP-preview (W60.4tn, ~8% below the W65tn consensus — the miss a *mix* effect, higher HBM revenue mix → lower blended ASP hike, not weakness) triggered SK Hynix's **biggest one-day drop on record in Seoul** (Bloomberg, "deepening Korea selloff"; @zerohedge -4.5%; @citrini's W1,850 technical line) — but the fundamental case held: **BofA (Simon Woo, 07-13, "DRAM ASPs look better than concerns")** called the correction overdone (conventional DRAM ASP +50-60% QoQ, Korea exports +193% YoY first-10-days-July, DRAM spot rallying since June), framing Korea's soft 2Q ASP as a 2Q-specific base effect rather than a 3Q trend, and the stock **rebounded ~+27% the next session to near all-time highs as the US ADR premium reasserted** (Barron's/@firstadopter/The_AI_Investor; @JulianKlymochko "$1tn company trading like a meme stonk," 3.8x fwd; @jimcramer "how did all of tech become hostage to SK Hynix"). Net-net the whipsaw was flow/ETF/sentiment-driven, not a thesis change. On the durability/supply leg, **Ray Wang (SemiAnalysis, on CNBC via @QQ_Timmy, 07-14)** sharpened the share-loss rebuttal — in a fully-sold-out HBM market, Samsung gaining share need not come at Hynix's expense because the bottleneck is **wafer capacity, not demand** — and SK hynix put concrete execution behind the capacity pull-forward: **Yongin Y1 equipment orders begun with the Phase-1 pilot line pulled forward to Feb-2027** and price-negotiations moved up a quarter (@jukan05, 07-14; @trendforce: supply gap could last beyond 2030). The residual bear item is long-dated China capacity (@EugeneNg/@zephyr_z9, 07-15: 2030E DRAM capacity — big-3 ~2x, CXMT ~3x, all-China ~4x). On the ADR, arbitrage between the Korean line and Nasdaq SKHY is "malfunctioning" — **two-way conversion only opens after the July-29 domestic listing** of the ADR-backing shares (@jukan05/KSD, 07-14), a technical explanation for the premium dislocation. Jefferies (Cristina Titu, 07-15) reiterates BUY on both Korean memory names. _(KIS 07-13 and Mirae 07-14 were already ingested on the 2026-07-14 run — not re-added.)_ **Added this run:** the positioning mechanics behind the 07-13 washout now carry named sources — **Citi "Dr Mo" (Apabhai, 07-13)** sized ~$3bn of leveraged-ETF Hynix selling into the close on a report of a 23-Jul earnings miss, and the **Goldman-panel PSI (07-13)** logged the 13-day foreign KOSPI sell streak / 46.6% SEC foreign-ownership (2009 low) / $29bn SKHY ADR overhang — both flow/positioning, not a fundamental change. **Didier's ASML post-2Q call (07-15)** adds an EUV read-through (SK Hynix a rising EUV buyer, Korean memory capex in ASML's backlog). And the **Wells Fargo CXMT deep-dive (07-14)** puts hard numbers (~8% DRAM rev share, HBM 2-4yr behind) on the low-end China threat, consistent with the roadshow's "CXMT ~20% wafer / ~6-7% bits." **07-24 (SemiAnalysis, AMD Advancing AI 2026) — the run's one genuinely new axis, and it is a *content* and *binning* axis, not a price axis.** On price the note is confirmatory but from an unusually valuable seat — the *buyer's* bill of materials: SemiAnalysis expects "a significant increase in HBM pricing in 2027 as memory suppliers move to match the margins they now earn on commodity DRAM," and models it as strong enough that AMD cannot pass it all through and takes a gross-margin hit on the MI455X package. That is a third-party estimate of HBM pricing power sufficient to move a customer's GM — the most direct external support yet for management's refusal (roadshow 07-06) to commit to HBM-to-DDR margin parity, and consistent with the commodity-DRAM-is-more-profitable inversion already on the page (Redburn's 60→50bn Gb-eq HBM-2027 cut). What is new is the offset: the same BOM shows MI455X DRAM content collapsing from an original 2025 estimate of **138TB across LPDDR5X and DDR5 MRDIMM** to a far lower DDR5 RDIMM figure with **LPDDR5X removed entirely** (the up-to-1TB per-accelerator direct-attached LPDDR tier simply gone on tight supply), summarised as "much of the DRAM price increases are offset by aggressive de-specs"; the custom parts are worse — **Meta's MI450/MI455 cuts HBM from 12 stacks to 6 and 12-Hi to 8-Hi**, at one of AMD's two largest GPU customers, on a program the same house says will "nuke AMD's volume at Meta" (SemiAnalysis 07-21/07-24). The marginal risk to 2027 memory revenue has therefore moved from *price* to *bits per accelerator*, and it bites hardest on the ~33-50% of the market the LTA book does not cover (Bernstein's coverage ceiling, 07-09). On differentiation, the **HBM4 pin-speed bake-off** (AMD 7.6 Gbps / 12 stacks / 432GB / 23.3 TB/s vs Rubin 10.7 Gbps / 8 stacks / 288GB / 22 TB/s, Nvidia having pushed above the JEDEC spec deliberately to close the bandwidth gap to AMD, forcing suppliers to rework HBM4 and requiring "a much higher-quality bin") makes HBM4 **two markets rather than one** — shifting the competitive question from wafer capacity toward **yield on the premium bin**, which is a genuinely different lens on this page's core HBM4-allocation swing factor. Two smaller but real reads: the note states the Rubin HBM4 upstream-output delay is now **resolved**, and it juxtaposes usefully with the page's existing "SK hynix HBM4 ES at ~10 Gbps, lower jitter than Samsung" datapoint — though SemiAnalysis names no bin winner and no such claim is made here. Net: price signal ✓ confirms, content and binning signals ⚠ new; no house rating, PT or estimate superseded.

- **2026-07-27** — **/wiki-ingest (Outlook, into the 7/28 AMC print; MS group callback with Shawn Kim 7/30). 4 intra-quarter rows. Nothing superseded.** **The weekend's headline is the NVDA / SK Group $500B+ initiative, and this page carries it with its verification status attached rather than as a booked number.** **What was announced: an [[SKHYNIX]] partnership to SECURE NEXT-GEN MEMORY SUPPLY FOR NVDA AND JOINTLY DEVELOP HBM; SK Telecom building a 2 GW AI datacentre on NVDA Vera Rubin with the FIRST FACILITY ONLINE IN 2027; SK ramping to 15 GW BY 2035; and NVDA investing $1B in Naver.** UBS sizes the SKT leg at **~$70-80B of NVDA revenue** on Vera Rubin plus SK hynix HBM4. **The verification, and it materially changes how much weight the headline carries: The Information reports that NVDA AND SK HAVE ONLY SIGNED LETTERS OF INTENT — 'letters of intent are not worth the paper they're written on' — with NVDA's own precedent being an [[OPENAI]] LOI last September for a 'landmark strategic partnership' that was ABANDONED A FEW MONTHS LATER. Further, the Friday release was 'MOSTLY A REPRISE OF TWO OTHER ANNOUNCEMENTS FROM EARLY JUNE' (the SKT gigawatt-scale AI Cloud on DSX, and the NVDA/SK hynix memory partnership) — and 'the latest update DOUBLED THE SCALE OF THE AI CLOUD IN KOREA TO 2 GIGAWATTS', which is the only genuinely new element. On the $500B itself, 'as to details… such as WHICH COMPANY IS PUTTING UP HOW MUCH, Nvidia isn't saying.'** Bernstein independently confirms the LOI status and that the $500B was announced earlier in the month. **NVDA fell 5% Monday on the headline.** **The strategic nuance worth carrying, from UBS and it cuts against reading this purely as a win: 'we expect NVDA to work even more closely with the memory supply chain to secure sufficient volumes and, TO SOME EXTENT, MAINTAIN ITS ABILITY TO LOCK IN PRICING ON MORE FAVORABLE TERMS — a key factor for NVDA to maintain ~75% gross margin over the long term.'** In other words the same agreement that guarantees SK hynix volume is described by the sell-side as a **pricing-leverage mechanism for the buyer** — which is the standing tension on this page between LTA volume security and the ASP melt-up. Context: memory revenue consensus of **$1.3T in '27/'28**, and this is the third $100B+ Korea-linked announcement of the weekend alongside [[AVGO]]/[[SAMSUNG]] $200B+. Cross-filed [[NVDA]], [[SAMSUNG]], [[AVGO]], [[OPENAI]], [themes/hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md).
- **2026-07-26 (/wiki-ingest — Outlook, weekend batch into the 7/28 AMC print). 3 intra-quarter rows. Nothing superseded.** **(1) The China-supply question got its first proper sell-side anchor: Nomura initiated CXMT (688825 CH) at Buy, TP CNY116, forecasting its global DRAM share going ~10% → ~18% by end-2028F on 40-45% bit-CAGR expansion — which independently corroborates the 17%-of-DRAM-supply-by-YE28 figure Susquehanna put on this page on 07-25, from a house that is long the stock.** Two bears agreeing is weak evidence; a bull and a bear agreeing on the supply number is much stronger, and that is the reason to carry it. CXMT **listed in Shanghai 7/27** at a debut valuation BofA's desk framed as **15-20x FY27 P/E → ~RMB5T (bull 30x → ~RMB9T)** — a multiple of SK hynix's own market cap on roughly 10% share, i.e. **the threat is cost of capital, not technology.** The gate is export control (**MATCH Act**; CXMT equipment localisation ~40%; worst case its **Shanghai capacity — 30-40% of total by 2028F — pushed out**). **(2) The same note contains the strongest structural demand statement now on this page, and it also comes from the competitor's analyst: global memory usage up more than SEVENFOLD over 2026-30F (60%+ bit CAGR) "even after accounting for potential adoption of memory-efficiency technologies (discounting memory usage by 4x)," against industry bit expansion of only 30-40% CAGR — "that could still be insufficient."** A 4x efficiency haircut and demand still outruns supply. **(3) Positioning is genuinely contradictory across desks and is left that way, because the disagreement is the information: MS Prime has HFs recovering ~50% of the capital unwound through June with the Global Memory (MSXXGMEM) L/S ratio at ~7x vs peaks >20x — i.e. re-engaged but nowhere near crowded — and MS's EU client poll has 61% saying now is the time to add to memory longs (Semis the preferred 2H26 TMT sector at 57%). JPM/Schlegel, on a long-term lookback, has global semis at the 98th %ile on both net exposure and L/S ratios (12m easing to 81st %ile). And Jefferies' own desk flow says "memory our most sold sector" on Friday.** The likely reconciliation is the lookback window (MS is comparing to 2026 peaks, JPM to history) but it is not reconciled here. Against all of it, memory was the best-performing sub-sector on the tape last week (**MU +8%, WDC +9%, STX +8%, SNDK +6%**) while the **SOX sat -19% off its June high** — the pricing checks (**+15-20% m/m July PC DRAM contract, implying +30-40% q/q vs TrendForce's +13-18%**) are being paid for even as the sector derates. Cross-filed [[MU]], [[SAMSUNG]], [[SNDK]], [themes/hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md), [themes/china-export](themes/china-export.md).
- **2026-07-25 (/wiki-ingest — Outlook). 2 intra-quarter rows; additive, nothing superseded. First Susquehanna mark carried on this page (Mehdi Hosseini, Neutral, ₩1,781,000).** The material addition is **"US Negotiating Leverage Bears Fruit"** (Technology Hardware weekly, 2026-07-25), which supplies a **policy-driven bear case on memory pricing that is independent of demand** — worth carrying precisely because the page's whole debate has been demand/ASP durability vs multiple. Hosseini's chain: Korean suppliers derive **>50% of revenue from US-based customers**; **CXMT and YMTC are barred from US OEMs** and sit on the **Pentagon's Section 1260H list** (YMTC also on the Commerce **Entity List**); Washington can therefore trade access for US fab commitments, and Korean makers are **expected to announce major US memory-fab investments** (Korean president in Silicon Valley; Tom's Hardware, 07-23). **Net-new sizing: CXMT and YMTC at 17% and 25% of DRAM and NAND supply respectively by YE28.** The kicker: **US greenfield memory fabs cannot come online before 2029**, so in the interim unlocking Chinese supply is the only fast remedy to tightness — "killing two birds with one stone." Second row logs the **conflicting Intel-Ohio reporting** (TrendForce: SK hynix eyed as a potential *operating partner*; BusinessKorea: SK Hynix denies a deal), which matches the 07-23 INTC read that the acquisition rumour is dead but collaboration paths are live — recorded as unresolved, and cross-referenced to [INTC.md](INTC.md). Also logged **Nanya's ~US$800bn 2026 memory-market call (≈half of all semiconductor revenue)** as an upper-bound marker on how far the industry is extrapolating this cycle. Confirmed the print date: **Tue 2026-07-28, 8:00 PM ET**.
- **2026-07-24 (/run-inbox — SemiAnalysis Advancing AI 2026 + 1 dedup pass; all additive, nothing superseded).**
  - **NET-NEW — SemiAnalysis "Can AMD break the CUDA Moat? AMD Advancing AI 2026" (2026-07-24).** The genuine route to this page. Added in four places: **(1) Current state** — the **HBM4 pin-speed bake-off** bullet: AMD MI455X **12 stacks / 432GB / 23.3 TB/s at 7.6 Gbps** vs Nvidia Rubin and Google at **8 stacks / 288GB**, Rubin **22 TB/s at 10.7 Gbps (40% faster)** — a near-tie despite AMD's 50% wider bus, because "Nvidia aggressively raised its targeted HBM4 pin speeds last year, well above the original JEDEC HBM4 specification… precisely driven by the need to make up for the memory bandwidth deficit to AMD MI455X," a change that "was challenging for memory suppliers to keep up with. Memory suppliers had to rework their HBM4 to deliver the spec Nvidia was targeting. This also pushed out upstream output for Rubin but **as of today these issues have been finally resolved**," and which means **Nvidia must use a much higher-quality bin than AMD**. Framed as a **two-tier HBM4 market** (easy 7.6 Gbps AMD bin vs premium 10.7 Gbps Nvidia bin) → the competitive question shifts from wafer capacity toward **yield on the top bin**, widening the quality/price spread between the three suppliers. Juxtaposed (explicitly flagged as a juxtaposition, not a claim) against the page's existing "SK hynix HBM4 ES ~10 Gbps, lower jitter / stronger package execution than Samsung's" datapoint from "Memory Mania" (02-06). **No bin winner asserted — the source names none.** **(2) Debate / thesis** — a **price-up/content-down** bullet: expects "a significant increase in HBM pricing in 2027 as memory suppliers move to match the margins they now earn on commodity DRAM," modelled as strong enough that AMD cannot pass it all through ("at a lower incremental margin than their corporate margins, dragging down gross margins on the MI455X package") — direct external support for management's refusal (roadshow 07-06) to commit to HBM-DDR margin parity — set against the offsetting de-spec: original 2025 MI455X BOM of **138TB DRAM across LPDDR5X + DDR5 MRDIMM**, now with **LPDDR5X entirely removed** (the roadmapped **up-to-1TB per-accelerator direct-attached LPDDR** tier gone on tight memory supply) plus a significant DDR5 RDIMM de-spec: "much of the DRAM price increases are offset by aggressive de-specs." Baseline noted: "HBM, DRAM, and NAND prices alike have increased dramatically since our baseline in mid-2025." **(3) Risks** — two new bullets: **de-spec / content destruction** (bits per accelerator, not price, is the exposed variable; bites hardest on the ~33-50% of the market the LTA book does not cover per Bernstein 07-09) and **HBM4 top-bin qualification** (risk concentrated in premium-bin yield rather than wafer output). **(4)** 1 Intra-quarter Full-log row (07-24, produto/mixed), an augmented **HBM demand** Signal cell (now **✓ confirms demand / ⚠ new binning nuance**), a synthesis addendum, and a Sources entry.
  - **DEDUP — SemiAnalysis "Meta's Infrastructure Team Needs A Culture Reset" (2026-07-21).** Already cited by [themes/hbm-memory.md](themes/hbm-memory.md) but **absent from this page**, so the quantified HBM-content cut was net-new here: Meta's custom MI450/MI455 **halves the compute silicon (8→4 dies), cuts HBM from 12 stacks to 6 per package, and downgrades the HBM4 from 12-Hi to 8-Hi**, on a program SemiAnalysis says "is going to nuke AMD's volume at Meta because TBD will vastly prefer Rubin" — doubly negative (fewer stacks on a program that may shrink). Added as 1 Intra-quarter Full-log row (07-21, demanda/bear), folded into the new de-spec Risks bullet, and a Sources entry.
